# Governing Body Authority & Responsibilities

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the authority, composition, functions, and oversight responsibilities of the Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The Governing Body is the ultimate authority accountable for the operation, management, fiscal viability, and regulatory compliance of the home health agency. This policy ensures the agency satisfies the requirements set forth in 42 CFR § 484.105 — Condition of Participation: Organization and Administration of Services, which mandates that a home health agency must have a governing body that assumes full legal authority and responsibility for the agency's overall operation and management.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All members of the Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. (including voting and non-voting members)
The Agency Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Compliance Officer
All senior leadership personnel who report directly to the Governing Body or Administrator
All contracted management entities performing governing body functions on behalf of the agency
This policy does not apply to day-to-day clinical or operational staff except to the extent that Governing Body decisions establish requirements, standards, or directives that govern their work.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a designated Governing Body that holds full legal authority and responsibility for the overall operation, management, and regulatory compliance of the home health agency, as required by 42 CFR § 484.105(a).
4.2 The Governing Body shall be responsible for ensuring that Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. operates in compliance with all applicable federal, state, and local laws, regulations, and licensure requirements at all times.
4.3 The Governing Body shall appoint a qualified Administrator who is authorized to act on behalf of the Governing Body in the day-to-day management of the agency and who meets the qualifications defined in agency policy GV-OG-002 and applicable California state requirements.
4.4 The Governing Body shall ensure the appointment and ongoing oversight of a qualified Clinical Manager (Director of Nursing) responsible for all clinical services, in compliance with 42 CFR § 484.105(c).
4.5 The Governing Body shall approve and oversee the agency's:
Scope of services (GV-OG-003)
Organizational structure and reporting lines (GV-OG-001)
Annual strategic plan and operational goals (GV-OG-004)
Policy framework and all REQUIRED-tier policies (GV-PM-001, GV-PM-002)
QAPI program (QA-PG-001, QA-PG-002)
Corporate compliance program (CO-CP-001)
Annual operating budget (FN-FP-005)
Emergency preparedness plan (OP-FM-005)
4.6 The Governing Body shall meet at a frequency sufficient to fulfill its oversight responsibilities, but not less than quarterly, with meetings documented in formal minutes per policy GV-GB-002.
4.7 The Governing Body shall not delegate its ultimate accountability for regulatory compliance, patient safety, or fiscal integrity. Delegation of specific authority shall comply with policy GV-OG-005.
4.8 All members of the Governing Body shall disclose and manage conflicts of interest in accordance with policy GV-GB-003.
4.9 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid. Superseded versions must not be used for any operational or compliance purpose. Any revision to this policy requires re-acknowledgment by all Governing Body members and senior leadership within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Governing Body | The individual(s), board of directors, trustees, partnership, corporation, or other legally constituted authority that has ultimate responsibility for the management and operation of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., as defined by 42 CFR § 484.2 and § 484.105. |
| Administrator | The individual appointed by the Governing Body who is responsible for managing the agency's day-to-day operations and who meets all qualifications specified in agency policy GV-OG-002 and applicable California state law. |
| Clinical Manager | The registered nurse (or qualified individual per California state law) designated by the Governing Body to oversee clinical services including patient care delivery, clinical staff supervision, and OASIS compliance. Also referred to as Director of Nursing (DON). |
| Fiduciary Duty | The legal obligation of Governing Body members to act in good faith, with due diligence, and in the best interest of the agency and the patients it serves. |
| Quorum | The minimum number of Governing Body members required to be present (physically or via approved teleconference) to conduct official business, as defined in the agency's bylaws or operating agreement. |
| QAPI | Quality Assessment and Performance Improvement — the structured program required by 42 CFR § 484.65 for ongoing quality monitoring and improvement. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Establishment and Composition of the Governing Body

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Agency Owner / Corporate Entity | Formally establish the Governing Body through articles of incorporation, operating agreement, partnership agreement, or equivalent legal instrument for Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The establishing document must identify: (a) the legal form of the Governing Body; (b) the minimum and maximum number of members; (c) the quorum requirement; (d) the terms of appointment or election. | Prior to initial Medicare certification and maintained continuously thereafter. |
| 6.1.2 | Governing Body Chair | Maintain a current roster of all Governing Body members including: full legal name, title/role, date of appointment, term expiration date, voting status, and contact information. | Updated within 7 calendar days of any membership change. |
| 6.1.3 | Governing Body | Ensure composition includes, at minimum, individuals with competency in: (a) healthcare operations or clinical services; (b) financial management; (c) regulatory compliance. If any competency area is not represented by a current member, the Governing Body shall retain qualified advisory counsel within 30 calendar days of identifying the gap. | Ongoing; reviewed annually at the first quarterly meeting of each calendar year. |
| 6.1.4 | Compliance Officer | Verify that no Governing Body member appears on the OIG List of Excluded Individuals/Entities (LEIE) or the System for Award Management (SAM) exclusion database at the time of appointment and monthly thereafter, per policy HR-TA-003. | At appointment and monthly thereafter. |

### 6.2 Core Responsibilities of the Governing Body
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall fulfill the following responsibilities directly and shall not delegate ultimate accountability for any of these functions:
#### 6.2.1 — Legal Authority and Agency Operations

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1.1 | Governing Body | Assume and maintain full legal authority for the overall operation, management, and fiscal viability of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. | Continuous. |
| 6.2.1.2 | Governing Body | Ensure the agency maintains current and valid: (a) California home health license — HCAI License No. 406412878; (b) Medicare certification; (c) Medicaid enrollment (if applicable); (d) accreditation (if applicable) — per policy GV-EA-004. | Continuous; verified at each quarterly meeting. |
| 6.2.1.3 | Governing Body | Review and approve the agency's defined scope of services (policy GV-OG-003) at least annually. Ensure the agency does not provide services beyond those for which it is licensed, staffed, and competent to deliver. | Annually, within 30 calendar days of the start of the fiscal year. |

#### 6.2.2 — Appointment and Oversight of Key Personnel

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.2.1 | Governing Body | Appoint a qualified Administrator and document the appointment in Governing Body minutes. The Administrator must meet all qualifications defined in policy GV-OG-002 and applicable California state law. | Prior to agency operation and within 30 calendar days of any vacancy. |
| 6.2.2.2 | Governing Body | Appoint or confirm the appointment of a qualified Clinical Manager (Director of Nursing) responsible for all clinical services, per 42 CFR § 484.105(c). | Prior to agency operation and within 30 calendar days of any vacancy. |
| 6.2.2.3 | Governing Body | Appoint or confirm the designation of a Compliance Officer with authority and independence to operate the corporate compliance program, per policy CO-CP-002. | Prior to agency operation and within 30 calendar days of any vacancy. |
| 6.2.2.4 | Governing Body | Conduct or commission an annual performance evaluation of the Administrator. Results and any corrective directives shall be documented in executive session minutes. | Annually; completed within 60 calendar days of the end of each fiscal year. |
| 6.2.2.5 | Governing Body | Review and approve the agency's succession plan for the Administrator, Clinical Manager, and Compliance Officer, per policy GV-GB-004. | Annually at the second quarterly meeting; updated within 14 calendar days of any key leadership vacancy. |

#### 6.2.3 — Policy and Compliance Oversight

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.3.1 | Governing Body | Approve all REQUIRED-tier policies prior to implementation and ensure a defined policy review cycle exists per policies GV-PM-001 and GV-PM-002. | Prior to implementation of each REQUIRED policy; review cycle approved annually. |
| 6.2.3.2 | Governing Body | Receive and review a compliance status report from the Compliance Officer at each quarterly meeting. The report must address: (a) active compliance investigations; (b) audit findings; (c) regulatory changes affecting the agency; (d) training completion rates. | Quarterly. |
| 6.2.3.3 | Compliance Officer | Prepare and submit the quarterly compliance report to the Governing Body no fewer than 7 calendar days before each scheduled quarterly meeting. | 7 calendar days before each quarterly meeting. |
| 6.2.3.4 | Governing Body | Review and act upon any compliance deficiency identified as high-risk within 14 calendar days of receiving the compliance report. Action must be documented in meeting minutes or a special resolution. | Within 14 calendar days of report receipt. |

#### 6.2.4 — Quality Assessment and Performance Improvement (QAPI) Oversight

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.4.1 | Governing Body | Approve the agency's QAPI plan, per policy QA-PG-002, and ensure that the plan includes measurable quality indicators, performance improvement projects, and patient safety initiatives. | Annually; approved at the first quarterly meeting of each calendar year. |
| 6.2.4.2 | Clinical Manager / QA Designee | Present a QAPI performance report to the Governing Body at each quarterly meeting including: (a) quality indicator trends; (b) status of active PIPs; (c) adverse event summary; (d) patient satisfaction data; (e) Star Rating / Home Health Compare trends (policy QA-SM-004). | Quarterly. |
| 6.2.4.3 | Governing Body | Review, discuss, and document its response to the QAPI report. If any quality indicator falls below the defined threshold for 2 consecutive reporting periods, the Governing Body shall direct corrective action and assign accountability with a defined resolution deadline. | At each quarterly meeting; corrective action directive within 14 calendar days if thresholds are breached. |

#### 6.2.5 — Financial Oversight

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.5.1 | Governing Body | Review and approve the annual operating budget per policy FN-FP-005. | Annually; approved no later than 30 calendar days before the start of each fiscal year. |
| 6.2.5.2 | Administrator | Present a financial performance report to the Governing Body at each quarterly meeting including: (a) revenue vs. budget variance; (b) accounts receivable aging; (c) claims denial rate and trending; (d) cash flow position. | Quarterly. |
| 6.2.5.3 | Governing Body | Review financial reports and direct corrective action if: (a) actual revenue deviates more than 10% below budget for 2 consecutive months; (b) claims denial rate exceeds 5%; (c) days in accounts receivable exceed 60. Directives must be documented in meeting minutes. | At each quarterly meeting. |

#### 6.2.6 — Emergency Preparedness

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.6.1 | Governing Body | Approve the agency's Emergency Operations and Business Continuity Plan per policy OP-FM-005 and 42 CFR § 484.102. | Annually; approved at the third quarterly meeting. |
| 6.2.6.2 | Administrator | Report the results of the most recent emergency preparedness drill or exercise to the Governing Body, including identified gaps and corrective actions. | At the quarterly meeting following each drill (minimum 2 drills per year). |

### 6.3 Governing Body Meetings

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Governing Body Chair | Schedule and convene regular Governing Body meetings no fewer than 4 times per calendar year (quarterly). The meeting schedule for the upcoming year must be established and distributed to all members by December 15 of the preceding year. | Quarterly; schedule distributed by December 15. |
| 6.3.2 | Governing Body Chair | Convene special meetings when urgent matters arise, including but not limited to: (a) CMS survey findings requiring immediate corrective action; (b) serious adverse events; (c) regulatory enforcement actions; (d) key leadership vacancies. Notice of a special meeting must be provided to all members at least 48 hours in advance unless the matter constitutes an imminent threat to patient safety, in which case notice may be shortened to the minimum practicable. | As needed; notice within 48 hours or shorter for imminent patient safety threats. |
| 6.3.3 | Designated Secretary / Administrator | Prepare and distribute the meeting agenda to all Governing Body members no fewer than 7 calendar days before each scheduled meeting. The agenda must include standing items: (a) approval of prior minutes; (b) Administrator report; (c) compliance report; (d) QAPI report; (e) financial report; (f) old business; (g) new business. | 7 calendar days before each meeting. |
| 6.3.4 | Designated Secretary | Record formal minutes for each meeting per policy GV-GB-002. Minutes must document: (a) date, time, and location; (b) members present and absent; (c) quorum determination; (d) all motions, seconds, and voting outcomes; (e) all directives issued with assigned responsible parties and deadlines; (f) executive session topics (without protected details). | Draft minutes completed within 14 calendar days of the meeting; approved at the next regular meeting. |
| 6.3.5 | Governing Body Chair | Ensure a quorum is present before conducting any official business. If quorum is not achieved, the meeting shall be rescheduled within 14 calendar days. | At the start of each meeting. |

### 6.4 Conflict of Interest Management

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | All Governing Body Members | Complete and submit the Conflict of Interest Disclosure Form (Appendix B) at the time of appointment, annually thereafter, and within 7 calendar days of any change in circumstances that could create a new conflict, per policy GV-GB-003. | At appointment; annually; within 7 days of change. |
| 6.4.2 | Compliance Officer | Review all submitted conflict of interest disclosures within 14 calendar days of receipt and present a summary to the Governing Body with recommendations for management or recusal. | Within 14 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.4.3 | Governing Body | Act on conflict of interest recommendations. Any member with a disclosed conflict shall recuse from discussion and voting on the affected matter. Recusals must be documented in meeting minutes. | At the meeting where the affected matter is addressed. |

### 6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Governing Body fails to meet quarterly | Administrator notifies all members and the Compliance Officer in writing. | Administrator schedules a make-up meeting. If Governing Body does not convene within 30 calendar days, the Compliance Officer documents the deficiency and initiates corrective action per QA-AE-003. | Make-up meeting within 30 calendar days of the missed quarter. |
| Quorum not achieved for 2 consecutive scheduled meetings | Governing Body Chair escalates to the full membership in writing. | Chair initiates membership recruitment or replacement per the agency's bylaws. Issue must be resolved before the next scheduled meeting. | Within 30 calendar days. |
| Key leadership vacancy (Administrator, Clinical Manager, Compliance Officer) exceeds 30 days unfilled | Governing Body Chair | Governing Body must appoint an interim designee within 14 calendar days of vacancy and document the appointment. Permanent appointment must occur within 90 calendar days. | Interim: 14 days. Permanent: 90 days. |
| CMS survey results in Condition-level deficiency | Administrator convenes a special Governing Body meeting. | Governing Body directs development of a Plan of Correction within CMS-required timeframes (typically 10 calendar days). Governing Body receives weekly status updates until resolution is confirmed. | Special meeting within 48 hours of receipt of findings; Plan of Correction per CMS deadline. |
| Compliance Officer reports fraud, waste, or abuse concern to Governing Body | Governing Body Chair | Governing Body directs investigation per CO-CP-007 and ensures non-retaliation per CO-CP-005. Governing Body receives investigation status updates at each meeting until resolution. | Investigation initiated within 7 calendar days; updates at each meeting. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Governing Body is legally established and documented. | Review of establishing documents (articles, bylaws, operating agreement). | Current, complete, and on file at all times. |
| Governing Body meets at least quarterly. | Review of meeting minutes with dates and attendance. | 4 or more meetings per calendar year with quorum present at each. |
| Key personnel (Administrator, Clinical Manager, Compliance Officer) are appointed and documented. | Review of Governing Body minutes; personnel files; appointment letters. | Current appointments documented; no vacancy exceeds 30 days without interim designee. |
| QAPI plan is reviewed and approved annually. | Review of Governing Body minutes for documented approval. | Annual approval documented at first quarterly meeting. |
| Compliance reports are presented quarterly. | Review of Governing Body agendas and minutes; compliance report files. | Reports submitted 7 days before each meeting; discussion documented in minutes. |
| Conflict of Interest disclosures are current. | Review of Appendix B forms for each member; annual renewal dates. | 100% completion rate; no lapsed disclosures. |
| Budget is approved annually. | Review of Governing Body minutes; budget document. | Approved no later than 30 days before fiscal year start. |
| Governing Body members are screened for exclusion. | Review of OIG/SAM screening logs. | Initial screening at appointment; monthly screening documented. |
| Policy acknowledgments are current. | Review of signed Appendix C forms. | 100% acknowledgment within 14 calendar days of effective date or new appointment. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under the State Operations Manual (SOM) Appendix B will specifically verify:
Evidence that a Governing Body exists and is functioning. Surveyors will request establishing documents and a current membership roster. A sole proprietor must demonstrate individual acceptance of governing body responsibilities.
Evidence that the Governing Body has appointed a qualified Administrator. Surveyors will review Governing Body minutes for appointment documentation and verify qualifications per California state requirements.
Evidence of Clinical Manager oversight. Surveyors will look for Governing Body minutes documenting appointment, reporting, and oversight of clinical services leadership.
Evidence that the Governing Body oversees QAPI. Surveyors will examine whether the Governing Body has reviewed, approved, and acted upon quality data. Passive receipt of reports without documented action is a common deficiency.
Evidence of policy oversight. Surveyors will verify that the Governing Body has approved the agency's policies and that a review cycle exists.
Evidence of fiscal oversight. Surveyors will review whether the Governing Body monitors the agency's financial viability and acts on adverse trends.
Meeting frequency and documentation quality. Surveyors will request all meeting minutes for the survey look-back period and assess completeness, including attendance, quorum, and documented decisions.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No documented evidence that a Governing Body exists or functions. | Condition-level deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.105. Potential termination of Medicare certification. | Maintain establishing documents, current roster, and quarterly minutes on file and readily accessible. |
| Governing Body meetings are held but not documented. | Surveyor will treat undocumented meetings as not having occurred. | Use Appendix D template; complete draft minutes within 14 calendar days. |
| Governing Body "rubber stamps" reports without documented discussion or action. | Surveyors will cite passive governance as failure to exercise oversight. | Minutes must document specific discussion points, questions, directives, and assigned follow-up. |
| Key leadership vacancies are unfilled for extended periods. | Surveyor will cite failure to ensure adequate management. | Fill or designate interim within 14 calendar days per Section 6.5. |
| No documented conflict of interest disclosures. | OIG compliance program requirement; potential survey finding. | Enforce annual disclosure per Appendix B; Compliance Officer tracks compliance. |
| QAPI plan approved but no evidence of Governing Body review of quality data. | Surveyor will cite failure of governing body oversight of QAPI (42 CFR § 484.65 cross-reference). | Require quarterly QAPI report presentation and document Governing Body response. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All Governing Body members of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall receive orientation to this policy within 14 calendar days of appointment. Orientation shall be conducted by the Administrator or Compliance Officer and must cover: (a) the legal authority and responsibilities of the Governing Body; (b) meeting and quorum requirements; (c) conflict of interest obligations; (d) QAPI, compliance, and financial oversight expectations; (e) CMS survey process and surveyor expectations for governance documentation.
10.2 All Governing Body members and senior leadership personnel within scope of this policy (Section 3) shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.3 The Administrator shall maintain a tracking log of all policy acknowledgments and report any non-compliance to the Governing Body Chair within 7 calendar days of the acknowledgment deadline. Failure to acknowledge within the required timeframe shall result in written notification from the Governing Body Chair with a mandatory completion deadline of 7 additional calendar days.
10.4 Annual refresher training on governing body responsibilities shall be conducted at the first quarterly meeting of each calendar year. Attendance shall be documented in meeting minutes.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment.

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the requirements for scheduling, conducting, documenting, and retaining records of all Governing Body meetings of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., including regular quarterly meetings, special meetings, and executive sessions. Formal, complete, and audit-defensible meeting minutes are the primary documentary evidence that the Governing Body is actively exercising its oversight responsibilities as required by 42 CFR § 484.105. This policy ensures that meeting records satisfy CMS surveyor expectations under the State Operations Manual (SOM) Appendix B, support the agency's continuous survey readiness posture, and provide a defensible record of all Governing Body decisions, directives, and actions.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body Chair of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
All voting and non-voting members of the Governing Body
The Designated Secretary responsible for recording meeting minutes
The Agency Administrator
The Compliance Officer
The Clinical Manager / Director of Nursing, when presenting reports to the Governing Body
Any guest, advisor, or subject matter expert invited to attend a Governing Body meeting in any capacity
This policy does not apply to departmental meetings, staff meetings, or committee meetings held below the Governing Body level, unless those meetings are convened as a formal subcommittee of the Governing Body and require Governing Body-level documentation.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall convene formal Governing Body meetings no fewer than four (4) times per calendar year on a quarterly basis, as required by policy GV-GB-001 and consistent with the oversight obligations established under 42 CFR § 484.105.
4.2 Every Governing Body meeting — whether regular, special, or executive session — shall be documented in formal written minutes that capture all required elements defined in Section 6.4 of this policy. The absence of written minutes for any meeting shall be treated as equivalent to the meeting not having occurred for purposes of CMS survey review.
4.3 Meeting minutes shall be drafted within fourteen (14) calendar days of each meeting, reviewed by the Governing Body Chair, and approved by the full Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Approved minutes shall constitute the official record of the meeting.
4.4 All Governing Body meetings shall require a quorum of voting members to be present — physically or via approved teleconference or video conference — before any official business may be conducted. No motion, vote, directive, or formal decision shall be recorded as valid if made without quorum.
4.5 Meeting agendas shall be prepared and distributed to all Governing Body members no fewer than seven (7) calendar days before each scheduled meeting. Standing agenda items as defined in Section 6.3 of this policy shall appear on every regular quarterly meeting agenda.
4.6 Special meetings may be convened between regular quarterly meetings when urgent matters require Governing Body action. Special meetings require a minimum of forty-eight (48) hours advance notice to all members, except in cases of imminent patient safety threat, in which case notice shall be provided at the earliest practicable time.
4.7 Executive sessions may be convened at any regular or special meeting to address legally sensitive, personnel, or confidential matters. Executive session topics shall be noted in the regular meeting minutes; the substantive content of executive session discussions shall be documented in a separate, restricted executive session log maintained by the Compliance Officer.
4.8 All Governing Body meeting records — including agendas, minutes, attendance logs, and executive session logs — shall be retained for a minimum of seven (7) years from the date of the meeting, in accordance with policy CO-HP-007, and shall be readily accessible for CMS survey review upon request.
4.9 Minutes shall reflect substantive discussion, questions raised, responses provided, directives issued, responsible parties assigned, and deadlines established. Minutes that reflect only the receipt of reports without documented discussion or action shall be considered deficient and shall be returned to the Designated Secretary for revision before approval.
4.10 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid. Any revision to this policy requires re-acknowledgment by all Governing Body members and senior leadership within fourteen (14) calendar days of the revised effective date.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Regular Quarterly Meeting | A formally scheduled Governing Body meeting convened at least once per calendar quarter to fulfill standing oversight obligations including review of compliance, QAPI, financial, and administrative reports. |
| Special Meeting | A Governing Body meeting convened outside the regular quarterly schedule to address urgent or time-sensitive matters that cannot wait until the next regular meeting. |
| Executive Session | A closed portion of a regular or special meeting, restricted to Governing Body members and invited individuals, convened to discuss legally sensitive, personnel, litigation-related, or confidential matters. |
| Quorum | The minimum number of voting Governing Body members required to be present — in person or via approved remote attendance — to conduct official business and record valid votes. Quorum is defined in the agency's bylaws or operating agreement and must be documented at the start of each meeting. |
| Meeting Minutes | The official written record of a Governing Body meeting documenting attendance, quorum status, all agenda items discussed, reports received, motions made, votes taken, directives issued, responsible parties assigned, and deadlines established. |
| Designated Secretary | The individual assigned responsibility for preparing the meeting agenda, recording minutes during the meeting, producing the draft minutes within the required timeframe, and maintaining the governance file. This role may be held by the Administrator or a qualified designee. |
| Directive | A formal instruction issued by the Governing Body during a meeting requiring a specific action, assigned to a named responsible party, with a defined completion deadline. Directives must be tracked and reported on at the subsequent meeting. |
| Executive Session Log | A separate, restricted record maintained by the Compliance Officer documenting the subject matter, participants, and any decisions made during executive sessions, without disclosing legally protected content. |
| Action Item Tracker | A running log maintained by the Designated Secretary and Administrator documenting all directives issued at each meeting, including status updates and resolution confirmations. |
| Governance File | The secure physical or electronic repository maintained by the Administrator containing all Governing Body meeting records, including agendas, minutes, attendance records, executive session logs, and supporting reports. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Annual Meeting Schedule

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Governing Body Chair | Establish the regular quarterly meeting schedule for the upcoming calendar year. The schedule must designate a specific date, time, and location (or remote platform) for each of the four quarterly meetings. | Completed and distributed to all Governing Body members by December 15 of the preceding year. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator | Distribute the approved annual meeting schedule to all Governing Body members, the Clinical Manager, the Compliance Officer, and any other standing presenters. | Within 3 calendar days of the Governing Body Chair's approval of the schedule. |
| 6.1.3 | Governing Body Chair | Reserve any required physical meeting space or establish remote attendance credentials (video conference link, dial-in number) for each scheduled meeting. | At least 30 calendar days before each scheduled meeting. |
| 6.1.4 | Governing Body Chair | If any scheduled meeting must be rescheduled, notify all members in writing with at least 14 calendar days advance notice and establish the rescheduled date within the same calendar quarter. | Notice within 14 calendar days of rescheduling; rescheduled meeting must occur within the same quarter. |
| 6.1.5 | Administrator | Maintain the annual meeting schedule in the governance file and update it upon any approved changes. | Ongoing; updated within 3 calendar days of any schedule change. |

### 6.2 Special Meeting Convening

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Governing Body Chair or Administrator | Identify the need for a special meeting. Circumstances warranting a special meeting include but are not limited to: (a) CMS survey findings requiring immediate Governing Body action; (b) a serious adverse patient event requiring Governing Body-level response; (c) a regulatory enforcement action or subpoena; (d) a key leadership vacancy requiring emergency appointment; (e) a material compliance violation. | Upon identification of qualifying circumstances. |
| 6.2.2 | Governing Body Chair | Issue written notice of the special meeting to all Governing Body members, specifying: (a) the date, time, and location or remote access method; (b) the specific purpose and agenda for the meeting. | At least 48 hours before the meeting. If the matter constitutes an imminent patient safety threat, notice shall be issued at the earliest practicable time with no minimum advance notice required. |
| 6.2.3 | Designated Secretary | Prepare a focused agenda for the special meeting limited to the specific matters requiring Governing Body action. | Distributed to all members with the meeting notice or as soon thereafter as practicable. |
| 6.2.4 | Designated Secretary | Record and produce formal minutes for the special meeting following the same standards as regular quarterly meeting minutes per Section 6.4 of this policy. | Draft minutes completed within 14 calendar days of the special meeting; approved at the next regular quarterly meeting. |

### 6.3 Agenda Preparation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator / Designated Secretary | Prepare the meeting agenda for each regular quarterly meeting. The agenda shall include the following standing items in the order listed: (a) Call to Order; (b) Confirmation of Quorum; (c) Approval of Prior Meeting Minutes; (d) Administrator Report; (e) Compliance Officer Report; (f) QAPI Report; (g) Financial Report; (h) Old Business — Status of Prior Meeting Directives; (i) New Business; (j) Executive Session (if applicable); (k) Adjournment and Next Meeting Date. | Draft prepared no later than 10 calendar days before the meeting. |
| 6.3.2 | Governing Body Chair | Review and approve the draft agenda, adding any items requiring Governing Body action that are not captured in standing items. | No later than 8 calendar days before the meeting. |
| 6.3.3 | Administrator / Designated Secretary | Distribute the approved agenda to all Governing Body members, along with all supporting materials (compliance report, QAPI report, financial report, action item tracker update, and any new business documents requiring advance review). | No fewer than 7 calendar days before the meeting. |
| 6.3.4 | Administrator | Confirm receipt of the agenda by all Governing Body members. If any member has not confirmed receipt by 3 calendar days before the meeting, the Administrator shall follow up by phone or email and document the follow-up attempt. | Confirmation confirmed by 4 calendar days before the meeting; follow-up by 3 calendar days before. |
| 6.3.5 | Governing Body Chair | Accept requests from members to add items to the agenda up to 5 calendar days before the meeting. Items requested after that point shall be deferred to New Business at the discretion of the Chair, unless the item constitutes an urgent matter. | Requests accepted through 5 calendar days before the meeting. |

### 6.4 Conducting the Meeting and Recording Minutes
#### 6.4.1 — Quorum Verification

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1.1 | Governing Body Chair | Call the meeting to order and direct the Designated Secretary to record attendance. Quorum must be confirmed before any official business is conducted. | At the start of each meeting. |
| 6.4.1.2 | Designated Secretary | Record the full name, role, and attendance method (in-person or remote) of each member present and each member absent. | At the start of each meeting. |
| 6.4.1.3 | Governing Body Chair | Declare whether quorum has been achieved based on the number of voting members present relative to the quorum requirement defined in the agency's bylaws or operating agreement. | Immediately after attendance is recorded. |
| 6.4.1.4 | Governing Body Chair | If quorum is not achieved, adjourn the meeting without conducting official business and reschedule within 14 calendar days. Document the failed quorum attempt in the governance file. | Within 14 calendar days of the failed meeting. |

#### 6.4.2 — Required Elements of Meeting Minutes

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.2.1 | Designated Secretary | Record all required elements in the meeting minutes. Minutes must include at minimum: (a) meeting type (regular quarterly, special, or combined); (b) date, start time, and end time; (c) meeting location or remote platform used; (d) names and roles of all members present and absent; (e) attendance method for each member (in-person or remote); (f) names and roles of any guests or non-members present, including their purpose of attendance; (g) quorum confirmation — number required and number present; (h) a statement confirming quorum was achieved (or a statement that official business was not conducted due to lack of quorum). | During and immediately following each meeting. |
| 6.4.2.2 | Designated Secretary | For each agenda item, record: (a) the name and role of the presenter; (b) a substantive summary of the report or information presented — not merely "report presented" or "report received"; (c) specific questions raised by Governing Body members; (d) responses provided; (e) any concerns, findings, or trends identified; (f) the Governing Body's documented response or position; (g) any directive issued, including the responsible party and the completion deadline. | During and immediately following each meeting. |
| 6.4.2.3 | Designated Secretary | For each motion made, record: (a) the exact language of the motion; (b) the name of the member who made the motion; (c) the name of the member who seconded the motion; (d) the vote outcome — number in favor, number opposed, number abstaining; (e) the names of any members who recused themselves and the stated reason for recusal; (f) whether the motion passed or failed. | During the meeting, for each motion. |
| 6.4.2.4 | Designated Secretary | Record all directives issued during the meeting in a dedicated "Directives Issued" section at the end of the minutes or within the relevant agenda item section, including: (a) a clear description of the required action; (b) the name and title of the responsible party; (c) the completion deadline; (d) the follow-up reporting mechanism (e.g., report at next quarterly meeting, written confirmation to Chair). | During the meeting. |
| 6.4.2.5 | Designated Secretary | For executive sessions, record in the regular minutes: (a) the time the executive session was convened; (b) the general subject matter category (e.g., personnel matter, litigation, compliance investigation) without disclosing protected content; (c) the names of individuals present during the executive session; (d) the time the executive session concluded; (e) any formal action taken as a result of the executive session (motion, vote, directive). | During the meeting. |

#### 6.4.3 — Report Presentation Standards

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.3.1 | Administrator | Present the Administrator Report at each regular quarterly meeting. The report must address at minimum: (a) agency operational status; (b) staffing levels and any critical vacancies; (c) regulatory activity (surveys, audits, correspondence from CMS or HCAI); (d) status of any active corrective action plans; (e) significant operational developments since the prior meeting. The written report must be submitted to the Governing Body with the agenda package 7 days before the meeting. | Written report submitted 7 days before the meeting; oral presentation at the meeting. |
| 6.4.3.2 | Compliance Officer | Present the Compliance Report at each regular quarterly meeting. The report must address at minimum: (a) status of all active compliance investigations; (b) audit findings and their resolution status; (c) regulatory changes affecting the agency in the past quarter; (d) training completion rates by department; (e) OIG/SAM screening status for all Governing Body members and applicable staff. The written report must be submitted to the Governing Body 7 days before the meeting per policy GV-GB-001 §6.2.3.3. | Written report submitted 7 days before the meeting; oral presentation at the meeting. |
| 6.4.3.3 | Clinical Manager / QA Designee | Present the QAPI Report at each regular quarterly meeting. The report must address at minimum: (a) quality indicator trends compared to prior quarter and national benchmarks; (b) status of all active Performance Improvement Projects (PIPs); (c) adverse event summary including near-misses; (d) patient satisfaction data and trending; (e) Home Health Compare and Star Rating trends per policy QA-SM-004. The written report must be submitted to the Governing Body with the agenda package 7 days before the meeting. | Written report submitted 7 days before the meeting; oral presentation at the meeting. |
| 6.4.3.4 | Administrator | Present the Financial Report at each regular quarterly meeting. The report must address at minimum: (a) revenue versus budget variance; (b) accounts receivable aging; (c) claims denial rate and trending; (d) cash flow position; (e) any financial corrective actions in progress. The written report must be submitted to the Governing Body with the agenda package 7 days before the meeting. | Written report submitted 7 days before the meeting; oral presentation at the meeting. |

### 6.5 Draft, Review, and Approval of Minutes

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Designated Secretary | Produce the draft minutes from notes, recordings (if used), and supporting materials. The draft must include all elements required by Section 6.4.2. | Draft completed within 14 calendar days of the meeting. |
| 6.5.2 | Designated Secretary | Submit the draft minutes to the Governing Body Chair for preliminary review. Include a cover note identifying any items requiring Chair clarification or correction. | Submitted to Chair within 14 calendar days of the meeting. |
| 6.5.3 | Governing Body Chair | Review the draft minutes for accuracy, completeness, and compliance with Section 6.4.2 requirements. The Chair shall not alter the substantive content of any recorded motion, vote, or directive. Factual corrections and formatting adjustments are permissible. | Chair review completed within 7 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.5.4 | Governing Body Chair | Return the reviewed draft to the Designated Secretary with any corrections noted. If the draft is materially deficient (missing required elements, failing to document substantive discussion), the Chair shall return it with written instructions for revision. | Within 7 calendar days of receipt of the draft. |
| 6.5.5 | Designated Secretary | Incorporate Chair corrections and distribute the revised draft to all Governing Body members for advance review before the next regular meeting, as part of the next meeting's agenda package. | Distributed with the next meeting's agenda package, at least 7 calendar days before that meeting. |
| 6.5.6 | Governing Body | At the next regular meeting, formally vote to approve the prior meeting's minutes as written or with stated amendments. The vote and any amendments shall be recorded in the current meeting's minutes. | At each regular quarterly meeting as the first substantive agenda item after quorum confirmation. |
| 6.5.7 | Designated Secretary | File the approved minutes in the governance file and mark them as "APPROVED — [Date of Approval]." Superseded draft versions shall be retained in the file marked "DRAFT — SUPERSEDED" and not destroyed. | Within 3 calendar days of approval. |

### 6.6 Executive Session Procedures

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | Governing Body Chair | Announce the convening of an executive session, state the general subject matter category, and identify the individuals permitted to remain present. All guests and non-members not specifically authorized shall exit the meeting room or remote session. | At the time executive session is convened. |
| 6.6.2 | Designated Secretary | Record in the regular meeting minutes: the time executive session commenced, the general subject matter category, and the names of individuals present. | During the meeting. |
| 6.6.3 | Compliance Officer | Maintain a separate, restricted Executive Session Log (Appendix D) documenting: (a) date and meeting to which the session pertained; (b) subject matter with sufficient specificity to allow future identification; (c) names of all individuals present; (d) any decisions made, motions passed, or directives issued during the executive session; (e) any legal counsel present and the nature of legal advice received (noting attorney-client privilege where applicable). | Executive Session Log entry completed within 7 calendar days of the meeting. |
| 6.6.4 | Governing Body Chair | Announce the conclusion of the executive session, note the time in the regular meeting, and reconvene the open session. Any formal actions taken during the executive session (motions, votes, directives) that are not legally protected shall be recorded in the regular meeting minutes. | At the conclusion of executive session. |
| 6.6.5 | Compliance Officer | Maintain the Executive Session Log in a restricted access file separate from the general governance file. Access shall be limited to Governing Body members, the Compliance Officer, and legal counsel. | Ongoing; restricted access enforced continuously. |

### 6.7 Action Item Tracking

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.7.1 | Designated Secretary | Maintain a running Action Item Tracker (Appendix E) documenting every directive issued at each meeting, including: (a) directive description; (b) meeting date issued; (c) responsible party; (d) completion deadline; (e) current status (open, in progress, completed, overdue); (f) date completed or escalated. | Updated within 3 calendar days of each meeting. |
| 6.7.2 | Administrator | Review the Action Item Tracker before each quarterly meeting and prepare a status update for all open and recently completed items. Overdue items must be flagged and an explanation provided. | Status update prepared and distributed with the agenda package 7 calendar days before each meeting. |
| 6.7.3 | Governing Body | Review the Action Item Tracker status update at the Old Business section of each regular meeting. The Governing Body shall formally acknowledge completion of resolved items and issue revised directives or escalation instructions for overdue items. | At each regular quarterly meeting. |
| 6.7.4 | Administrator | If any directive remains open and overdue beyond two consecutive quarterly meetings without resolution, escalate to the Governing Body Chair in writing and initiate a corrective action plan per policy QA-AE-003. | Within 7 calendar days of the second consecutive quarterly meeting at which the item remains unresolved. |

### 6.8 Record Retention and Governance File Maintenance

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.8.1 | Administrator | Maintain the governance file in a secure physical or electronic location accessible to the Governing Body Chair, Governing Body members, Compliance Officer, and authorized agency staff. The governance file shall contain: (a) the annual meeting schedule; (b) all meeting agendas; (c) all approved meeting minutes and superseded drafts; (d) all supporting reports presented at each meeting (compliance, QAPI, financial, administrator); (e) attendance records; (f) the Action Item Tracker; (g) Governing Body appointment documentation per GV-GB-001. | Maintained continuously; reviewed for completeness quarterly before each meeting. |
| 6.8.2 | Compliance Officer | Maintain the Executive Session Log in a separately secured, restricted access file as described in Section 6.6.5. | Maintained continuously. |
| 6.8.3 | Administrator | Retain all governance records — including agendas, minutes (approved and draft), attendance records, supporting reports, and executive session logs — for a minimum of 7 years from the date of the meeting. | Retention policy enforced continuously per CO-HP-007. |
| 6.8.4 | Administrator | Ensure all governance records are organized in a manner that permits rapid retrieval during a CMS survey or audit. Records shall be indexed by meeting date, meeting type, and document type. | Governance file index reviewed and updated quarterly before each meeting. |
| 6.8.5 | Administrator | When transitioning to a new electronic records system or document management platform, ensure all historical governance records are migrated completely and the 7-year retention clock is preserved based on the original meeting date, not the migration date. | Completed before any legacy record system is decommissioned. |

### 6.9 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Quorum not achieved at a scheduled regular quarterly meeting | Governing Body Chair notifies all members in writing. | Chair reschedules the meeting within the same calendar quarter. If quorum is not achieved for 2 consecutive scheduled meetings, Chair initiates membership recruitment or replacement per the agency's bylaws and notifies the Compliance Officer to document the deficiency. | Rescheduled within 14 calendar days; deficiency documented within 7 calendar days of second consecutive failure. |
| Draft minutes not completed within 14 calendar days of the meeting | Governing Body Chair | Chair issues a written notice to the Designated Secretary requiring immediate completion. If draft is not produced within 7 additional calendar days, Administrator assumes responsibility for producing the draft. | Written notice within 15 calendar days of the meeting; administrator assumes drafting within 22 calendar days of the meeting. |
| Governing Body approves minutes that are later found to contain a material error | Governing Body Chair | Chair convenes a correction process at the next regular meeting. An errata notation is added to the governance file referencing the corrected element. The original approved minutes are never altered; the correction is appended as a formal amendment. | Correction presented at the next regular quarterly meeting. |
| Required supporting reports (compliance, QAPI, financial) not submitted 7 days before the meeting | Administrator | Administrator notifies the responsible presenter in writing requiring immediate submission. If the report is not received by 3 days before the meeting, the Administrator notifies the Governing Body Chair. The Governing Body may elect to proceed with the meeting and receive the report orally, or defer the agenda item to the next meeting. | Written notice immediately upon identification of non-compliance; Chair notified by 3 days before the meeting. |
| Governance file found incomplete during pre-survey review | Administrator | Administrator conducts an immediate audit of all missing records and reconstructs any retrievable documents. Compliance Officer is notified. A written summary of any unrecoverable records is prepared for the Governing Body. | Audit completed within 7 calendar days of identification; Compliance Officer notified immediately. |
| A special meeting is needed and the Governing Body Chair is unavailable | Vice Chair or most senior voting member | The available senior voting member may convene the special meeting, document the Chair's absence, and ensure full minutes are recorded. The Governing Body Chair must ratify the actions taken at the special meeting at the next regular meeting. | Ratification documented at the next regular quarterly meeting. |

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Annual meeting schedule | Written schedule identifying date, time, and location of each quarterly meeting for the calendar year (Appendix A). | Governing Body Chair (approval); Administrator (maintenance) | Governance file; distributed to all members. | Established and distributed by December 15 of the preceding year; updated within 3 days of any change. |
| Meeting agendas | Approved agenda for each regular quarterly and special meeting, including all supporting materials distributed to members (Appendix B template). | Designated Secretary / Administrator | Governance file. | Distributed 7 calendar days before each meeting; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Meeting attendance records | Attendance log for each meeting documenting member names, roles, presence/absence, and attendance method. | Designated Secretary | Incorporated into meeting minutes; governance file. | Recorded at each meeting; retained for minimum 7 years as part of minutes. |
| Draft meeting minutes | Unsupported draft of meeting minutes produced within 14 calendar days of each meeting and marked "DRAFT." | Designated Secretary | Governance file (retained even after approval). | Completed within 14 calendar days of the meeting. |
| Approved meeting minutes | Final minutes approved by the Governing Body at the subsequent regular meeting, marked "APPROVED — [date]." | Designated Secretary (preparation); Governing Body (approval) | Governance file; copy distributed to all members. | Approved at the next regular meeting; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Action Item Tracker | Running log of all directives issued at each meeting, including status updates (Appendix E). | Designated Secretary / Administrator | Governance file; distributed with each agenda package. | Updated within 3 calendar days of each meeting; reviewed at every regular meeting. |
| Executive Session Log | Restricted log of all executive session topics, participants, decisions, and directives per Section 6.6 (Appendix D). | Compliance Officer | Restricted executive session file (separate from governance file). | Completed within 7 calendar days of each meeting where executive session is held; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Supporting reports | Compliance, QAPI, financial, and administrator reports submitted for each quarterly meeting. | Each respective presenter | Governance file (filed by meeting date). | Submitted 7 calendar days before each meeting; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Quorum documentation | Documented confirmation of quorum at the start of each meeting within the meeting minutes. | Designated Secretary | Meeting minutes; governance file. | Recorded at each meeting. |
| Minutes correction / errata | Written amendment or errata notation appended to approved minutes if a material error is later identified. | Designated Secretary (preparation); Governing Body (approval) | Governance file, attached to the original approved minutes. | Presented and approved at the next regular meeting following identification of the error. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy by all Governing Body members and senior leadership within scope (Appendix C). | Each member / leader (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of policy effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Governing Body meets at least quarterly. | Review of meeting minutes confirming date, attendance, and quorum for each quarter. | Four or more meetings per calendar year; quorum confirmed at each. |
| Meeting agendas are distributed at least 7 days before each meeting. | Review of agenda distribution records and email timestamps. | 100% of agendas distributed within required timeframe; no exceptions without documented cause. |
| Draft minutes are completed within 14 calendar days of each meeting. | Review of draft completion dates on file. | 100% of drafts completed on time; no draft outstanding beyond 14 calendar days without a documented extension approved by the Chair. |
| Minutes capture substantive discussion, not just receipt of reports. | Content review of approved minutes for specificity of discussion, questions, responses, and directives. | All minutes include specific discussion summaries, questions raised, Governing Body responses, and directives with named responsible parties and deadlines. |
| All motions, seconds, and votes are recorded. | Review of motion log within approved minutes. | 100% of formal actions recorded with mover, seconder, vote count, and outcome. |
| Action items are tracked and reported at each subsequent meeting. | Review of Action Item Tracker and Old Business section of subsequent minutes. | All open directives tracked; status reported at each regular meeting; no item overdue beyond two consecutive quarters without documented escalation. |
| Supporting reports submitted 7 days before each meeting. | Review of submission dates for compliance, QAPI, financial, and administrator reports. | 100% of reports submitted within required timeframe for at least 90% of meetings in any rolling 12-month period. |
| Governance file is complete and organized for rapid retrieval. | Physical or electronic file audit conducted quarterly by Administrator. | All required records present, indexed, and retrievable within 1 business day. |
| Executive Session Logs are maintained for all executive sessions. | Review of restricted executive session file by Compliance Officer. | Log entry completed for 100% of executive sessions within 7 calendar days of each meeting. |
| Meeting records retained for minimum 7 years. | Review of records retention schedule; audit of governance file index. | All records with a date within the past 7 years present and intact. No premature destruction documented. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under the State Operations Manual (SOM) Appendix B will specifically verify:
Evidence that Governing Body meetings are occurring at least quarterly. Surveyors will request all meeting minutes from the survey look-back period (typically 12–24 months) and verify that four or more meetings occurred in each calendar year with documented quorum.
Quality of meeting minutes as evidence of active governance. Surveyors distinguish between minutes that document passive receipt of reports and minutes that demonstrate substantive engagement. Entries such as "QAPI report received — no action" are a common deficiency. Surveyors expect to see specific data points referenced, questions asked, and directives issued.
Evidence that all required reports were presented. Surveyors will cross-reference the agenda and minutes to verify that compliance, QAPI, financial, and administrator reports were presented at each meeting. Missing reports for any quarter are a cited deficiency.
Evidence of follow-through on directives. Surveyors will trace directives issued in one quarter's minutes to the subsequent quarter's minutes to confirm reported status. Directives that appear in minutes but are never revisited suggest a non-functional governance structure.
Agenda distribution and meeting notice compliance. Surveyors may request proof that agendas were distributed at least 7 days in advance. Email distribution records and governance file timestamps are the primary evidence.
Completeness of quorum documentation. Every set of minutes must identify the quorum requirement, the number of members present, and a declaration that quorum was achieved. Any meeting that lacks quorum documentation is at risk of being treated as non-compliant.
Executive session documentation. Surveyors will verify that executive sessions are noted in the regular minutes. They will not review the content of a properly designated executive session log; however, they will cite the absence of any notation as a documentation deficiency.
Record retention. Surveyors will request records going back to the beginning of the look-back period. Missing records from any portion of that period — whether due to poor maintenance, system migration, or inadvertent destruction — will result in a citation.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Minutes reflect only "report received" without discussion or directives. | Surveyors cite as passive governance; failure to exercise oversight under 42 CFR § 484.105. | Train Designated Secretary to record specific questions, data points referenced, and Governing Body responses. Chair reviews drafts for substance before distribution. |
| Draft minutes not completed timely; approved months after the meeting. | Creates audit gap; surveyor may question whether the meeting occurred as recorded. | Enforce 14-calendar-day draft deadline; Chair escalates to Administrator if deadline is missed. |
| Quorum not documented in minutes. | Surveyor treats meeting as non-compliant even if quorum was in fact present. | Designated Secretary uses Appendix B template which includes quorum as a required field before any agenda item is recorded. |
| Agendas not distributed 7 days in advance. | Survey finding; evidence of inadequate governance administration. | Administrator sets calendar reminders 10 days before each meeting; tracks distribution dates in governance file. |
| Action items issued at one meeting never appear in subsequent minutes. | Surveyor cites as evidence of non-functional governance; directives without follow-through. | Maintain Action Item Tracker (Appendix E); include as mandatory Old Business agenda item at every meeting. |
| No minutes produced for a special meeting. | Surveyor treats the special meeting as not having occurred; any decisions made at the special meeting have no documented basis. | Policy requires minutes for every meeting type; Designated Secretary is responsible for all meeting documentation. |
| Executive session held with no notation in regular minutes. | Survey finding; creates appearance that Governing Body is conducting undocumented business. | Regular minutes template (Appendix B) includes an executive session field; Chair confirms notation before minutes are finalized. |
| Governance file incomplete or inaccessible during survey. | Surveyors cannot verify compliance; creates presumption of non-compliance for unverified periods. | Quarterly governance file audit by Administrator; records indexed for same-day retrieval. |
| Minutes not retained for 7 years. | Violation of record retention requirements under CO-HP-007; inability to demonstrate historical compliance. | Apply 7-year retention schedule to all governance records from the date of the meeting; enforce destruction moratorium. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 The Designated Secretary — whether the Administrator or an assigned designee — shall receive orientation to this policy and the agency's meeting documentation standards within fourteen (14) calendar days of assuming the Designated Secretary role. Orientation shall be conducted by the Administrator or Compliance Officer and must cover: (a) required elements of meeting minutes per Section 6.4.2; (b) quorum documentation requirements; (c) agenda preparation and distribution timelines; (d) executive session notation procedures; (e) action item tracking obligations; (f) governance file maintenance and retention requirements.
10.2 All Governing Body members shall receive orientation to this policy within fourteen (14) calendar days of appointment, as part of the broader Governing Body orientation required by policy GV-GB-001 §10.1. Orientation for members shall cover: (a) what constitutes substantive minutes and why passive documentation creates survey risk; (b) the member's responsibility to raise questions, provide direction, and ensure their participation is reflected in the minutes; (c) the minutes approval process and how to raise corrections at the next meeting.
10.3 All Governing Body members and senior leadership within the scope of this policy (Section 3) shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within fourteen (14) calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.4 The Administrator shall maintain a tracking log of all policy acknowledgments and report any non-compliance to the Governing Body Chair within seven (7) calendar days of the acknowledgment deadline.
10.5 Annual refresher on meeting documentation standards — including review of any citations or findings from the prior year's survey or internal audit — shall be conducted at the first quarterly meeting of each calendar year and documented in those meeting minutes.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within fourteen (14) calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment.
