# Conflict of Interest Disclosure
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-GB-003 |
| Policy Title | Conflict of Interest Disclosure |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | GB — Governing Body |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 4 — Privileged |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Governing Body |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the requirements for identifying, disclosing, managing, and resolving actual, potential, and perceived conflicts of interest for all Governing Body members, senior leadership, and key personnel of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Conflicts of interest — whether financial, professional, or organizational — pose a direct risk to the integrity of the Governing Body's fiduciary duty, the agency's regulatory compliance posture, and its standing with CMS and the OIG. This policy ensures that all individuals in positions of governance authority act solely in the best interest of the agency and the patients it serves, that no individual benefits personally from decisions made in their governance capacity, and that the agency maintains the transparent, documented conflict management program required by OIG Compliance Program Guidance for Home Health Agencies and expected by CMS surveyors conducting surveys under the State Operations Manual Appendix B.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All voting and non-voting members of the Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Agency Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Compliance Officer
Any contracted management entity or individual performing governing body functions on behalf of the agency
Senior leadership personnel who participate in budget approval, vendor selection, contract execution, or strategic decision-making on behalf of the agency
Any individual appointed to serve on a subcommittee of the Governing Body with decision-making authority
This policy does not apply to front-line clinical or administrative staff except to the extent they are appointed to a role listed above or are involved in a specific transaction or decision that gives rise to a conflict of interest subject to this policy.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. requires all individuals within the scope of this policy to place the interests of the agency and the patients it serves above any personal, financial, or organizational interest when exercising governance authority or making decisions on behalf of the agency.
4.2 All individuals within scope shall complete and submit a Conflict of Interest Disclosure Form (Appendix A) at the time of initial appointment, annually at the first quarterly Governing Body meeting of each calendar year, and within seven (7) calendar days of any change in circumstances that creates a new actual or potential conflict.
4.3 The Compliance Officer shall review all submitted disclosures, assess the nature and materiality of each disclosed conflict, and present a written summary of findings and management recommendations to the Governing Body within fourteen (14) calendar days of receipt.
4.4 Any individual with a disclosed conflict of interest shall recuse themselves from all discussion, deliberation, and voting on the matter to which the conflict pertains. Recusals shall be documented in Governing Body meeting minutes and in the individual's conflict of interest file.
4.5 The Governing Body shall formally act on all conflict of interest disclosures presenting a material risk to agency integrity, document its determinations in meeting minutes, and maintain a conflict management plan for each active disclosed conflict.
4.6 No Governing Body member, senior leader, or key personnel shall use their position of governance authority to obtain personal financial gain, preferential vendor treatment, employment benefits, or any other advantage for themselves or their immediate family members that is not available to the general workforce.
4.7 Failure to disclose a known conflict of interest, submission of a materially false disclosure, or failure to recuse from a matter involving a disclosed conflict shall constitute a violation of this policy and may result in removal from the Governing Body, termination of employment or contractual relationship, and referral to appropriate regulatory authorities.
4.8 All conflict of interest records — including disclosure forms, Compliance Officer reviews, Governing Body determinations, and recusal documentation — shall be maintained in a restricted access compliance file and retained for a minimum of seven (7) years from the date of disclosure in accordance with policy CO-HP-007.
4.9 This policy shall be reviewed annually by the Governing Body. Any revision requires re-acknowledgment by all individuals within scope within fourteen (14) calendar days of the revised effective date.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Conflict of Interest | Any actual, potential, or perceived situation in which an individual's personal, financial, or organizational interests could impair — or reasonably appear to impair — their ability to act in the best interest of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. and the patients it serves. |
| Financial Interest | Any direct or indirect financial stake in a transaction, entity, or decision affecting the agency, including ownership interests, compensation arrangements, loans, gifts, or investment positions held by the individual or their immediate family. |
| Immediate Family | Spouse, domestic partner, parent, stepparent, child, stepchild, sibling, or any individual sharing a household with the disclosing party. |
| Material Conflict | A conflict of interest of sufficient magnitude — based on financial value, decision-making authority, or reputational risk — to require formal Governing Body action beyond standard recusal. Materiality is determined by the Compliance Officer with Governing Body concurrence. |
| Recusal | The formal withdrawal of an individual from discussion, deliberation, and voting on a specific matter due to a disclosed conflict of interest. Recusal must be announced, recorded in meeting minutes, and the individual must physically or virtually leave the relevant portion of the meeting unless directed otherwise by the Governing Body Chair. |
| Referral Source | Any physician, physician group, hospital, skilled nursing facility, assisted living facility, or other healthcare provider that refers patients to or receives referrals from Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Vendor | Any company, individual, or entity that provides goods, services, technology, or professional services to Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. under a contractual or recurring relationship. |
| Whistleblower | Any individual who reports a suspected conflict of interest violation by another party in good faith, entitled to full protection under policy CO-CP-005. |
| Annual Disclosure | The mandatory completion of the Conflict of Interest Disclosure Form by all individuals within scope at the beginning of each calendar year, regardless of whether any new conflict exists. |
| Conflict Management Plan | A written plan developed by the Compliance Officer and approved by the Governing Body defining the specific measures taken to manage, monitor, and mitigate an active disclosed conflict of interest, including recusal requirements, monitoring obligations, and review intervals. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Initial Disclosure at Appointment

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Administrator / Governing Body Chair | At the time of appointment or onboarding of any individual within the scope of this policy, provide the individual with: (a) a copy of this policy GV-GB-003; (b) a blank Conflict of Interest Disclosure Form (Appendix A); (c) a verbal or written explanation of the disclosure requirements, recusal obligations, and consequences of non-disclosure. | At the time of appointment; no later than the individual's first day in their governance role. |
| 6.1.2 | Newly Appointed Individual | Complete and sign the Conflict of Interest Disclosure Form in full. All sections must be completed — no section may be left blank. If no conflict exists in a given category, the individual shall affirmatively mark "None" or "No." | Completed and submitted to the Compliance Officer within 7 calendar days of appointment. |
| 6.1.3 | Compliance Officer | Receive and log the disclosure form in the conflict of interest tracking log (Appendix C). Assign a unique disclosure reference number. | Within 3 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.1.4 | Compliance Officer | Review the disclosure for completeness. If any section is incomplete or ambiguous, return the form to the individual with written instructions for correction within 5 calendar days of receipt. | Within 5 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.1.5 | Compliance Officer | Assess each disclosed item for materiality and conflict risk using the criteria in Section 6.4. Prepare a written review memo documenting: (a) each disclosed item; (b) materiality assessment; (c) recommended management action. | Within 14 calendar days of receiving a complete disclosure. |
| 6.1.6 | Compliance Officer | Present the review memo to the Governing Body at the next regular or special meeting. If the appointment occurs between meetings and the conflict is assessed as material, the Compliance Officer shall request a special meeting or secure written Governing Body concurrence within 14 calendar days of the disclosure review. | Presented at the next Governing Body meeting; material conflicts actioned within 14 calendar days of review. |

### 6.2 Annual Disclosure Process

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Compliance Officer | Distribute blank Conflict of Interest Disclosure Forms to all individuals within scope no later than 14 calendar days before the first quarterly Governing Body meeting of each calendar year. Distribution shall be accompanied by a written reminder of the disclosure obligation, recusal requirements, and the deadline for submission. | Distributed no later than 14 calendar days before the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.2.2 | Each Individual Within Scope | Complete and submit the annual Conflict of Interest Disclosure Form to the Compliance Officer by the deadline specified in the distribution notice. Annual disclosures must reflect the individual's current circumstances as of the date of signature — disclosures cannot simply reference the prior year's submission. | Submitted no later than 7 calendar days before the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.2.3 | Compliance Officer | Track receipt of all annual disclosures against the current roster of individuals within scope. Identify any individuals who have not submitted by the deadline and issue a written delinquency notice with a 5-calendar-day cure period. | Receipt tracking completed by the deadline; delinquency notices issued within 1 calendar day of the deadline for non-submitters. |
| 6.2.4 | Administrator | Report to the Governing Body Chair at the Q1 meeting the name of any individual within scope who has failed to submit their annual disclosure within the cure period. The Governing Body shall determine appropriate follow-up action including potential suspension of governance participation until disclosure is complete. | Reported at the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.2.5 | Compliance Officer | Review all annual disclosures using the same materiality assessment process in Section 6.4. Prepare a consolidated annual conflict of interest status report summarizing: (a) total disclosures received; (b) disclosures identifying active conflicts; (c) status of all existing conflict management plans; (d) any new material conflicts identified; (e) recommended actions. | Annual report completed and submitted to the Governing Body at the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.2.6 | Governing Body | Review the annual conflict of interest status report at the Q1 meeting. Formally act on any new material conflicts and confirm the continued adequacy of all existing conflict management plans. Document review and actions in Q1 meeting minutes. | At the Q1 regular quarterly meeting. |

### 6.3 Mid-Year Disclosure for Changed Circumstances

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Individual Within Scope | Upon becoming aware of any new circumstance that creates or could create an actual, potential, or perceived conflict of interest, immediately complete and submit a supplemental Conflict of Interest Disclosure Form to the Compliance Officer. Changed circumstances requiring disclosure include but are not limited to: (a) accepting employment, a consulting engagement, or a board seat with a vendor, competitor, or referral source; (b) acquiring a financial interest in any entity doing business with or competing with the agency; (c) beginning or ending a personal or family relationship with a vendor, referral source, or competitor; (d) receiving a gift, loan, or compensation from any entity in a business relationship with the agency. | Submitted within 7 calendar days of becoming aware of the new circumstance. |
| 6.3.2 | Compliance Officer | Review the supplemental disclosure using the materiality assessment process in Section 6.4. If the disclosed conflict is assessed as material and the next regular quarterly meeting is more than 30 calendar days away, the Compliance Officer shall request a special meeting or secure written Governing Body concurrence. | Review completed within 7 calendar days of receipt; material conflicts actioned within 14 calendar days. |
| 6.3.3 | Compliance Officer | If a mid-year disclosure reveals a conflict that may have affected a prior Governing Body decision, notify the Governing Body Chair immediately and preserve all documentation related to the prior decision. The Governing Body shall determine whether the prior decision should be revisited. | Notification within 3 calendar days of identifying potential impact on a prior decision. |

### 6.4 Materiality Assessment

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Compliance Officer | Assess each disclosed item against the following materiality criteria: (a) Financial Threshold — any financial interest with a fair market value exceeding $500 annually, or any ownership interest exceeding 1% in a relevant entity, is presumed material absent specific contrary finding; (b) Decision-Making Nexus — any conflict involving a matter on which the disclosing individual exercises or influences agency decision-making is presumed material; (c) Referral Relationship — any financial or professional relationship with a referral source or competitor is presumed material regardless of dollar amount; (d) OIG Sensitivity — any arrangement that could implicate the Anti-Kickback Statute or Stark Law, regardless of dollar value, is automatically material. | Completed for each disclosure within 14 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.4.2 | Compliance Officer | Document the materiality assessment in writing. For each disclosed item, record: (a) the disclosure reference number; (b) the nature of the disclosed interest; (c) the materiality determination (material / non-material); (d) the basis for the determination; (e) the recommended management action. | Documented within 14 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.4.3 | Compliance Officer | For any disclosure assessed as non-material, file the assessment memo in the individual's conflict of interest file and note the non-material determination in the next regular conflict of interest status report to the Governing Body. No additional Governing Body action is required for non-material disclosures. | Filed within 14 calendar days of receipt; noted in next status report. |
| 6.4.4 | Compliance Officer | For any disclosure assessed as material, develop a draft Conflict Management Plan (Appendix D) and submit it to the Governing Body for approval. The draft plan must specify: (a) the nature of the conflict; (b) recusal requirements — which specific matters require recusal; (c) monitoring obligations — how the conflict will be monitored over time; (d) review interval — when the plan will be reassessed; (e) termination criteria — conditions under which the conflict is resolved and the plan retired. | Draft submitted to the Governing Body within 14 calendar days of the materiality determination. |

### 6.5 Recusal Procedures

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Governing Body Chair / Administrator | Before each Governing Body meeting, review the current conflict of interest file and identify any agenda item that could implicate a disclosed conflict for any member or attendee. Notify the affected individual in writing at least 3 calendar days before the meeting of the specific agenda item requiring their recusal. | At least 3 calendar days before the meeting. |
| 6.5.2 | Individual Subject to Recusal | At the meeting, when the conflicted agenda item is called: (a) publicly announce the recusal and the general nature of the conflict (without disclosing legally protected details); (b) physically or virtually withdraw from the meeting room or session for the duration of the discussion and vote; (c) not return to the meeting until the conflicted item is fully concluded and the Chair announces the individual may rejoin. | At the time the conflicted agenda item is called. |
| 6.5.3 | Designated Secretary | Record in the meeting minutes: (a) the name and role of the individual who recused; (b) the agenda item from which they recused; (c) the stated basis for recusal (general description only); (d) the time of withdrawal and return; (e) confirmation that the individual was not present during discussion or voting on the item. | In the meeting minutes for each meeting at which a recusal occurs. |
| 6.5.4 | Compliance Officer | File a copy of the recusal record in the individual's conflict of interest file within 7 calendar days of the meeting. | Within 7 calendar days of the meeting. |
| 6.5.5 | Governing Body | If an individual subject to a recusal requirement fails to recuse, the Governing Body Chair shall immediately halt discussion on the affected item, direct the individual to withdraw, and restart the discussion after withdrawal. The failure to recuse shall be documented in the meeting minutes and referred to the Compliance Officer for determination of appropriate corrective action. | Immediately upon identification of failure to recuse. |

### 6.6 Conflict Management Plan Administration

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | Governing Body | Review and approve the Conflict Management Plan (Appendix D) submitted by the Compliance Officer for each material conflict. Approval shall be documented in meeting minutes. | At the next Governing Body meeting following submission of the draft plan; no longer than 30 calendar days after the materiality determination. |
| 6.6.2 | Compliance Officer | Distribute the approved Conflict Management Plan to the affected individual and maintain the original in the conflict of interest file. The individual shall sign an acknowledgment of the plan within 7 calendar days of receipt. | Within 3 calendar days of Governing Body approval. |
| 6.6.3 | Compliance Officer | Monitor compliance with each active Conflict Management Plan at the interval specified in the plan. Monitoring activities may include: (a) confirming the conflict has not expanded in scope; (b) verifying recusal compliance at each meeting; (c) confirming no agency decision has been influenced by the conflict without proper disclosure and recusal. | Per the interval specified in each plan; minimum quarterly. |
| 6.6.4 | Compliance Officer | Report the status of all active Conflict Management Plans to the Governing Body in the annual conflict of interest status report and at any quarterly meeting where a material change in a conflict's status is identified. | Annually at Q1; additionally as needed. |
| 6.6.5 | Compliance Officer | When the termination criteria specified in a Conflict Management Plan are met — for example, the individual has divested the financial interest, ended the conflicting relationship, or left the role triggering the conflict — prepare a written plan termination memo and submit it to the Governing Body for approval. | Within 14 calendar days of the termination criteria being satisfied. |

### 6.7 Investigation of Undisclosed Conflicts

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.7.1 | Any Individual | Report a suspected undisclosed conflict of interest through any available channel: (a) directly to the Compliance Officer; (b) via the compliance hotline per policy CO-CP-006; (c) in writing to the Governing Body Chair. All reports shall be treated as confidential. Reporters acting in good faith shall be protected from retaliation per policy CO-CP-005. | Upon becoming aware of a suspected undisclosed conflict. |
| 6.7.2 | Compliance Officer | Upon receipt of a report of suspected undisclosed conflict, initiate a preliminary inquiry within 7 calendar days to assess whether the report has sufficient basis to warrant a formal investigation. The preliminary inquiry shall include: (a) review of all prior disclosure forms from the subject individual; (b) review of relevant meeting minutes for evidence of participation in matters where a conflict may have existed; (c) interview with the reporting party if appropriate. | Preliminary inquiry initiated within 7 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.7.3 | Compliance Officer | If the preliminary inquiry supports a reasonable basis for concern, initiate a formal compliance investigation per policy CO-CP-007. Notify the Governing Body Chair in writing within 3 calendar days of determining a formal investigation is warranted. | Within 3 calendar days of the decision to initiate a formal investigation. |
| 6.7.4 | Governing Body | Upon notification of a formal investigation involving a Governing Body member, direct the subject member to recuse from all Governing Body matters until the investigation is concluded. Document the directive and recusal in meeting minutes. | Immediately upon notification; documented at the next meeting or in a special resolution. |
| 6.7.5 | Compliance Officer | Complete the investigation per policy CO-CP-007 and present findings and recommendations to the Governing Body in executive session. | Within the timeframe defined in policy CO-CP-007; findings presented at the next Governing Body meeting after investigation completion. |
| 6.7.6 | Governing Body | Based on investigation findings, determine appropriate action which may include: (a) no action — conflict was disclosed and managed; (b) required retroactive disclosure and enhanced monitoring; (c) removal from the Governing Body; (d) referral to legal counsel; (e) voluntary repayment of improperly obtained benefits; (f) notification to applicable regulatory authorities. Document the determination in executive session minutes. | Determination made at the meeting where findings are presented; documented in executive session log per GV-GB-002. |

### 6.8 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Individual within scope fails to submit annual disclosure by deadline and does not respond to delinquency notice. | Administrator notifies Governing Body Chair in writing. | Governing Body Chair issues a formal written demand for disclosure with a 7-calendar-day deadline. Continued non-compliance results in suspension of governance participation until disclosure is complete. Governing Body determines additional consequences at next meeting. | Governing Body Chair notified within 3 calendar days of delinquency notice expiration; suspension effective immediately upon non-response to Chair demand. |
| Individual participates in a vote or decision on a matter involving an undisclosed conflict. | Compliance Officer notifies Governing Body Chair immediately. | Governing Body evaluates whether the decision should be voided and re-decided without the conflicted individual's participation. Compliance investigation initiated per Section 6.7. | Compliance Officer notification within 1 business day of identification; Governing Body evaluation at next meeting or via special resolution. |
| Disclosed conflict involves an arrangement potentially violating the Anti-Kickback Statute or Stark Law. | Compliance Officer notifies Governing Body Chair and agency legal counsel immediately. | Agency legal counsel engaged within 7 calendar days. No further action on the conflicted matter until legal counsel advises. All related documentation preserved under litigation hold. | Legal counsel engaged within 7 calendar days; litigation hold immediate. |
| Governing Body Chair is the subject of a conflict of interest investigation. | Compliance Officer notifies the full Governing Body (excluding the Chair) in writing. | The remaining Governing Body members designate an acting Chair for the duration of the investigation. The subject Chair recuses from all Governing Body matters. | Designation of acting Chair within 5 calendar days of notification. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All individuals within scope submit annual disclosures by the Q1 deadline. | Review of disclosure tracking log against current roster of in-scope individuals. | 100% submission rate; no lapsed disclosures; cure period applied and resolved for any late submissions. |
| Compliance Officer reviews all disclosures within 14 calendar days. | Review of materiality assessment memo dates against disclosure receipt dates in the tracking log. | 100% of disclosures reviewed within 14 calendar days; no review outstanding. |
| Annual conflict of interest status report presented at Q1 meeting. | Review of Q1 meeting agenda and minutes for presentation confirmation. | Report presented at 100% of Q1 meetings; documented in minutes. |
| All material conflicts have an approved Conflict Management Plan. | Review of active Conflict Management Plans against list of material disclosures. | 100% of material conflicts have an approved, current plan; no plan expired or lapsed. |
| All recusals are properly documented in meeting minutes. | Content review of meeting minutes for recusal entries whenever a conflict is on file for a member. | 100% of required recusals documented in minutes with individual name, item, basis, and confirmation of withdrawal. |
| Individuals under recusal obligation do not participate in conflicted matters. | Cross-reference of meeting minutes with active Conflict Management Plans and recusal requirements. | Zero instances of documented participation in a conflicted matter without proper recusal. |
| No undisclosed conflicts identified through audit or investigation. | Annual review of vendor relationships, contracts, and referral sources cross-referenced against Governing Body member profiles. | Zero confirmed undisclosed conflicts; any identified conflicts investigated and resolved within defined timeframes. |
| All conflict records retained for minimum 7 years. | Records retention audit against governance file index. | All records with a date within the past 7 years present and intact per CO-HP-007. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors and OIG reviewers assessing the agency's compliance program under SOM Appendix B and OIG Compliance Program Guidance will specifically verify:
Evidence that a formal conflict of interest policy exists and is implemented. Surveyors will request the policy and verify it applies to all Governing Body members and senior leadership, not just the board chair.
Evidence of annual disclosure completion. Surveyors will request completed disclosure forms for all current Governing Body members and verify they are current. A disclosure form that is more than 12 months old without a renewal is a deficiency.
Evidence that disclosures are reviewed and acted upon. Surveyors will look for Compliance Officer review memos and Governing Body action in meeting minutes. A stack of disclosure forms with no evidence of review is a compliance program deficiency.
Evidence of recusal compliance. If surveyors identify a disclosed conflict, they will cross-reference meeting minutes to verify the individual did not participate in discussion or vote on the conflicted matter. Meeting minutes that do not document recusals are a common deficiency.
Evidence of conflict management plans for material conflicts. Surveyors will look for written plans defining how active conflicts are monitored. No plan means no management — surveyors treat this as a governance failure.
Evidence of a referral relationship review. OIG reviewers in particular will assess whether the agency has examined its Governing Body members' and senior leaders' relationships with referral sources and vendors for Anti-Kickback Statute risk.
Confidentiality and access controls on disclosure records. Surveyors will verify that disclosure forms are maintained in a restricted access file, not in general operational files visible to all staff.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Disclosure forms collected but never reviewed by Compliance Officer. | OIG and CMS citation for a non-functional compliance program; disclosure without review equals no management. | Compliance Officer completes materiality assessment memo for every disclosure within 14 calendar days; tracked in Appendix C log. |
| Annual disclosures lapse — forms not collected every year. | Surveyor treats the program as non-functional if disclosures are not current. | Compliance Officer issues distribution notices 14 days before Q1 meeting; Administrator tracks and reports non-submitters at Q1. |
| Recusal not documented in meeting minutes even when member verbally stepped out. | Surveyor treats undocumented recusal as non-recusal; prior decision may be voided. | Designated Secretary uses Appendix A meeting minutes template from GV-GB-002 which includes a recusal documentation field. |
| Conflict Management Plans not developed for material conflicts. | No evidence of active conflict management; OIG finding of inadequate compliance program. | Compliance Officer drafts plan within 14 calendar days of materiality determination; Governing Body approval documented in minutes. |
| Disclosure forms contain blanks or "N/A" without explanation. | Incomplete disclosure treated as failure to disclose; individual may be presumed to have undisclosed interests. | Compliance Officer returns incomplete forms within 5 calendar days with written correction instructions; no form is filed until complete. |
| Conflict of interest file accessible to general staff. | HIPAA and confidentiality risk; chilling effect on disclosure if employees fear exposure. | Restricted file maintained separately by Compliance Officer; access limited to Governing Body, Administrator, and legal counsel. |
| Governing Body votes to approve a vendor contract without checking whether any member holds an interest in the vendor. | Anti-Kickback Statute exposure; OIG sanction risk; potential voiding of contract. | Administrator reviews all active Conflict Management Plans and annual disclosures before each contract approval agenda item; any potential nexus flagged to Chair in advance. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All individuals within the scope of this policy shall receive orientation to this policy within fourteen (14) calendar days of appointment to a covered role. Orientation shall be conducted by the Compliance Officer and must cover: (a) the definition of a conflict of interest and examples relevant to a home health agency governance context; (b) disclosure obligations — when and how to disclose; (c) recusal requirements and procedures; (d) consequences of non-disclosure or failure to recuse; (e) available reporting channels for suspected conflicts by others; (f) whistleblower protections.
10.2 All individuals within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) within fourteen (14) calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.3 Annual refresher on conflict of interest obligations shall be incorporated into the annual conflict of interest disclosure process at the Q1 meeting. The Compliance Officer shall distribute a written summary of key policy requirements and any updates with the annual disclosure forms. The Governing Body Chair shall confirm completion of the refresher in Q1 meeting minutes.
10.4 The Administrator shall maintain a tracking log of all policy acknowledgments and report any non-compliance to the Governing Body Chair within seven (7) calendar days of the acknowledgment deadline.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within fourteen (14) calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

## 1. Policy Header

| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | GB — Governing Body |

| Review Cycle | Biennial |
| Access Tier | Tier 4 — Privileged |

| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the requirements for succession planning for the key leadership positions of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. — specifically the Administrator, the Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager, and the Compliance Officer. Leadership vacancies in these roles, if unfilled or inadequately managed, directly jeopardize the agency's regulatory compliance, patient safety, and operational continuity. CMS requires under 42 CFR § 484.105 that a qualified Administrator and Clinical Manager are in place at all times; extended vacancies without a designated interim constitute a Condition-level deficiency risk. This policy ensures the Governing Body maintains a proactive, documented succession strategy that enables the agency to fill or cover key leadership vacancies with qualified individuals without interruption to agency operations or compliance obligations.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Governing Body Chair
The Agency Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Compliance Officer
The HR Director (in support of succession candidate identification and readiness assessment)
This policy governs succession planning for the following key leadership positions:
Administrator — the individual responsible for day-to-day management of the agency, appointed by the Governing Body per 42 CFR § 484.105(b).
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager — the registered nurse responsible for oversight of all clinical services, per 42 CFR § 484.105(c).
Compliance Officer — the individual designated with authority and independence to operate the corporate compliance program per policy CO-CP-002.
This policy does not govern succession planning for positions below the three key leadership roles listed above unless specifically directed by the Governing Body.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a current, Governing Body-approved Succession Plan (Appendix A) identifying a designated interim successor for each of the three key leadership positions at all times.
4.2 The Succession Plan shall be reviewed and approved by the Governing Body at the second quarterly meeting (Q2) of each calendar year and updated within fourteen (14) calendar days of any key leadership vacancy, change in successor availability, or material change in the agency's organizational structure.
4.3 For each key leadership position, the Succession Plan shall identify: (a) the current role holder; (b) a primary interim designee who is pre-qualified and available to assume interim responsibility on short notice; (c) a secondary interim designee as backup; (d) the qualifications confirmed for each designee; and (e) the conditions and notification process for activating the succession plan.
4.4 Upon a key leadership vacancy — whether anticipated (planned departure, leave of absence) or unanticipated (sudden resignation, incapacitation, termination) — the Governing Body shall appoint a qualified interim designee within fourteen (14) calendar days of the vacancy and a permanent replacement within ninety (90) calendar days, consistent with the escalation requirements of policy GV-GB-001 §6.5.
4.5 An interim designee appointed under this policy must meet the minimum qualifications for the role as defined in policy GV-OG-002 (Administrator), applicable California state law (Clinical Manager), or policy CO-CP-002 (Compliance Officer). No individual shall serve as interim in a role for which they do not meet minimum qualifications.
4.6 The Succession Plan shall be maintained as a privileged, restricted-access document. Distribution shall be limited to the Governing Body, the Administrator, and the Compliance Officer.
4.7 This policy shall be reviewed biennially by the Governing Body, consistent with its ESSENTIAL classification tier. Any substantive revision requires re-acknowledgment by all individuals within scope within fourteen (14) calendar days of the revised effective date.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Key Leadership Position | For purposes of this policy: the Administrator, Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager, and Compliance Officer of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Vacancy | Any condition in which a key leadership position is not filled by a currently active, qualified individual. Includes planned and unplanned departures, extended leaves of absence exceeding 30 calendar days, incapacity, termination, and death. |
| Interim Designee | A pre-qualified individual identified in the Succession Plan and authorized to assume interim responsibility for a key leadership position upon activation of this policy. |
| Succession Plan | The written document approved by the Governing Body identifying current role holders, primary and secondary interim designees, confirmed qualifications, and activation procedures for all three key leadership positions. |
| Anticipated Vacancy | A vacancy that is known to be forthcoming with at least 30 calendar days advance notice, including planned retirements, resignations with notice, and scheduled leaves of absence. |
| Unanticipated Vacancy | A vacancy that arises without advance notice, including sudden resignation, incapacitation, death, or immediate termination for cause. |
| Activation | The formal process of invoking the Succession Plan to appoint an interim designee to a vacant key leadership position. |
| Permanent Replacement | A newly recruited and appointed individual who permanently assumes a key leadership role, replacing the interim designee. |
| Qualification Verification | The documented confirmation that a designee meets all minimum qualifications for the key leadership role, including education, licensure, experience, and background check requirements. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Development and Maintenance of the Succession Plan

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Administrator | Prepare a draft Succession Plan (Appendix A) for all three key leadership positions. For each position, identify: (a) the current role holder's name, qualifications, and tenure; (b) the primary interim designee — name, title, confirmed qualifications, and contact information; (c) the secondary interim designee — same information; (d) a brief notation of any known factors that could affect designee availability (e.g., dual roles, geographic limitations). | Draft prepared no later than 30 calendar days before the Q2 Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator | Verify the qualifications of each identified designee against the minimum qualification standards for the respective role: (a) Administrator designee — per policy GV-OG-002 and applicable California state requirements; (b) Clinical Manager designee — registered nurse license current and active in California; meets supervisory experience requirements under California Code of Regulations and CMS CoP; (c) Compliance Officer designee — sufficient knowledge of healthcare compliance, fraud and abuse, and HIPAA to operate the compliance program independently. | Qualification verification completed before submitting the draft plan. |
| 6.1.3 | Administrator | Submit the draft Succession Plan and qualification verification documentation to the Governing Body Chair no fewer than 7 calendar days before the Q2 meeting. | At least 7 calendar days before Q2 meeting. |
| 6.1.4 | Governing Body | Review, discuss, and approve or request revision of the Succession Plan at the Q2 regular quarterly meeting. The Governing Body shall specifically confirm: (a) the adequacy of each designee's qualifications; (b) the realism of designee availability; (c) the adequacy of the activation process. Approval shall be documented in Q2 meeting minutes. | At the Q2 regular quarterly meeting. |
| 6.1.5 | Administrator | Distribute the approved Succession Plan to the Governing Body Chair and Compliance Officer. File the original in the restricted governance file. Notify each identified designee that they have been named in the plan and confirm their willingness to serve in the interim role if activated. Obtain written confirmation from each designee. | Within 7 calendar days of Governing Body approval. |
| 6.1.6 | Administrator | Review and update the Succession Plan on a biennial basis in preparation for the Q2 meeting of the biennial review year, or immediately upon any of the following triggering events: (a) a current role holder departs, announces departure, or begins an extended leave; (b) an identified designee is no longer available, willing, or qualified; (c) a material change in the agency's organizational structure affects role definitions or reporting relationships; (d) the Governing Body directs an update. | Biennial review completed before Q2 of the review year; triggering event updates within 14 calendar days. |

### 6.2 Anticipated Vacancy Management

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Current Role Holder | Provide written notice of an anticipated departure or extended leave to the Administrator and Governing Body Chair as early as possible and no less than 30 calendar days before the effective date of the vacancy, unless exigent circumstances prevent advance notice. | At least 30 calendar days before the vacancy effective date. |
| 6.2.2 | Administrator | Upon receipt of an anticipated vacancy notice, immediately review the current Succession Plan and confirm the availability and readiness of the primary interim designee for the affected position. | Within 5 calendar days of receiving vacancy notice. |
| 6.2.3 | Administrator | Notify the Governing Body Chair in writing of the anticipated vacancy, confirming: (a) the position affected; (b) the effective date of the vacancy; (c) the primary interim designee identified in the Succession Plan; (d) confirmation of the designee's current qualifications; (e) the recommended date for interim appointment. | Within 5 calendar days of confirming designee availability. |
| 6.2.4 | Governing Body | Formally appoint the interim designee by Governing Body resolution or at the next regular or special meeting. Document the appointment in meeting minutes, including the designee's qualifications, the effective date of interim appointment, and the anticipated duration. | No later than the date of the vacancy if an anticipated vacancy allows advance scheduling; otherwise within 14 calendar days of the vacancy effective date. |
| 6.2.5 | Administrator | Initiate recruitment for the permanent replacement immediately upon confirming the anticipated vacancy. Coordinate the transition plan between the departing role holder, the interim designee, and the recruitment process. | Recruitment initiated within 14 calendar days of confirmed vacancy notice. |
| 6.2.6 | Departing Role Holder | Complete a knowledge transfer package (Appendix B) for the interim designee covering: (a) active projects and pending matters; (b) regulatory deadlines and upcoming compliance obligations; (c) key external contacts and relationships; (d) location of critical files, records, and passwords (as applicable per IT-SC-002); (e) open corrective actions or investigation matters. | Completed before the effective date of departure; if departure is immediate, completed within 7 calendar days as circumstances permit. |

### 6.3 Unanticipated Vacancy Management

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Governing Body Chair or Administrator (whichever first learns of the vacancy) | Upon learning of an unanticipated vacancy in a key leadership position, immediately contact the primary interim designee named in the current Succession Plan to confirm availability for immediate activation. | Within 24 hours of learning of the vacancy. |
| 6.3.2 | Governing Body Chair | Issue written notification to all Governing Body members of the unanticipated vacancy and initiate the appointment process. If the primary designee is not available, contact the secondary designee. | Within 24 hours of confirming the vacancy. |
| 6.3.3 | Governing Body | Formally appoint the interim designee by Governing Body resolution (which may be obtained in writing between meetings for urgent appointment) or at a special meeting. Document the appointment with the designee's name, qualifications, effective date, and anticipated interim duration. | Within 14 calendar days of the vacancy; interim appointment formal documentation as soon as practicable. |
| 6.3.4 | Administrator | Compile an emergency knowledge transfer summary from available records and brief the interim designee on: (a) all immediate operational obligations; (b) any active regulatory, compliance, or clinical matters requiring immediate attention; (c) CMS or HCAI reporting obligations that may be pending. | Within 48 hours of interim appointment activation. |
| 6.3.5 | Administrator | Notify the Compliance Officer of the vacancy and confirm that no reportable event obligations are triggered by the vacancy (e.g., state licensure reporting requirements for DON vacancy). | Within 24 hours of confirmed vacancy. |
| 6.3.6 | Compliance Officer | Verify whether any federal or state regulatory body requires notification of the key leadership vacancy and, if so, ensure timely notification per applicable requirements. | Within 3 calendar days of confirmed vacancy; notification submitted within applicable regulatory timeframes. |
| 6.3.7 | Administrator | Initiate recruitment for the permanent replacement immediately following the interim appointment. | Recruitment initiated within 7 calendar days of interim appointment. |

### 6.4 Permanent Replacement Process

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator | Conduct a search for qualified permanent replacement candidates using the minimum qualification standards applicable to the role. For Administrator and Clinical Manager searches, involve the Governing Body Chair in the candidate selection process. | Search initiated within 14 calendar days of the vacancy; Governing Body Chair engaged from initiation. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator | Present the final candidate for each key leadership position to the Governing Body for formal appointment approval. Provide the Governing Body with documentation of: (a) the candidate's resume and credentials; (b) qualification verification including license current and active status confirmation; (c) background check and OIG/SAM screening results per policies HR-TA-002 and HR-TA-003; (d) reference check results. | Presented to the Governing Body within 75 calendar days of the vacancy to allow appointment by the 90-day deadline. |
| 6.4.3 | Governing Body | Formally appoint the permanent replacement by vote at a regular or special meeting. Document in meeting minutes: (a) the candidate's name and qualifications; (b) the vote outcome; (c) the permanent appointment effective date. | Appointment made no later than 90 calendar days from the date of the vacancy per GV-GB-001 §6.5. |
| 6.4.4 | Interim Designee | Transition responsibilities to the permanent replacement in an organized manner. Prepare an updated knowledge transfer package (Appendix B) reflecting activities and developments during the interim period. | Transition completed within 14 calendar days of the permanent appointment effective date. |
| 6.4.5 | Administrator | Update the Succession Plan (Appendix A) to reflect the new permanent role holder and reassess designee availability now that the interim period has concluded. Submit the updated plan to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. | Updated plan submitted within 30 calendar days of permanent appointment; presented at next regular quarterly meeting. |

### 6.5 Succession Plan Activation Log

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator | Maintain an Activation Log (Appendix C) documenting each instance in which the Succession Plan is activated, including: (a) the position affected; (b) the nature of the vacancy (anticipated or unanticipated); (c) the date of vacancy; (d) the interim designee appointed; (e) the effective date of interim appointment; (f) the permanent replacement appointed; (g) the effective date of permanent appointment; (h) the total duration of the vacancy. | Log updated within 3 calendar days of each activation event milestone. |
| 6.5.2 | Administrator | Present the Activation Log to the Governing Body at any meeting following a succession event and confirm all required actions were completed within the policy timeframes. | At the next regular Governing Body meeting following each activation. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| No qualified primary or secondary interim designee is available at the time of a vacancy. | Administrator immediately notifies Governing Body Chair. | Governing Body convenes a special meeting within 5 calendar days to identify and appoint an emergency interim designee. If no internal candidate is available, the Governing Body shall engage a qualified interim management consultant or staffing agency. | Special meeting within 5 calendar days; interim appointment within 14 calendar days of vacancy regardless of internal vs. external source. |
| Interim period exceeds 90 calendar days without a permanent appointment. | Compliance Officer notifies Governing Body Chair in writing and documents as a compliance deficiency. | Governing Body directs immediate escalation of the recruitment effort and issues a written timeline for permanent appointment with weekly status updates to the Governing Body Chair. Compliance Officer documents the deficiency per policy QA-AE-003. | Compliance Officer notification on day 91; written timeline issued within 7 calendar days; weekly updates until resolved. |
| Identified interim designee refuses activation when called upon. | Administrator notifies Governing Body Chair immediately. | Secondary designee activated. Administrator revises Succession Plan to replace the non-available primary designee within 14 calendar days. | Secondary activation immediate; plan revision within 14 calendar days. |
| Interim designee's qualifications are challenged during the interim period. | Compliance Officer notifies Governing Body Chair. | Governing Body reviews qualification documentation and determines whether the interim appointment should continue or be replaced. Resolution documented in meeting minutes. | Governing Body review within 7 calendar days of notification. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Approved Succession Plan | Current Governing Body-approved Succession Plan (Appendix A) for all three key leadership positions. | Administrator (preparation); Governing Body (approval) | Restricted governance file; copies to Governing Body Chair and Compliance Officer. | Approved biennially at Q2; updated within 14 calendar days of triggering events. |
| Designee qualification verification | Documentation confirming qualifications of each identified interim designee (license verification, credential check, background check results). | Administrator | Restricted governance file. | Completed before plan submission to Governing Body; updated at each biennial review. |
| Designee written confirmation | Written confirmation from each identified designee of their willingness to serve in the interim role if activated. | Administrator (collection) | Restricted governance file. | Obtained within 7 calendar days of plan approval; renewed at each biennial review. |
| Governing Body appointment documentation | Governing Body meeting minutes or written resolution documenting the appointment of each interim and permanent replacement. | Designated Secretary | Governing Body meeting minutes; governance file. | At time of appointment; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Knowledge transfer package | Completed Appendix B knowledge transfer documentation for each succession event. | Departing role holder (completion); Administrator (filing) | Restricted governance file. | Completed before departure or within 7 calendar days for unanticipated vacancies. |
| Activation log | Cumulative log of all succession plan activations (Appendix C). | Administrator | Restricted governance file. | Updated within 3 calendar days of each activation milestone; presented to Governing Body after each event. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy by all individuals within scope (Appendix D). | Each individual (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Succession Plan is current and Governing Body-approved. | Review of approved plan in governance file; confirmation of Governing Body approval in Q2 meeting minutes (biennial review years). | Current approved plan on file at all times; approval documented in meeting minutes within the biennial cycle. |
| Each key position has identified primary and secondary designees with verified qualifications. | Review of Succession Plan Appendix A; qualification verification documentation. | All three positions have current primary and secondary designees with complete qualification verification on file. |
| Interim designee appointed within 14 calendar days of any vacancy. | Review of Governing Body minutes or written resolution for appointment date against vacancy date. | 100% of vacancies result in interim appointment within 14 calendar days; zero instances of vacancy exceeding 14 days without documented appointment. |
| Permanent replacement appointed within 90 calendar days. | Review of appointment minutes against vacancy date. | 100% of vacancies result in permanent appointment within 90 calendar days; any exception documented with escalation evidence. |
| Activation log maintained and presented to Governing Body. | Review of activation log for completeness; confirmation of presentation in meeting minutes following each event. | Log current and presented after each activation; no event undocumented. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under SOM Appendix B will specifically verify:
Evidence that the Administrator and Clinical Manager positions are continuously filled. Surveyors will confirm no undesignated vacancy exceeds 30 calendar days. They will request appointment documentation and verify interim designees meet minimum qualifications.
Evidence of a proactive succession plan. Surveyors increasingly look for evidence of governance planning rather than reactive crisis management. A documented, Governing Body-approved succession plan with named and pre-qualified designees demonstrates governance maturity.
Evidence that interim appointments were timely and documented. Surveyors will cross-reference the date of any vacancy against the date of interim appointment documentation. An undocumented gap is a survey risk even if the vacancy was brief.
Evidence that interim designees were qualified. Surveyors will request credential documentation for interim designees. An interim Clinical Manager who is not a currently licensed RN in California is a Condition-level deficiency.
Evidence that permanent replacements were recruited actively. Surveyors expect to see evidence of recruitment activity, not merely passive waiting, during the interim period.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No written Succession Plan exists — vacancies handled ad hoc. | CMS survey finding; demonstrates lack of governance planning; delays interim appointment beyond 14-day deadline. | Succession Plan maintained and approved biennially at Q2; updated on all triggering events per Section 6.1.6. |
| Interim designee does not meet minimum qualifications for the role. | Condition-level deficiency for Administrator or Clinical Manager vacancy; compliance program integrity failure for Compliance Officer vacancy. | Qualifications verified before plan approval; verification documented in restricted governance file. |
| Interim appointment not formally documented by Governing Body. | Surveyor treats position as unfilled even if someone is functionally performing the role. | All appointments documented in meeting minutes or written resolution per Section 6.2.4 and 6.3.3. |
| Succession Plan not reviewed after a vacancy is filled. | Plan remains stale; designees may no longer be available or qualified for future vacancies. | Section 6.4.5 requires plan update within 30 calendar days of permanent appointment. |
| Knowledge transfer not completed, causing operational disruption. | Clinical or compliance matters fall through gaps during transition; audit trail breaks. | Knowledge transfer package (Appendix B) required for all succession events; Administrator oversees completion. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 The Administrator, Governing Body members, and each identified interim designee shall receive orientation to this policy within fourteen (14) calendar days of appointment or designation. Orientation shall cover: (a) the three key leadership positions covered; (b) qualification requirements for each role; (c) activation procedures for anticipated and unanticipated vacancies; (d) the 14-day interim and 90-day permanent appointment deadlines; (e) knowledge transfer expectations.
10.2 Each identified interim designee shall confirm in writing that they have reviewed this policy and understand their obligations upon plan activation. Written confirmation shall be maintained in the restricted governance file.
10.3 All individuals within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix D) within fourteen (14) calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.4 The Succession Plan shall be reviewed biennially as a standing Q2 agenda item. The review shall include confirmation of designee willingness and qualification currency.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy is valid. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision requires: (a) Governing Body approval documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within fourteen (14) calendar days; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

ACTIVATION INSTRUCTIONS
Upon a vacancy in any key leadership position:
Administrator (or Governing Body Chair if Administrator position is vacant) contacts the primary interim designee within 24 hours.
If primary designee is unavailable, contact secondary designee immediately.
Administrator notifies Governing Body Chair in writing within 24 hours of confirming the vacancy.
Governing Body appoints interim designee within 14 calendar days of vacancy.
Administrator initiates permanent recruitment within 14 calendar days of vacancy (anticipated) or 7 calendar days (unanticipated).
Permanent appointment made within 90 calendar days of vacancy.
