# Annual Governance Self-Assessment
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-GB-005 |
| Policy Title | Annual Governance Self-Assessment |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | GB — Governing Body |
| Classification Tier | RECOMMENDED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 4 — Privileged |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Governing Body |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the requirements for the Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to conduct an annual self-assessment of its own governance effectiveness, regulatory compliance performance, and fulfillment of its oversight obligations. While not explicitly mandated as a standalone requirement by 42 CFR § 484.105, an annual governance self-assessment is the recognized industry best practice for demonstrating that the Governing Body is actively evaluating — and continuously improving — the quality and effectiveness of its governance functions. The self-assessment provides documented evidence that the Governing Body is not merely a passive recipient of reports but an active, reflective governance body that measures itself against defined standards. This evidence is increasingly relied upon by CMS surveyors assessing governance functioning under SOM Appendix B and by OIG reviewers evaluating compliance program effectiveness. This policy establishes a structured, consistent self-assessment process that produces a written record usable for continuous quality improvement, survey readiness, and strategic governance planning.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All voting and non-voting members of the Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Governing Body Chair (primary responsibility for process administration)
The Agency Administrator (support role — logistics, scheduling, record maintenance)
The Compliance Officer (support role — self-assessment tool preparation and results filing)
This policy governs the conduct of the annual Governing Body self-assessment only. It does not govern operational self-assessments, clinical audits, departmental performance reviews, or the QAPI program self-assessment activities, which are governed by their respective domain policies.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall conduct an annual self-assessment of its governance effectiveness using the structured self-assessment tool provided in Appendix A of this policy.
4.2 The annual self-assessment shall be conducted at the first quarterly meeting (Q1) of each calendar year, immediately following or as part of the annual governance refresher training required by policy GV-GB-001 §10.4.
4.3 The self-assessment shall be completed individually by each Governing Body member and then reviewed collectively by the full Governing Body as a group discussion item. Individual responses shall remain confidential; only the aggregated findings and identified improvement priorities shall be documented in meeting minutes.
4.4 The Governing Body Chair shall prepare or oversee preparation of a written Governance Self-Assessment Summary Report (Appendix B) compiling the aggregated findings, identifying governance strengths, and defining a Governance Improvement Action Plan for any area rated as needing improvement.
4.5 The Governance Self-Assessment Summary Report and Improvement Action Plan shall be filed in the restricted governance file and referenced in Q1 meeting minutes as completed. Individual member assessment forms shall be maintained as privileged records accessible only to the Governing Body Chair and Compliance Officer.
4.6 At the Q1 meeting of the following year, the Governing Body shall review the prior year's Improvement Action Plan as the first step in the new self-assessment cycle, confirming which improvement actions were completed and identifying any that remain open.
4.7 This policy shall be reviewed annually. Any revision requires re-acknowledgment by all Governing Body members within fourteen (14) calendar days of the revised effective date.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Governance Self-Assessment | A structured, documented process by which the Governing Body evaluates its own performance against defined governance standards, identifies strengths and improvement areas, and develops action plans to address deficiencies. |
| Self-Assessment Tool | The standardized questionnaire provided in Appendix A of this policy, used by each Governing Body member individually to rate governance performance across defined domains. |
| Aggregated Findings | The compiled, anonymized results of all individual member self-assessments, analyzed to identify patterns, consensus strengths, and consensus improvement areas across the Governing Body as a whole. |
| Governance Improvement Action Plan | A written action plan developed from the self-assessment findings, identifying specific improvement priorities, responsible parties, target completion dates, and success metrics. |
| Self-Assessment Cycle | The annual process from individual assessment completion through group discussion, summary report preparation, action plan development, implementation monitoring, and prior year action plan review at the next cycle's Q1 meeting. |
| Privileged Record | A governance document classified as Tier 4 — Privileged, accessible only to Governing Body members, the Compliance Officer, and authorized legal counsel, and not subject to general staff access or CMS surveyor review without legal counsel guidance. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Self-Assessment Preparation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Compliance Officer | Prepare the annual self-assessment materials for distribution to each Governing Body member. Materials shall include: (a) a copy of the Self-Assessment Tool (Appendix A); (b) a copy of the prior year's Governance Self-Assessment Summary Report and Improvement Action Plan for reference; (c) a copy of the current year's policy GV-GB-001 (Governing Body Authority & Responsibilities) for reference; (d) instructions for completing the self-assessment form. | Materials prepared and distributed to all Governing Body members no later than 14 calendar days before the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.1.2 | Governing Body Chair | Review the prior year's Improvement Action Plan and prepare a brief status summary identifying: (a) improvement actions completed since the prior Q1; (b) improvement actions still in progress; (c) improvement actions not initiated with explanation. This summary will be presented at Q1 before the new self-assessment discussion begins. | Summary prepared no later than 7 calendar days before the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.1.3 | Administrator | Schedule dedicated time within the Q1 meeting agenda for the self-assessment process. The agenda shall allocate a minimum of 30 minutes for: (a) prior year action plan status review (10 minutes); (b) individual member self-assessment completion if not completed in advance (10 minutes); (c) group discussion of aggregated findings (20 minutes). If members are asked to complete the self-assessment before the meeting, the agenda shall allocate at least 20 minutes for group discussion. | Scheduled and reflected in the Q1 agenda distributed 7 calendar days before the meeting. |

### 6.2 Individual Self-Assessment Completion

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Each Governing Body Member | Complete the Self-Assessment Tool (Appendix A) individually and independently. Responses shall reflect the member's honest, independent assessment of Governing Body performance over the prior 12 months. Members shall not consult with one another before completing their individual forms. | Completed either before the Q1 meeting (submitted to Compliance Officer by 3 days before the meeting) or at the start of the Q1 meeting before group discussion. |
| 6.2.2 | Each Governing Body Member | Submit the completed Self-Assessment Tool to the Compliance Officer. Forms shall not be submitted to the Administrator, the Governing Body Chair, or any other individual. Submission to the Compliance Officer ensures confidentiality of individual responses. | Submitted to Compliance Officer per the timeline in step 6.2.1. |
| 6.2.3 | Compliance Officer | Receive all individual assessment forms, confirm receipt from each member, and compile aggregated results by domain using the aggregation template within Appendix A. Individual responses shall not be attributed to specific members in any compilation or report. | Aggregation completed before the Q1 meeting; if forms are submitted at the meeting, aggregation completed during the meeting break or immediately following individual completion. |

### 6.3 Group Discussion and Findings Review

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Governing Body Chair | Present the prior year's Improvement Action Plan status summary at the start of the self-assessment agenda item. The Governing Body shall formally acknowledge completed actions and note any actions remaining open. | At the Q1 meeting, before new self-assessment discussion begins. |
| 6.3.2 | Compliance Officer | Present the aggregated findings from the current year's individual assessments by domain. The presentation shall identify: (a) domains rated as strength (majority of members rated as performing well); (b) domains rated as needing improvement (significant number of members identified gaps or weaknesses); (c) any domain rated critically low by two or more members independently. | At the Q1 meeting, following presentation of the prior year status summary. |
| 6.3.3 | Governing Body | Discuss the aggregated findings as a group. Discussion shall address: (a) the root causes of any domain rated as needing improvement; (b) whether the identified gaps reflect a training need, a process gap, a resource gap, or a structural governance issue; (c) specific, actionable improvements the Governing Body can make in the coming year; (d) whether any finding requires immediate corrective action (e.g., a gap in QAPI oversight or conflict of interest management that poses regulatory risk). | At the Q1 meeting; minimum 20 minutes allocated. |
| 6.3.4 | Designated Secretary | Record in Q1 meeting minutes: (a) that the annual governance self-assessment was conducted; (b) the domains identified as strengths; (c) the domains identified as needing improvement (without attribution to individual members); (d) a summary of group discussion; (e) the Governance Improvement Action Plan items identified. Individual scores and individual responses shall not appear in meeting minutes. | In Q1 meeting minutes; completed within 14 calendar days of the meeting. |

### 6.4 Governance Self-Assessment Summary Report and Improvement Action Plan

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Governing Body Chair | Prepare or oversee preparation of the Governance Self-Assessment Summary Report (Appendix B) following the Q1 meeting. The report shall document: (a) the assessment period (calendar year assessed); (b) the number of members who completed individual assessments; (c) aggregated domain ratings — strength, satisfactory, or needs improvement; (d) identified governance strengths; (e) identified improvement priorities with supporting rationale. | Draft completed within 21 calendar days of the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.4.2 | Governing Body Chair | Develop the Governance Improvement Action Plan as part of the Summary Report. For each domain rated as needing improvement, the plan shall specify: (a) the specific improvement action required; (b) the responsible party (Governing Body Chair, Administrator, or Compliance Officer); (c) the target completion date; (d) the success metric by which completion will be confirmed. | Included in the Summary Report draft within 21 calendar days of the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.4.3 | Governing Body Chair | Distribute the draft Summary Report and Improvement Action Plan to all Governing Body members for review and comment. Members shall have 7 calendar days to provide written feedback. | Distributed within 21 calendar days of Q1 meeting; feedback period closes 7 days later. |
| 6.4.4 | Governing Body Chair | Finalize the Summary Report incorporating member feedback and submit the final document to the Compliance Officer for filing. | Finalized within 35 calendar days of the Q1 meeting. |
| 6.4.5 | Compliance Officer | File the finalized Summary Report and all individual self-assessment forms in the restricted governance file. Maintain individual forms as privileged records accessible only to Governing Body members and the Compliance Officer. | Filed within 3 calendar days of receipt. |

### 6.5 Improvement Action Plan Monitoring

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Governing Body Chair | Monitor progress on each Improvement Action Plan item throughout the year. At each quarterly Governing Body meeting, include a brief standing item in the agenda for action plan status update. | Quarterly; included in each regular meeting agenda. |
| 6.5.2 | Responsible Party for Each Action Item | Report status of assigned improvement actions at each quarterly meeting where the item remains open. Status shall include: (a) actions completed; (b) actions in progress with percentage completion and current obstacles; (c) actions not yet initiated with explanation and revised target date. | At each quarterly meeting where the item remains open. |
| 6.5.3 | Governing Body Chair | If any improvement action is not completed by its target date without documented justification and a revised timeline, escalate to the full Governing Body for discussion and direction at the next quarterly meeting. | At the quarterly meeting following the missed target date. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Fewer than a majority of Governing Body members complete individual self-assessment forms. | Governing Body Chair notifies Administrator in writing. | Chair issues a follow-up request to non-completing members with a 7-calendar-day deadline. If quorum of completed forms is not achieved, the group discussion and action plan are deferred to a special meeting. | Follow-up within 3 calendar days of Q1 meeting; special meeting if needed within 30 calendar days. |
| Self-assessment identifies a critical regulatory compliance gap (e.g., QAPI oversight failure, conflict of interest management deficiency). | Governing Body Chair immediately escalates to Compliance Officer. | Compliance Officer assesses whether the gap constitutes an active compliance risk requiring corrective action under policy QA-AE-003. If so, a corrective action plan is developed separately from the Improvement Action Plan and tracked through the compliance program. | Compliance Officer assessment within 7 calendar days of Q1 meeting; corrective action plan within 14 calendar days if indicated. |
| Governing Body declines to conduct the self-assessment in a given year. | Compliance Officer documents the failure in the annual compliance status report to the Governing Body. | Compliance Officer presents the documentation gap to the Governing Body at the next meeting with a recommendation to schedule the deferred assessment. | Documented in next compliance status report; presented at next quarterly meeting. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Individual self-assessment forms | Completed Self-Assessment Tool (Appendix A) for each Governing Body member. | Each member (completion); Compliance Officer (receipt and filing) | Restricted governance file — privileged records; individual forms not accessible to general staff. | Filed within 3 calendar days of Q1 meeting; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Aggregated findings compilation | Compiled aggregated domain ratings prepared by Compliance Officer. | Compliance Officer | Restricted governance file. | Completed at or immediately after Q1 meeting; filed within 3 calendar days. |
| Q1 meeting minutes — self-assessment section | Documentation in Q1 meeting minutes of: assessment conducted, domain findings (aggregate only), discussion summary, and action plan items. | Designated Secretary | Governing Body meeting minutes; governance file. | Within 14 calendar days of Q1 meeting per GV-GB-002. |
| Governance Self-Assessment Summary Report | Finalized annual report per Appendix B. | Governing Body Chair (preparation); Compliance Officer (filing) | Restricted governance file. | Finalized within 35 calendar days of Q1 meeting; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Governance Improvement Action Plan | Written action plan for identified improvement areas, integrated into the Summary Report. | Governing Body Chair (preparation); Governing Body (review) | Restricted governance file. | Finalized within 35 calendar days of Q1 meeting; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Quarterly action plan status updates | Status updates on Improvement Action Plan items documented in each quarterly meeting's Old Business section. | Responsible party (status report); Designated Secretary (minutes documentation) | Governing Body meeting minutes. | Documented at each quarterly meeting where open items remain; retained for minimum 7 years as part of minutes. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy by all Governing Body members (Appendix C). | Each member (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Annual self-assessment is conducted at Q1 each year. | Review of Q1 meeting minutes confirming self-assessment was conducted and aggregated findings were discussed. | Self-assessment conducted and documented in Q1 minutes in every calendar year. |
| Majority of Governing Body members complete individual assessments. | Compliance Officer records number of completed forms received against current member roster. | Minimum 75% completion rate; 100% target; any non-completion documented with reason. |
| Governance Self-Assessment Summary Report completed within 35 days of Q1 meeting. | Review of report filing date against Q1 meeting date. | Report finalized and filed within 35 calendar days of Q1 meeting in every calendar year. |
| Governance Improvement Action Plan developed for all domains rated as needing improvement. | Review of Summary Report and Improvement Action Plan for completeness. | Every domain rated as needing improvement has at least one specific, time-bound action item with a named responsible party. |
| Prior year's action plan reviewed at each Q1. | Review of Q1 meeting agenda and minutes for prior year action plan status item. | Prior year status reviewed at 100% of Q1 meetings; completed actions confirmed; open actions tracked. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors assessing governance functioning under SOM Appendix B and OIG reviewers evaluating compliance program effectiveness will look for:
Evidence that the Governing Body is self-reflective and improvement-oriented. A documented self-assessment process with a written action plan demonstrates that the Governing Body takes its oversight responsibilities seriously and proactively identifies its own gaps — a hallmark of governance maturity.
Evidence that identified gaps are acted upon. Surveyors and auditors are skeptical of self-assessment processes that produce findings but no action. The Improvement Action Plan and quarterly monitoring evidence demonstrate follow-through.
Linkage between self-assessment findings and other compliance activities. Surveyors will look for evidence that critical gaps identified in the self-assessment triggered formal corrective action through the compliance program, not merely internal discussion.
Confidentiality and integrity of the process. While surveyors will not review individual member forms, they will assess whether the process appears genuine and structured versus perfunctory. A Summary Report that identifies only strengths with no improvement areas is likely to be viewed with skepticism.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Self-assessment conducted but no written report or action plan produced. | Evidence of process without product; surveyor may view as perfunctory compliance exercise with no governance value. | Section 6.4 requires a finalized Summary Report and action plan within 35 days of Q1; filed in restricted governance file. |
| All domains rated as satisfactory or excellent with no improvement areas identified. | Surveyor skepticism; implies the self-assessment was not conducted honestly or rigorously. | Governance Chair sets the expectation that the self-assessment is a genuine improvement tool; Compliance Officer frames distribution materials accordingly. |
| Individual self-assessment forms not treated as privileged — filed in general governance file accessible to surveyors. | Members less likely to respond honestly if forms are not confidential; privacy risk. | Individual forms maintained in restricted privileged file by Compliance Officer; only aggregated findings filed in general governance file. |
| Improvement action plan items never completed or tracked. | Self-assessment becomes a check-the-box exercise with no governance improvement value; same gaps recur annually. | Quarterly tracking built into standing agenda per Section 6.5; Chair escalates missed deadlines at next quarterly meeting. |
| Self-assessment skipped in a given year without documentation. | Compliance gap; governance effectiveness cannot be demonstrated; prior year improvement gaps persist unaddressed. | If skipped, Compliance Officer documents in annual compliance report and presents gap to Governing Body per Section 6.6. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All Governing Body members shall receive orientation to this policy within fourteen (14) calendar days of appointment. Orientation shall cover: (a) the purpose and value of the self-assessment as a governance improvement tool; (b) how to complete the Self-Assessment Tool (Appendix A) honestly and independently; (c) the confidentiality of individual responses; (d) how aggregated findings are used in the group discussion; (e) the member's responsibility to contribute meaningfully to the group discussion and action planning.
10.2 All Governing Body members shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within fourteen (14) calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.3 The annual self-assessment process shall be conducted as part of the Q1 annual governance refresher per policy GV-GB-001 §10.4, integrating it into the established annual governance education calendar.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy is valid. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision requires: (a) Governing Body approval documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within fourteen (14) calendar days; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

# Policy 1 of 7: GV-OG-001
# Organizational Structure & Reporting
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-OG-001 |
| Policy Title | Organizational Structure & Reporting |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | OG — Organizational Governance |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the formal organizational structure, reporting relationships, and lines of authority for Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. A clearly defined organizational structure is essential to ensure that all personnel understand their reporting relationships, that accountability is maintained at every level, and that the agency operates in compliance with the organizational and administrative requirements of 42 CFR § 484.105. The Governing Body, as the ultimate authority for the agency's operation and management, must ensure that an organizational structure exists that supports effective service delivery, regulatory compliance, and clear communication from the Governing Body through all operational levels to the point of patient care.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Agency Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Compliance Officer
All department heads, supervisors, and managers
All clinical and non-clinical employees, contractors, and per diem staff
All individuals performing services under the direction of the agency
This policy does not apply to patients, families, or external referral sources, except to the extent that organizational structure information is shared with these parties per patient rights requirements (CL-PR-001) or interagency agreement obligations (GV-EA-001).
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a formally documented organizational structure that defines the reporting relationships, lines of authority, and accountability for all agency functions, as required by 42 CFR § 484.105(a) and California Health and Safety Code requirements for home health agencies.
4.2 The organizational structure shall clearly delineate the relationship between the Governing Body, the Administrator, the Clinical Manager (Director of Nursing), the Compliance Officer, and all departments and positions within the agency.
4.3 The Administrator shall maintain a current organizational chart that accurately reflects the agency's structure. The organizational chart shall be approved by the Governing Body and updated within 14 calendar days of any change to leadership positions, departmental structure, or reporting relationships.
4.4 The organizational structure shall ensure that:
The Administrator reports directly to the Governing Body (GV-GB-001, GV-OG-002)
The Clinical Manager / Director of Nursing has direct authority over all clinical operations (GV-OG-002, 42 CFR § 484.105(c))
The Compliance Officer has a direct reporting line to the Governing Body independent of the Administrator for compliance matters (CO-CP-002)
All clinical staff have a defined clinical supervision chain that terminates at the Director of Nursing
All administrative and operational staff have a defined management chain that terminates at the Administrator
4.5 No individual shall be required to report to two supervisors for the same function without a formally documented dual-reporting arrangement approved by the Administrator and documented in the individual's job description (HR-JD domain).
4.6 The organizational structure shall support the agency's defined scope of services (GV-OG-003) and shall be staffed at levels sufficient to deliver all authorized services safely and effectively (HR-WM-001).
4.7 When the agency operates branch offices or satellite locations, the organizational structure must clearly identify the supervisory relationships and reporting lines for those locations back to the central office, per policy OP-FM-002.
4.8 Only the most current approved version of this policy and the associated organizational chart shall be considered valid. Superseded versions must not be used for any operational or compliance purpose. Any revision to this policy requires re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Organizational Structure | The formal system of authority, accountability, and communication relationships within Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., as documented in the agency's organizational chart and supporting position descriptions. |
| Organizational Chart | A visual diagram depicting the hierarchical relationships, reporting lines, and departmental groupings within the agency. Also referred to as an "org chart." |
| Line of Authority | The direct chain of command through which supervisory responsibility and decision-making authority flow from the Governing Body through the Administrator and department heads to individual staff members. |
| Reporting Relationship | The formally documented supervisory connection between an employee and their designated supervisor, defining who the employee reports to and who is accountable for their performance and oversight. |
| Span of Control | The number of direct reports assigned to a single supervisor or manager. Span of control must be reasonable to ensure adequate oversight of all assigned personnel. |
| Dual Reporting | A formally documented arrangement in which an individual reports to two different supervisors for distinct and defined functions (e.g., Compliance Officer reports to Administrator for administrative matters and directly to the Governing Body for compliance matters). |
| Department | A functional grouping of staff and activities organized around a common operational area (e.g., Clinical Operations, Human Resources, Finance, Compliance). |
| Direct Report | An individual who reports directly to a specified supervisor in the organizational hierarchy. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Establishment and Documentation of the Organizational Structure

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Administrator | Develop and maintain a formal organizational chart for Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. that depicts: (a) the Governing Body at the top of the hierarchy; (b) the Administrator reporting to the Governing Body; (c) the Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager reporting to the Administrator with authority over all clinical operations; (d) the Compliance Officer with a direct reporting line to the Governing Body for compliance matters; (e) all departments, units, and positions within the agency; (f) all supervisory and reporting relationships. | Established prior to initial operations; maintained continuously. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator | Ensure the organizational chart clearly distinguishes between: (a) line authority (solid lines indicating direct supervisory relationships); (b) advisory or coordination relationships (dashed lines indicating indirect, consultative, or dual-reporting relationships); (c) contracted or outsourced functions (labeled accordingly). | At initial development and each revision. |
| 6.1.3 | Administrator | Submit the organizational chart to the Governing Body for formal review and approval. The Governing Body shall document its approval in meeting minutes per GV-GB-002. | Annually at the first quarterly meeting; and within 14 calendar days of any material structural change. |
| 6.1.4 | Administrator | Distribute the approved organizational chart to all department heads and make it accessible to all agency personnel. A copy shall be maintained in the agency governance file, the Administrator's office, and the agency's electronic document management system. | Within 7 calendar days of Governing Body approval. |
| 6.1.5 | HR Director | Ensure that each employee's job description (HR-JD domain) accurately reflects the reporting relationship and supervisory chain depicted in the organizational chart. Any discrepancy between a job description and the organizational chart must be resolved within 14 calendar days of identification. | Ongoing; verified at each new hire and annual review. |

### 6.2 Required Organizational Positions and Reporting Lines
The following positions and reporting lines are required to meet 42 CFR § 484.105 and California state requirements. The Governing Body shall ensure these positions are filled at all times (per GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.2 and Section 6.5).

| Position | Reports To | Authority / Scope | Regulatory Basis |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Governing Body | N/A (Ultimate authority) | Full legal authority for the agency's overall operation, management, and fiscal viability. | 42 CFR § 484.105(a) |
| Administrator | Governing Body | Day-to-day management and operation of the agency. Authority to act on behalf of the Governing Body within delegated limits (GV-OG-005). | 42 CFR § 484.105(b); California HCAI requirements |
| Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager | Administrator (administrative); Governing Body (clinical quality reporting) | Authority over all clinical operations, patient care delivery, clinical staff supervision, and OASIS compliance. | 42 CFR § 484.105(c) |
| Compliance Officer | Administrator (administrative); Governing Body (compliance reporting) | Authority and independence to operate the corporate compliance program including investigation, reporting, and corrective action. | OIG Compliance Program Guidance; CO-CP-002 |
| Clinical Supervisors (RN) | Director of Nursing | Supervision of assigned clinical staff, clinical oversight of patient care within assigned caseload. | 42 CFR § 484.115; California BRN |
| Home Health Aides | Supervising RN / Director of Nursing | Delivery of personal care services under the direction and supervision of a registered nurse. | 42 CFR § 484.80 |
| Therapy Staff (PT, OT, SLP) | Director of Nursing (clinical); Administrator (administrative) | Delivery of discipline-specific therapy services under clinical oversight. | 42 CFR § 484.115 |
| Medical Social Worker | Director of Nursing | Delivery of medical social work services under clinical oversight. | 42 CFR § 484.115 |
| Administrative / Operational Staff | Administrator or designated department head | Performance of assigned administrative, operational, or support functions. | Agency policy; California employment law |

### 6.3 Annual Review and Update of the Organizational Structure

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator | Conduct a comprehensive review of the organizational structure at least annually. The review shall assess: (a) whether the current structure supports the agency's defined scope of services (GV-OG-003); (b) whether staffing levels are adequate for each position and department (HR-WM-001); (c) whether all required positions are filled; (d) whether reporting lines are clear, functional, and free of gaps; (e) whether the structure supports regulatory compliance. | Annually; completed prior to the first quarterly Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.3.2 | Administrator | Present the results of the annual organizational structure review to the Governing Body. The presentation must include: (a) the current organizational chart; (b) any recommended changes; (c) identified gaps or risks; (d) staffing adequacy assessment. | At the first quarterly Governing Body meeting of each calendar year. |
| 6.3.3 | Governing Body | Review, discuss, and approve or direct modification of the organizational structure. Approval or directed modifications must be documented in meeting minutes per GV-GB-002. | At the first quarterly meeting; follow-up within 30 calendar days if modifications are directed. |

### 6.4 Changes to the Organizational Structure

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator | When a change to the organizational structure is required outside the annual review cycle (e.g., new department, position elimination, reorganization, branch office opening), the Administrator shall prepare a written proposal that includes: (a) description of the change; (b) rationale; (c) impact on reporting lines; (d) impact on staffing and service delivery; (e) updated organizational chart draft. | As needed; proposal prepared within 14 calendar days of identifying the need. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator | Submit material structural changes to the Governing Body for approval at the next regular meeting, or at a special meeting if the change is urgent. Material changes include: creation or elimination of a department, change to a key leadership reporting line, addition or closure of a branch office, or any change affecting more than 10% of the workforce. | At next regular Governing Body meeting; or special meeting if urgent. |
| 6.4.3 | Administrator | For non-material changes (e.g., addition of a single position within an existing department, change of a non-leadership reporting relationship), the Administrator may approve the change and report it to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. | Implemented immediately; reported at next Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.4.4 | Administrator | Update the organizational chart within 14 calendar days of any approved change. Distribute the updated chart to all department heads and make it accessible to all agency personnel. | Within 14 calendar days of change. |
| 6.4.5 | HR Director | Update affected job descriptions, personnel records, and onboarding materials to reflect the structural change. Notify affected employees in writing of any change to their reporting relationship. | Within 14 calendar days of approved change. |

### 6.5 Communication of the Organizational Structure

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator | Ensure the current organizational chart is included in: (a) the agency's new employee orientation program (HR-TA-005); (b) the agency governance file maintained for CMS survey review; (c) the agency's electronic document management system accessible to all staff. | Ongoing. |
| 6.5.2 | HR Director | Review the organizational chart with each new employee during orientation, clearly identifying: (a) the employee's position; (b) their direct supervisor; (c) the chain of command above them; (d) how to escalate concerns if the direct supervisor is unavailable. | At orientation for each new employee. |
| 6.5.3 | Administrator | When the organizational structure changes, communicate the change in writing to all affected personnel. Communication must include: (a) description of the change; (b) effective date; (c) updated reporting relationships; (d) point of contact for questions. | Within 7 calendar days of change. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Key leadership position (Administrator, Director of Nursing, Compliance Officer) is vacant. | Governing Body per GV-GB-001, Section 6.5. | Governing Body appoints interim designee within 14 calendar days; permanent appointment within 90 calendar days. | Interim: 14 days. Permanent: 90 days. |
| Organizational chart has not been updated within 14 calendar days of a known change. | Compliance Officer flags the deficiency to the Administrator. | Administrator updates the chart within 7 calendar days of notification. If not corrected, Compliance Officer reports to the Governing Body. | 7 calendar days from notification. |
| An employee's job description conflicts with the organizational chart regarding reporting relationship. | HR Director escalates to Administrator. | Administrator resolves the discrepancy and updates whichever document is incorrect within 14 calendar days. | Within 14 calendar days. |
| CMS surveyor identifies a gap or ambiguity in the organizational structure during survey. | Administrator convenes immediate corrective response. | Administrator prepares corrective documentation within the CMS-required timeframe (typically 10 calendar days) and presents to the Governing Body. | Per CMS Plan of Correction deadline. |
| Organizational structure does not support the agency's scope of services. | Administrator reports to Governing Body. | Governing Body directs restructuring or scope of services modification within 30 calendar days. | Within 30 calendar days. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Current organizational chart | Approved organizational chart depicting all positions, departments, and reporting lines. | Administrator (preparation); Governing Body (approval) | Agency governance file; Administrator's office; electronic document management system. | Maintained continuously; updated within 14 calendar days of any change. Retained for minimum 7 years per CO-HP-007. |
| Governing Body approval of organizational structure | Meeting minutes documenting review and approval of the organizational chart per GV-GB-002. | Designated Secretary | Agency governance file. | Annually at first quarterly meeting; and upon each material change. |
| Job descriptions aligned to structure | Written job descriptions for each position that reflect the supervisory chain depicted in the organizational chart. | HR Director | Personnel files; HR records. | Current at all times; verified at hire and annual review. |
| Structural change documentation | Written proposal and approval documentation for material changes to the organizational structure. | Administrator | Agency governance file. | As changes occur; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Employee notification of structural change | Written notification to affected employees documenting changes to their reporting relationships. | Administrator / HR Director | Employee personnel file; agency records. | Within 7 calendar days of change. |
| Annual organizational structure review | Documentation of the Administrator's annual review of the organizational structure including findings and recommendations. | Administrator | Agency governance file. | Annually; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy by all personnel within scope (Appendix B). | Each individual (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of policy effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A current, Governing Body-approved organizational chart exists. | Review of organizational chart for approval date, accuracy, and completeness. | Current chart on file; approved by Governing Body within the past 12 months or since last material change. |
| Organizational chart accurately reflects actual reporting relationships. | Comparison of organizational chart to job descriptions, personnel records, and staff interviews. | 100% alignment between chart, job descriptions, and actual practice. |
| All required positions are filled. | Review of organizational chart and personnel records for vacancies. | No required position vacant for more than 30 calendar days without a documented interim designee. |
| Annual review of the organizational structure is documented. | Review of Governing Body minutes and Administrator's annual review documentation. | Annual review completed and presented to the Governing Body. |
| Structural changes are documented and communicated. | Review of change documentation, updated charts, and employee notification records. | All changes documented within 14 calendar days; employees notified within 7 calendar days. |
| Policy acknowledgments are current. | Review of signed Appendix B forms. | 100% acknowledgment within 14 calendar days of effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under the State Operations Manual (SOM) Appendix B will specifically verify:
Evidence that a defined organizational structure exists. Surveyors will request the current organizational chart and review it for completeness, clarity, and evidence of Governing Body approval.
Evidence that the Administrator is accountable to the Governing Body. Surveyors will look for a clear line of authority from the Governing Body to the Administrator, documented in the organizational chart and Governing Body minutes.
Evidence that the Clinical Manager has authority over clinical operations. Surveyors will verify that the organizational chart positions the Director of Nursing with direct authority over all clinical services and clinical staff.
Evidence that reporting relationships are understood by staff. Surveyors may interview staff at various levels to confirm they know their supervisor, chain of command, and escalation path.
Evidence that the organizational structure supports the scope of services. Surveyors will assess whether positions and departments exist to support all services the agency claims to provide.
Evidence that the organizational chart is current. Surveyors will compare the chart to actual staff roster and check for recent updates reflecting any changes.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No organizational chart exists or the chart is outdated. | Surveyor will cite as a deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.105 (organization and administration). | Maintain a current chart; update within 14 calendar days of any change; review annually with Governing Body. |
| Organizational chart does not show Governing Body at the top of the hierarchy. | Surveyor may cite failure to demonstrate Governing Body authority over the agency. | Ensure the chart clearly positions the Governing Body as the ultimate authority with the Administrator reporting directly to it. |
| Reporting relationships on the chart do not match actual practice. | Surveyor interviews may reveal discrepancies, resulting in a finding of inadequate organizational structure. | Verify chart accuracy against job descriptions and actual practice at least annually; update both simultaneously. |
| Compliance Officer does not have a direct reporting line to the Governing Body. | OIG compliance program deficiency; potential CMS survey finding regarding inadequate compliance program structure. | Document dual-reporting relationship for Compliance Officer: administrative to Administrator, compliance to Governing Body. |
| Key leadership positions are vacant with no documented interim designee. | Surveyor will cite failure to ensure adequate management and clinical oversight. | Follow GV-GB-001 Section 6.5 escalation: interim within 14 days, permanent within 90 days. |
| Staff cannot articulate their reporting relationships or chain of command. | Surveyor interviews reveal lack of organizational clarity, indicating inadequate communication. | Include organizational chart review in new employee orientation and annual training. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All personnel within the scope of this policy (Section 3) shall receive orientation to the agency's organizational structure during initial onboarding per HR-TA-005. Orientation shall include: (a) review of the current organizational chart; (b) identification of the employee's position, direct supervisor, and chain of command; (c) explanation of how to escalate concerns through the chain of command; (d) identification of the Compliance Officer and the independent compliance reporting pathway.
10.2 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new hire/appointment.
10.3 The Administrator shall maintain a tracking log of all policy acknowledgments and report any non-compliance to the Governing Body Chair within 7 calendar days of the acknowledgment deadline. Failure to acknowledge within the required timeframe shall result in written notification with a mandatory completion deadline of 7 additional calendar days.
10.4 When the organizational structure changes, the Administrator shall communicate the change to all affected personnel in writing per Section 6.5.3. Affected employees shall confirm receipt and understanding in writing within 7 calendar days of notification.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment.

# Policy 2 of 7: GV-OG-002
# Administrator Qualifications & Responsibilities
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-OG-002 |
| Policy Title | Administrator Qualifications & Responsibilities |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | OG — Organizational Governance |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Governing Body |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 6.0 |

| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |

## 2. Purpose
This policy defines the minimum qualifications, duties, authority, and accountability requirements for the Administrator of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The Administrator is the individual appointed by the Governing Body who is responsible for the day-to-day management and operation of the home health agency. This policy ensures compliance with 42 CFR § 484.105(b), which requires a home health agency to designate a qualified administrator who is authorized to act on behalf of the governing body and who is responsible for the agency's overall management, including financial operations, personnel administration, and regulatory compliance. This policy also addresses California-specific administrator qualification requirements as established by the California Department of Public Health and the Health Care Access and Information (HCAI) division.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. (as the appointing authority)
The current Administrator
Any individual being considered for appointment as Administrator
The Administrator Designee (GV-OG-005, HR-JD-002)
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (as a direct report)
The Compliance Officer
The HR Director (for qualification verification and personnel file maintenance)
This policy does not apply to other agency staff except to the extent that the Administrator's responsibilities establish requirements, standards, or directives that govern their work.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall at all times have a designated Administrator who has been formally appointed by the Governing Body and who meets all qualification requirements established in this policy, 42 CFR § 484.105(b), and applicable California state law.
4.2 The Administrator shall be authorized by the Governing Body to act on its behalf in the day-to-day management and operation of the agency, subject to the limitations defined in the Delegation of Authority policy (GV-OG-005) and the Governing Body's retained responsibilities (GV-GB-001).
4.3 The Administrator shall be accountable to the Governing Body for all aspects of agency operations including, but not limited to: organizational management, personnel administration, financial management, regulatory compliance, service delivery oversight, and maintenance of the agency's licensure and certification.
4.4 The Administrator shall report directly to the Governing Body and shall provide regular reports on agency operations, financial performance, compliance status, and quality metrics at each quarterly Governing Body meeting, as required by GV-GB-001.
4.5 If the Administrator position becomes vacant, the Governing Body shall appoint an interim Administrator who meets the minimum qualifications within 14 calendar days of the vacancy and shall appoint a permanent Administrator within 90 calendar days, per GV-GB-001, Section 6.5.
4.6 The Governing Body shall conduct or commission an annual performance evaluation of the Administrator, as required by GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.2.4.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid. Superseded versions must not be used for any operational or compliance purpose.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Administrator | The individual appointed by the Governing Body who is responsible for managing the day-to-day operations of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. and who meets all qualifications specified in this policy, 42 CFR § 484.105(b), and applicable California state law. |
| Administrator Designee | The individual formally authorized to act on behalf of the Administrator during planned or unplanned absences, per policy GV-OG-005 and HR-JD-002. The designee must meet minimum qualifications defined in this policy. |
| Governing Body | The legally constituted authority that has ultimate responsibility for the management and operation of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. and that appoints the Administrator. |
| Day-to-Day Management | The ongoing operational oversight of all agency functions including personnel, financial, clinical, compliance, and administrative operations, exercised within the authority delegated by the Governing Body. |
| Personnel Administration | The management of all human resources functions including hiring, training, supervision, evaluation, discipline, and separation of agency personnel, in accordance with HR domain policies. |
| Fiscal Management | The oversight of the agency's financial operations including budgeting, revenue cycle management, expenditure control, and financial reporting. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Minimum Qualifications for the Administrator

| Requirement Category | Minimum Qualification | Regulatory Basis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Education | A bachelor's degree from an accredited institution, or a combination of education and experience that demonstrates competency in healthcare administration or management. California does not mandate a specific degree for home health agency administrators, but the Governing Body shall require documented evidence of healthcare management competency. | 42 CFR § 484.105(b); California HCAI |
| Experience | A minimum of two (2) years of experience in healthcare administration, management, or supervisory capacity, of which at least one (1) year shall be in a home health or home care setting. | Agency standard per Governing Body requirement; aligns with CMS expectation of qualified administrator. |
| Licensure / Certification | No specific license is required by CMS or California for the home health agency administrator position. However, if the Administrator also serves as the Clinical Manager, the individual must hold a current, active California registered nurse license. | 42 CFR § 484.105(b); California BRN if dual role. |
| Knowledge Competency | Demonstrated knowledge of: (a) Medicare Conditions of Participation (42 CFR Part 484); (b) California home health agency licensure requirements; (c) HIPAA privacy and security requirements; (d) home health agency financial management; (e) quality improvement principles; (f) human resources management. | 42 CFR § 484.105(b); CMS SOM Appendix B interpretive guidance. |
| Exclusion Screening | Not listed on the OIG List of Excluded Individuals/Entities (LEIE) or the System for Award Management (SAM) exclusion database at the time of appointment and monthly thereafter, per HR-TA-003. | OIG requirements; 42 USC § 1320a-7. |
| Criminal Background | Satisfactory criminal background check per HR-TA-002 and applicable California law. | California Health and Safety Code; HR-TA-002. |

### 6.2 Appointment of the Administrator

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Governing Body | Formally appoint the Administrator through a documented action recorded in Governing Body meeting minutes per GV-GB-002. The appointment documentation must include: (a) the individual's name; (b) effective date of appointment; (c) qualifications verified; (d) scope of delegated authority (referencing GV-OG-005); (e) reporting relationship (reports directly to Governing Body). | Prior to initial agency operation; within 30 calendar days of any vacancy per GV-GB-001. |
| 6.2.2 | HR Director | Verify all minimum qualifications prior to the Governing Body's formal appointment. Verification shall include: (a) education credentials (original or certified copies); (b) employment history verification; (c) professional references; (d) criminal background check per HR-TA-002; (e) OIG/SAM exclusion screening per HR-TA-003; (f) any required licensure verification. Verification results shall be documented in the Administrator's personnel file. | Completed prior to formal appointment; results available for Governing Body review. |
| 6.2.3 | Governing Body Chair | Issue a formal written appointment letter to the Administrator that includes: (a) effective date; (b) reporting relationship (Governing Body); (c) reference to this policy (GV-OG-002) and the Delegation of Authority policy (GV-OG-005); (d) expectation for annual performance evaluation; (e) statement that the appointment is subject to ongoing qualification requirements. | Within 7 calendar days of formal Governing Body action. |
| 6.2.4 | Administrator | Acknowledge receipt of the appointment letter and this policy by signing the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C). | Within 14 calendar days of appointment. |

### 6.3 Core Responsibilities of the Administrator
The Administrator of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall fulfill the following responsibilities. While the Administrator may delegate specific tasks in accordance with GV-OG-005, the Administrator retains accountability to the Governing Body for all functions.
#### 6.3.1 — Agency Operations Management

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1.1 | Administrator | Oversee the day-to-day operations of the agency including all clinical, administrative, financial, and compliance functions. Ensure all operations comply with federal, state, and local laws, the agency's policies, and the directives of the Governing Body. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.1.2 | Administrator | Maintain the agency's organizational structure (GV-OG-001) and ensure all positions are filled with qualified personnel. Report any vacancy in a key leadership position (Director of Nursing, Compliance Officer) to the Governing Body within 48 hours. | Continuous; vacancy reported within 48 hours. |
| 6.3.1.3 | Administrator | Ensure the agency maintains current and valid: (a) California home health agency license; (b) Medicare certification; (c) Medicaid enrollment (if applicable); (d) accreditation (if applicable), per policy GV-EA-004. Report any lapse or risk of lapse to the Governing Body immediately. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.1.4 | Administrator | Implement and enforce all policies approved by the Governing Body and ensure operational compliance across all departments. | Continuous. |

#### 6.3.2 — Personnel Administration

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.2.1 | Administrator | Ensure that the agency recruits, hires, and retains a sufficient number of qualified personnel to deliver all services within the defined scope of services (GV-OG-003), in compliance with HR domain policies. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.2.2 | Administrator | Ensure that all employees complete required orientation (HR-TA-005), mandatory training (HR-TD-001), and competency evaluations (HR-TD-003) within required timeframes. | Continuous; monitored quarterly. |
| 6.3.2.3 | Administrator | Ensure compliance with all applicable employment laws including California wage and hour requirements, anti-discrimination laws, and workplace safety regulations. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.2.4 | Administrator | Oversee the agency's human resources functions including performance evaluation (HR-ER-001), disciplinary action (HR-ER-002), and separation processes (HR-ER-006). | Continuous. |

#### 6.3.3 — Financial Management

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.3.1 | Administrator | Prepare the annual operating budget for Governing Body review and approval per FN-FP-005. | Annually; submitted to Governing Body at least 30 calendar days before fiscal year start. |
| 6.3.3.2 | Administrator | Oversee the agency's revenue cycle including billing, claims submission, denial management, and collections per FN domain policies. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.3.3 | Administrator | Present a quarterly financial performance report to the Governing Body including: (a) revenue vs. budget variance; (b) accounts receivable aging; (c) claims denial rate and trending; (d) cash flow position, per GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.5.2. | Quarterly at each Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.3.3.4 | Administrator | Ensure the agency's financial records are maintained in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles and applicable regulatory requirements. | Continuous. |

#### 6.3.4 — Regulatory Compliance and Quality Oversight

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.4.1 | Administrator | Ensure the agency maintains continuous compliance with all applicable federal, state, and local regulations, CMS Conditions of Participation (42 CFR Part 484), and California home health agency licensure requirements. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.4.2 | Administrator | Support the Compliance Officer in operating the agency's corporate compliance program (CO-CP-001) and ensure the Compliance Officer has the authority, resources, and independence to perform compliance functions. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.4.3 | Administrator | Support the Director of Nursing and QAPI Coordinator in operating the agency's QAPI program (QA-PG-001) and ensure quality improvement activities receive adequate resources and leadership support. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.4.4 | Administrator | Ensure the agency maintains continuous survey readiness (CO-RA-003) and coordinate the agency's response to any CMS survey, state survey, or accreditation review. | Continuous. |

#### 6.3.5 — Governing Body Reporting and Communication

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.5.1 | Administrator | Attend all regular and special Governing Body meetings and present the Administrator's report including: (a) operational status; (b) key personnel updates; (c) regulatory compliance status; (d) significant incidents; (e) financial performance summary. | At each Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.3.5.2 | Administrator | Report critical events to the Governing Body Chair immediately upon occurrence, including but not limited to: (a) CMS or state survey findings; (b) serious patient safety events; (c) regulatory enforcement actions; (d) key leadership vacancies; (e) media inquiries involving the agency; (f) potential fraud or compliance violations. | Immediately upon occurrence. |
| 6.3.5.3 | Administrator | Implement all directives issued by the Governing Body within the timeframes specified. Report completion or request an extension in writing to the Governing Body Chair. | Per Governing Body-specified deadline. |

### 6.4 Administrator Performance Evaluation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Governing Body | Conduct or commission an annual performance evaluation of the Administrator per GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.2.4. The evaluation shall assess: (a) operational management effectiveness; (b) regulatory compliance record; (c) financial performance; (d) quality outcomes; (e) personnel management; (f) implementation of Governing Body directives; (g) leadership and communication. | Annually; completed within 60 calendar days of the end of each fiscal year. |
| 6.4.2 | Governing Body Chair | Document the evaluation results and any corrective directives in executive session minutes. Provide written feedback to the Administrator within 14 calendar days of the evaluation meeting. | Within 14 calendar days of evaluation. |
| 6.4.3 | Administrator | Acknowledge receipt of the performance evaluation by signing the evaluation document. Develop a written response or improvement plan if directed by the Governing Body. | Within 14 calendar days of receipt. |

### 6.5 Administrator Absence and Succession

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator | Designate a qualified Administrator Designee (HR-JD-002) to act on the Administrator's behalf during planned absences. The designation must be documented in writing and the designee must meet minimum qualifications defined in Section 6.1. | Prior to any planned absence. |
| 6.5.2 | Administrator | Notify the Governing Body Chair in writing of any planned absence exceeding 5 consecutive business days, identifying the designee and the dates of absence. | At least 7 calendar days before the planned absence begins. |
| 6.5.3 | Governing Body Chair | In the event of an unplanned Administrator absence (illness, incapacitation, termination, resignation), activate the succession plan (GV-GB-004) and appoint an interim Administrator within 14 calendar days per GV-GB-001, Section 6.5. | Interim: within 14 calendar days. Permanent: within 90 calendar days. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Administrator position becomes vacant. | Governing Body Chair notifies all Governing Body members. | Governing Body appoints interim designee within 14 calendar days; permanent appointment within 90 calendar days per GV-GB-001, Section 6.5. | Interim: 14 days. Permanent: 90 days. |
| Administrator's qualification status changes (e.g., exclusion listing, criminal charge). | Compliance Officer notifies Governing Body Chair immediately. | Governing Body convenes within 48 hours to assess status and determine action, which may include suspension or removal. | Within 48 hours. |
| Administrator fails to submit required reports to the Governing Body. | Governing Body Chair issues written notice to Administrator. | Administrator must cure within 7 calendar days. Failure to cure is documented in meeting minutes and may trigger performance review or disciplinary action. | 7 calendar days to cure. |
| Governing Body determines Administrator performance is unsatisfactory. | Governing Body documents in executive session minutes. | Governing Body may issue a performance improvement plan, place the Administrator on probation, or terminate the appointment, per the agency's employment policies. | Per Governing Body determination. |
| CMS surveyor identifies a deficiency related to administrator qualifications or oversight. | Administrator reports to Governing Body Chair immediately. | Governing Body directs development of a Plan of Correction within CMS-required timeframes. | Per CMS Plan of Correction deadline. |

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Administrator appointment | Written appointment documented in Governing Body minutes (GV-GB-002); formal appointment letter. | Governing Body Chair | Governing Body minutes; Administrator personnel file. | At time of appointment. |
| Qualification verification | Documented verification of education, experience, background check, exclusion screening, and any licensure. | HR Director | Administrator personnel file. | Completed prior to appointment; maintained continuously. |
| Annual performance evaluation | Written evaluation documented in executive session minutes; written feedback to Administrator. | Governing Body | Governing Body executive session minutes; Administrator personnel file. | Annually; within 60 calendar days of fiscal year end. |
| Ongoing exclusion screening | Monthly OIG/SAM screening results per HR-TA-003. | Compliance Officer | Compliance file; Administrator personnel file. | Monthly. |
| Absence notification | Written notification to Governing Body Chair for absences exceeding 5 business days; designee identification. | Administrator | Agency governance file. | At least 7 calendar days before planned absence. |
| Financial reports to Governing Body | Quarterly financial performance reports. | Administrator | Governing Body meeting files; financial records. | Quarterly. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy (Appendix C). | Administrator | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of appointment, policy effective date, or revision. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A qualified Administrator is appointed and documented. | Review of Governing Body minutes, appointment letter, and qualification verification records. | Current appointment documented; all qualifications verified and on file. |
| Administrator meets minimum qualification requirements. | Review of education credentials, experience documentation, background check, and exclusion screening results. | All qualifications verified prior to appointment; no exclusion listing; background check satisfactory. |
| Administrator fulfills reporting obligations to Governing Body. | Review of Governing Body meeting minutes for Administrator reports at each quarterly meeting. | Administrator report documented at each quarterly meeting. |
| Annual performance evaluation is conducted. | Review of executive session minutes and evaluation documentation. | Evaluation completed within 60 calendar days of fiscal year end. |
| Administrator absence is managed per policy. | Review of absence notifications, designee documentation. | All absences exceeding 5 days have documented designee and Governing Body notification. |
| Exclusion screening is current. | Review of monthly OIG/SAM screening logs. | Monthly screening documented with no exclusion finding. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under the State Operations Manual (SOM) Appendix B will specifically verify:
Evidence that an Administrator has been appointed by the Governing Body. Surveyors will review Governing Body minutes for documented appointment.
Evidence that the Administrator meets qualification requirements. Surveyors will review the Administrator's personnel file for education, experience, and background verification.
Evidence that the Administrator is responsible for day-to-day management. Surveyors will look for documented authority, reporting relationships, and evidence of active management oversight.
Evidence that the Administrator reports to the Governing Body. Surveyors will verify reporting relationship through the organizational chart, Governing Body minutes, and interview.
Evidence that the Administrator ensures regulatory compliance. Surveyors will assess the overall compliance posture of the agency as an indicator of administrator effectiveness.
Evidence that a succession plan exists for the Administrator position. Surveyors may inquire about plans for management continuity in the event of an Administrator vacancy.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No documented Governing Body appointment of the Administrator. | Condition-level deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.105(b). | Document appointment in Governing Body minutes; issue formal appointment letter. |
| Administrator's qualifications are not verified or documented. | Surveyor may find insufficient evidence of a "qualified" administrator. | Verify all qualifications prior to appointment; maintain verification records in personnel file. |
| Administrator vacancy exceeds 30 calendar days without interim designee. | Surveyor will cite failure to ensure adequate management. | Maintain succession plan (GV-GB-004); appoint interim within 14 days per GV-GB-001. |
| No evidence of Administrator reporting to the Governing Body. | Surveyor may find that the Governing Body is not exercising required oversight. | Ensure Administrator presents report at each quarterly meeting; document in minutes. |
| Administrator is listed on OIG/SAM exclusion database. | Violation of federal law; potential termination of Medicare certification. | Conduct monthly screening per HR-TA-003; take immediate action upon discovery of listing. |
| No documented annual performance evaluation. | Demonstrates lack of Governing Body oversight of the Administrator. | Schedule evaluation per Section 6.4; document in executive session minutes. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 The Administrator shall receive comprehensive orientation to this policy and all Governing Body-level governance policies (GV-GB-001 through GV-GB-005, GV-OG-001 through GV-OG-005) within 14 calendar days of appointment. Orientation shall be conducted by the Governing Body Chair or designated member and shall cover: (a) qualifications and ongoing eligibility requirements; (b) scope of authority and delegation limits; (c) reporting obligations to the Governing Body; (d) financial, compliance, and quality oversight responsibilities; (e) performance evaluation process; (f) absence and succession requirements.
10.2 The Administrator and all personnel within scope (Section 3) shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.3 The Governing Body Chair shall verify that the Administrator has acknowledged this policy. Failure to acknowledge within the required timeframe shall be documented and addressed at the next Governing Body meeting.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Governing Body Chair with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.
