# Policy 3 of 7: GV-OG-003
# Scope of Services Definition
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-OG-003 |
| Policy Title | Scope of Services Definition |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | OG — Organizational Governance |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy formally defines the range of home health services that Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. is authorized, licensed, certified, and staffed to provide. A clearly defined scope of services is essential to ensure the agency does not accept patients or provide services beyond its capabilities, licensure, or certification. This policy ensures compliance with 42 CFR § 484.105(a), which requires the governing body to assume responsibility for ensuring the agency provides services in accordance with its established scope, and with California HCAI licensure requirements that mandate agencies operate only within the services for which they are licensed. The Governing Body must approve the scope of services annually to ensure ongoing alignment between the agency's authorized services, available competencies, and patient population needs.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Agency Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All department heads and clinical supervisors
All intake and admissions staff
All clinical staff involved in service delivery
All referral sources and contracted service providers
This policy does not apply to services provided by other agencies or facilities to which Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. may refer patients. It applies only to services directly provided or arranged by this agency.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a formally documented scope of services that defines every service the agency is authorized, licensed, certified, staffed, and competent to provide, as required by 42 CFR § 484.105(a) and California home health agency licensure requirements.
4.2 The scope of services shall be approved by the Governing Body annually and shall be reviewed any time there is a change in the agency's licensure, certification, staffing, or service capabilities.
4.3 The agency shall not accept patients requiring services that fall outside its defined scope of services. All intake and admissions personnel must verify that each referred patient's service needs can be met within the scope defined by this policy before accepting the patient for admission, per OP-IM-002.
4.4 The agency shall not provide any service for which it does not hold the required licensure, certification, or qualified staffing. Any expansion or reduction of the scope of services requires prior Governing Body approval and, where applicable, regulatory notification or approval.
4.5 The scope of services shall be communicated to all referral sources, clearly published in the agency's marketing and informational materials, and included in the agency's orientation program for new staff.
4.6 The defined scope of services must be supported by the agency's organizational structure (GV-OG-001) and staffing plan (HR-WM-001) at all times.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy and the scope of services document (Appendix A) shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Scope of Services | The formally documented list of all healthcare services that Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. is authorized, licensed, certified, staffed, and competent to provide to patients. |
| Authorized Services | Services that the agency has the legal authority to provide based on its licensure, certification, and applicable federal and state regulations. |
| Core Services | Services that the agency provides directly using its own employed or contracted clinical staff. |
| Arranged Services | Services that the agency does not provide directly but arranges through contractual agreements with qualified third-party providers, for which the agency retains oversight responsibility. |
| Service Expansion | The addition of a new service discipline, specialty program, or service type to the agency's scope of services. |
| Service Reduction | The elimination or suspension of a service discipline, specialty program, or service type from the agency's scope of services. |
| Competency-Based Service Limitation | A restriction on providing a specific service that the agency is licensed to offer but for which it does not currently maintain sufficient trained or competent staff. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Defined Scope of Services
Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. is authorized and provides the following services within its defined scope. This list constitutes the agency's official scope of services and is maintained in detail in Appendix A.
#### 6.1.1 — Core Services (Directly Provided)

| Service Category | Service | Regulatory / Licensure Basis | Governing Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Skilled Nursing | Comprehensive nursing assessment and skilled nursing care | 42 CFR § 484.75; California HCAI license | CL-SD-001 |
| Physical Therapy | Physical therapy evaluation and treatment | 42 CFR § 484.115; California PT Board | CL-SD-002 |
| Occupational Therapy | Occupational therapy evaluation and treatment | 42 CFR § 484.115; California OT Board | CL-SD-003 |
| Speech-Language Pathology | Speech-language pathology evaluation and treatment | 42 CFR § 484.115; California SLP Board | CL-SD-004 |
| Medical Social Work | Medical social work assessment and services | 42 CFR § 484.115; California BBS | CL-SD-005 |
| Home Health Aide | Personal care and supportive services under RN supervision | 42 CFR § 484.80; California HCAI | CL-SD-006 |
| Medication Management | Medication administration, reconciliation, and management | 42 CFR § 484.60; NPA | CL-SD-012, CL-SD-013 |
| Wound Care | Wound assessment, treatment, and management | 42 CFR § 484.60; NPA | CL-SD-011 |
| Patient Education | Individualized patient and caregiver education | 42 CFR § 484.60 | CL-SD-017 |

#### 6.1.2 — Specialty Services (Directly Provided Based on Staffing Competency)

| Service Category | Service | Staffing Requirement | Governing Policy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV Therapy / Infusion | Intravenous therapy and infusion services | RN with demonstrated IV therapy competency | CL-SD-010 |
| Diabetic Management | Comprehensive diabetic care and monitoring | RN with diabetic care competency | CL-SD-018 |
| Cardiac Care | Cardiac assessment, monitoring, and management | RN with cardiac care competency | CL-SD-019 |
| Respiratory Care | Respiratory assessment and oxygen therapy management | RN with respiratory care competency | CL-SD-020 |
| Palliative Care | Palliative care support and hospice referral coordination | RN with palliative care training | CL-SD-023 |
| Telehealth | Telehealth and remote patient monitoring | Clinicians trained in telehealth platform | CL-SD-009 |
| Behavioral Health Screening | Depression and behavioral health screening and referral | Clinicians trained in PHQ screening | CL-SD-022 |

#### 6.1.3 — Services Not Within Scope
Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. does not provide the following services. Patients requiring these services shall be referred to an appropriate provider:

| Service | Reason Not in Scope |
| --- | --- |
| Hospice services | Separate licensure and certification required; agency is not hospice-certified. |
| Private duty nursing (non-Medicare) | Not covered under Medicare home health benefit; separate licensure may apply. |
| Durable medical equipment (DME) provision | DME supplier certification required; agency refers to qualified DME suppliers. |
| Pharmacy / prescription dispensing | Pharmacy licensure required; not within home health scope. |
| Inpatient or residential services | Home health agency is community-based only. |
| Pediatric services (under age 18) | Not currently staffed with pediatric-competent clinicians; may be added per Section 6.3 if staffing capability is established. |

### 6.2 Annual Review and Approval of Scope of Services

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Administrator | Conduct an annual review of the scope of services in consultation with the Director of Nursing. The review shall assess: (a) current licensure and certification status; (b) current staffing and competency capabilities; (c) community need and referral patterns; (d) quality and outcome data for each service line; (e) financial viability of each service line; (f) regulatory changes affecting the scope of services. | Annually; completed at least 30 calendar days before the first quarterly Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.2.2 | Administrator | Prepare a written scope of services review report and present it to the Governing Body. The report must include: (a) the current scope of services document (Appendix A); (b) any recommended additions or reductions; (c) supporting rationale for each recommended change; (d) staffing and resource implications; (e) regulatory implications. | Presented at the first quarterly Governing Body meeting or within 30 calendar days of fiscal year start. |
| 6.2.3 | Governing Body | Review, discuss, and formally approve the scope of services for the upcoming year. Approval must be documented in meeting minutes per GV-GB-002. Any modifications directed by the Governing Body must be implemented by the Administrator within 30 calendar days. | At the first quarterly meeting. |
| 6.2.4 | Administrator | Update Appendix A and distribute the approved scope of services to all department heads, intake staff, and referral sources within 14 calendar days of Governing Body approval. | Within 14 calendar days of approval. |

### 6.3 Scope of Services Expansion

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator | When a service expansion is proposed (new discipline, specialty program, or service type), the Administrator shall prepare a service expansion proposal that includes: (a) description of the proposed service; (b) clinical and community justification; (c) regulatory requirements (licensure, certification, notification); (d) staffing requirements and recruitment plan; (e) competency requirements and training plan; (f) financial analysis (revenue, cost, break-even); (g) policy development requirements; (h) implementation timeline. | As needed. |
| 6.3.2 | Administrator | Submit the service expansion proposal to the Governing Body for approval at a regular or special meeting. | At next regular meeting; special meeting if urgent. |
| 6.3.3 | Governing Body | Review and approve or deny the service expansion. Approval must be documented in meeting minutes. Approval conditions may include: staffing thresholds, competency requirements, pilot period, or financial milestones. | At Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.3.4 | Administrator | Upon approval, implement the service expansion including: (a) obtaining any required regulatory approvals or notifications; (b) hiring and training qualified staff; (c) developing applicable policies; (d) updating Appendix A; (e) notifying referral sources; (f) updating marketing materials; (g) updating intake screening criteria (OP-IM-002). | Per approved implementation timeline; regulatory requirements must be met before service delivery begins. |

### 6.4 Scope of Services Reduction

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator | When a service reduction is proposed or necessitated (staffing loss, financial non-viability, regulatory change), the Administrator shall prepare a service reduction analysis that includes: (a) description of the service to be reduced or eliminated; (b) reason for reduction; (c) impact on current patients; (d) patient transition plan; (e) staff impact; (f) regulatory notification requirements; (g) timeline. | As needed; immediately upon identifying necessity. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator | Submit the service reduction proposal to the Governing Body for approval. If the reduction is necessitated by an immediate safety or regulatory concern, the Administrator may implement an emergency temporary reduction pending Governing Body ratification at the next meeting. | At next regular meeting; emergency reduction implemented immediately with Governing Body notification within 48 hours. |
| 6.4.3 | Governing Body | Review and approve the service reduction. Ensure the patient transition plan is adequate and compliant with CL-CP-007 (Transfer & Referral Procedures). Document approval in meeting minutes. | At Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.4.4 | Administrator | Implement the service reduction including: (a) transitioning affected patients to alternative providers per CL-CP-007; (b) notifying referral sources; (c) updating Appendix A; (d) notifying applicable regulatory bodies; (e) updating intake criteria (OP-IM-002); (f) updating marketing materials. | Per approved timeline; patient transitions completed before service cessation. |

### 6.5 Intake Verification Against Scope of Services

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Intake Staff | At the time of each new referral, verify that the patient's service needs can be met within the agency's defined scope of services (Appendix A). If any ordered service falls outside the scope, do not accept the patient for that service. | At each new referral, prior to acceptance. |
| 6.5.2 | Intake Staff | If a patient requires a combination of services where some are within scope and some are not, coordinate with the Director of Nursing to determine: (a) whether the agency can accept the patient for in-scope services while referring out-of-scope services; (b) whether the patient's overall care needs require a single provider, making referral to another agency more appropriate. | At each new referral requiring services outside scope. |
| 6.5.3 | Director of Nursing | When a referred service is a specialty service (Section 6.1.2), verify that the agency currently has qualified staff with demonstrated competency to deliver that service before accepting the referral. | At each referral for specialty services. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Agency accepts a patient for a service outside its defined scope. | Director of Nursing notifies Administrator immediately. | Administrator conducts immediate review. If service cannot be provided safely and legally, patient is transferred per CL-CP-007 within 48 hours. Incident documented and reported to Compliance Officer. | Within 48 hours of identification. |
| Staffing loss results in inability to provide a currently scoped service. | Director of Nursing notifies Administrator. | Administrator implements emergency service reduction per Section 6.4.2. Governing Body notified within 48 hours. Affected patients transitioned per CL-CP-007. | Immediate; Governing Body notification within 48 hours. |
| Referral source sends referrals for out-of-scope services. | Intake staff notifies Administrator. | Administrator contacts referral source in writing to clarify the agency's scope of services. Updated scope document provided. | Within 7 calendar days of identification. |
| CMS surveyor questions whether the agency provides services within its scope. | Administrator provides Appendix A, Governing Body approval minutes, and staffing competency documentation. | If deficiency is found, Administrator develops Plan of Correction within CMS-required timeframes. | Per CMS Plan of Correction deadline. |

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Defined scope of services | Approved scope of services document (Appendix A). | Administrator (preparation); Governing Body (approval) | Agency governance file; intake department; marketing materials. | Maintained continuously; updated within 14 calendar days of any change. |
| Governing Body approval | Meeting minutes documenting annual review and approval of scope of services per GV-GB-002. | Designated Secretary | Agency governance file. | Annually; and upon any change. |
| Annual scope of services review | Administrator's written review report. | Administrator | Agency governance file. | Annually; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Service expansion/reduction proposals | Written proposals with supporting analysis. | Administrator | Agency governance file. | As changes occur; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Intake verification | Documentation in intake/referral records that patient service needs were verified against scope. | Intake Staff | Patient intake records. | At each new referral. |
| Referral source communication | Written communication to referral sources regarding scope of services. | Administrator | Agency records. | As needed; at least annually. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy (Appendix B). | Each individual (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of policy effective date, revision, or new hire. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A defined, Governing Body-approved scope of services exists. | Review of Appendix A and Governing Body minutes for approval. | Current scope on file; approved by Governing Body within the past 12 months. |
| Agency does not provide services outside its scope. | Audit of patient records and services delivered against scope document. | Zero instances of services delivered outside defined scope. |
| Intake staff verify service needs against scope at each referral. | Review of intake records for scope verification documentation. | 100% of intake records document scope verification. |
| Staffing supports the defined scope of services. | Comparison of scope document to current staffing roster and competency records. | All scoped services have qualified staff available. |
| Referral sources are informed of the scope of services. | Review of referral source communication records. | Scope communicated to active referral sources at least annually. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under the State Operations Manual (SOM) Appendix B will specifically verify:
Evidence that the agency has a defined scope of services. Surveyors will request the scope of services document and verify Governing Body approval.
Evidence that the agency provides only services within its defined scope. Surveyors will compare services delivered (from clinical records) against the defined scope.
Evidence that the agency has qualified staff for each service it provides. Surveyors will verify staffing competencies for each service discipline within the scope.
Evidence that the scope of services aligns with licensure and certification. Surveyors will compare the scope to the agency's California license and Medicare certification.
Evidence that intake criteria reference the scope of services. Surveyors may review intake processes to verify that patients are not accepted for services outside the scope.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No documented scope of services. | Surveyor will cite deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.105. | Maintain Appendix A with Governing Body approval; update annually. |
| Agency provides services for which it is not licensed or certified. | Potential enforcement action, termination of certification, state penalties. | Verify all services against licensure and certification before including in scope. |
| Agency provides specialty services without qualified staff. | Surveyor will cite inadequate staffing; potential patient safety risk. | Verify competency for all specialty services per Section 6.5.3 before accepting referrals. |
| Scope of services not reviewed annually by the Governing Body. | Surveyor will cite failure of governing body oversight. | Schedule annual review per Section 6.2; document approval in minutes. |
| Intake staff accept patients for services outside scope. | Regulatory violation; potential harm; billing risk. | Train intake staff on scope verification; audit compliance quarterly. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All intake staff, clinical staff, and department heads shall receive training on the agency's defined scope of services during initial orientation per HR-TA-005 and upon each annual update. Training shall include: (a) the current list of core and specialty services; (b) services not within scope; (c) intake verification requirements; (d) how to handle referrals for out-of-scope services.
10.2 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new hire.
10.3 The Administrator shall maintain a tracking log of all acknowledgments and report non-compliance per the escalation process defined in GV-PM-003.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

# Strategic Planning & Annual Goals

## 2. Purpose
This policy requires the Governing Body and the Administrator of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to establish, document, and review annual strategic goals and operational objectives that guide the direction of the agency. Strategic planning ensures the agency operates with purpose and direction, aligns resources with priorities, and proactively addresses opportunities and threats in the regulatory, clinical, financial, and operational environment. While CMS Conditions of Participation do not mandate a specific strategic planning process, effective strategic planning is a governance best practice that supports the Governing Body's oversight responsibilities under 42 CFR § 484.105 and is expected by accreditation bodies, OIG compliance guidance, and IBM enterprise governance standards. A defined strategic plan also provides the framework against which the Governing Body evaluates the Administrator's performance (GV-OG-002) and the QAPI program's effectiveness (QA-PG-002).
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Agency Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Compliance Officer
All department heads and senior leadership personnel
The QAPI Coordinator
This policy does not apply to clinical or administrative line staff, except to the extent that strategic goals and operational objectives are communicated to them and reflected in their departmental performance expectations.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall develop, document, and maintain an annual strategic plan that defines the agency's priorities, goals, and operational objectives for the upcoming year.
4.2 The strategic plan shall be developed under the direction of the Administrator with input from all department heads and presented to the Governing Body for review and approval at the first quarterly meeting of each calendar year or within 30 calendar days of the start of the agency's fiscal year.
4.3 The strategic plan shall include, at minimum: (a) a mission and vision statement; (b) strategic priorities for the planning period; (c) measurable goals with defined timelines and accountable parties; (d) alignment with the QAPI plan (QA-PG-002); (e) alignment with the annual operating budget (FN-FP-005); (f) a risk assessment identifying threats and mitigation strategies.
4.4 The Governing Body shall receive quarterly progress reports on strategic goal achievement and shall direct corrective action when goals are not on track.
4.5 The strategic plan shall be treated as a living document. Material changes to strategic direction require Governing Body approval, documented in meeting minutes.
4.6 Only the most current approved version of the strategic plan shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Strategic Plan | A documented plan approved by the Governing Body that defines the agency's priorities, direction, and measurable goals for a defined planning period. |
| Strategic Priority | A high-level area of focus for the agency during the planning period, derived from mission alignment, market conditions, regulatory requirements, quality data, and financial performance. |
| Operational Objective | A specific, measurable, actionable goal that supports achievement of a strategic priority, with a defined timeline and accountable party. |
| Key Performance Indicator (KPI) | A quantifiable metric used to measure progress toward an operational objective. |
| SWOT Analysis | A structured assessment of the agency's Strengths, Weaknesses, Opportunities, and Threats used to inform strategic planning. |
| Planning Period | The time horizon covered by the strategic plan, typically one fiscal or calendar year with a 3-year outlook for longer-term priorities. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Annual Strategic Planning Process

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Administrator | Initiate the annual strategic planning process by convening a strategic planning session with all department heads (Director of Nursing, Compliance Officer, HR Director, CFO/Revenue Cycle Director, Operations Director, IT Director, Risk Manager, QAPI Coordinator). | Q4 of the current planning period; at least 60 calendar days before the start of the new planning period. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator / Department Heads | Conduct an environmental assessment including: (a) SWOT analysis; (b) review of prior year strategic goal achievement; (c) regulatory environment scan (upcoming CMS rule changes, state requirements, OASIS updates); (d) financial performance review; (e) quality data review (Star Ratings, HHCAHPS, QAPI outcomes); (f) workforce analysis; (g) community and market assessment; (h) technology and infrastructure needs. | During strategic planning session(s). |
| 6.1.3 | Administrator | Develop a draft strategic plan document that includes: (a) reaffirmed or updated mission and vision statement; (b) 3–5 strategic priorities for the upcoming year; (c) operational objectives for each priority (minimum 2 per priority) with KPIs, targets, timelines, and accountable parties; (d) alignment crosswalk to QAPI plan and budget; (e) risk assessment summary. | Within 30 calendar days of the strategic planning session. |
| 6.1.4 | Administrator | Distribute the draft strategic plan to all department heads for review and comment. Incorporate feedback and finalize the draft. | Review period: 14 calendar days. |
| 6.1.5 | Administrator | Present the final draft strategic plan to the Governing Body for review and approval. The presentation must include: (a) the full strategic plan document; (b) prior year goal achievement scorecard; (c) resource requirements; (d) risk assessment. | At the first quarterly Governing Body meeting of the planning period. |
| 6.1.6 | Governing Body | Review, discuss, and formally approve the strategic plan. Approval must be documented in meeting minutes per GV-GB-002. The Governing Body may approve as presented, approve with modifications, or direct revisions with a defined resubmission deadline. | At the first quarterly meeting. |
| 6.1.7 | Administrator | Distribute the approved strategic plan to all department heads within 7 calendar days of Governing Body approval. Each department head shall communicate relevant goals and expectations to their teams within 14 calendar days. | Distribution: 7 days. Team communication: 14 days. |

### 6.2 Quarterly Progress Review

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Administrator | Prepare a quarterly strategic plan progress report that includes: (a) status of each operational objective (on track / at risk / off track / completed); (b) KPI performance against targets; (c) barriers and challenges identified; (d) corrective actions taken or proposed; (e) resource reallocation needs. | Prepared at least 7 calendar days before each quarterly Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.2.2 | Administrator | Present the quarterly strategic plan progress report to the Governing Body as part of the Administrator's regular report per GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.5.2. | At each quarterly Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.2.3 | Governing Body | Review the progress report and direct corrective action for any objective that is off track for 2 consecutive quarters. Corrective action directives must be documented in meeting minutes with assigned responsible parties and deadlines. | At each quarterly meeting; corrective action directive within 14 calendar days if thresholds are breached. |

### 6.3 Strategic Plan Modification

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator | When a material change in circumstances requires modification to the strategic plan (e.g., regulatory change, significant financial event, market shift, public health emergency), the Administrator shall prepare a written plan modification proposal. | As needed. |
| 6.3.2 | Administrator | Submit the modification proposal to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting or at a special meeting if urgent. The proposal must include: (a) the recommended change; (b) rationale; (c) impact on other strategic objectives; (d) resource implications. | At next meeting; special meeting if urgent. |
| 6.3.3 | Governing Body | Review and approve or deny the modification. Approval documented in meeting minutes. | At Governing Body meeting. |

### 6.4 Alignment with QAPI and Budget

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator / QAPI Coordinator | Ensure that the annual QAPI plan (QA-PG-002) aligns with and supports the strategic plan. Quality improvement goals should directly support at least one strategic priority. | During annual QAPI plan development; verified at first quarterly meeting. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator / CFO | Ensure that the annual operating budget (FN-FP-005) allocates resources sufficient to support strategic plan implementation. Budget variances that threaten strategic goal achievement must be reported to the Governing Body. | During annual budget development; monitored quarterly. |

### 6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Strategic plan is not developed or approved by the required deadline. | Governing Body Chair directs Administrator to present draft at a special meeting. | Draft presented and approved within 30 calendar days. | Within 30 calendar days. |
| More than 50% of operational objectives are off track at mid-year (Q2 review). | Administrator reports to Governing Body with root cause analysis. | Governing Body directs corrective action, resource reallocation, or plan modification. | Within 30 calendar days of Q2 review. |
| Department head fails to communicate strategic goals to their team. | Administrator addresses directly with the department head. | Department head communicates goals within 7 calendar days. Repeated failure results in performance management action per HR-ER-002. | 7 calendar days. |
| External event requires immediate strategic pivot (e.g., pandemic, CMS enforcement). | Administrator convenes emergency leadership meeting and notifies Governing Body Chair. | Strategic plan modification proposed per Section 6.3. | Emergency response immediate; modification proposal within 14 calendar days. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Annual strategic plan | Written strategic plan document with priorities, objectives, KPIs, timelines, and accountable parties. | Administrator (development); Governing Body (approval) | Agency governance file; distributed to department heads. | Annually; approved at first quarterly meeting. Retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Governing Body approval | Meeting minutes documenting review and approval of the strategic plan per GV-GB-002. | Designated Secretary | Agency governance file. | At time of approval. |
| Quarterly progress reports | Written progress reports on strategic goal achievement. | Administrator | Agency governance file; Governing Body meeting files. | Quarterly; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| SWOT analysis | Documented environmental assessment. | Administrator / Department Heads | Agency governance file. | Annually; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Plan modifications | Written modification proposals and Governing Body approval. | Administrator | Agency governance file. | As changes occur. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy (Appendix B). | Each individual (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of policy effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| An annual strategic plan exists and is Governing Body-approved. | Review of strategic plan document and Governing Body minutes. | Current plan on file; approved by Governing Body within the current planning period. |
| Strategic plan contains measurable objectives with KPIs. | Review of plan content for specificity and measurability. | Each priority has at least 2 objectives with KPIs, targets, timelines, and accountable parties. |
| Quarterly progress reports are presented to the Governing Body. | Review of Governing Body minutes for progress report documentation. | Progress report documented at each quarterly meeting. |
| Strategic plan aligns with QAPI plan and budget. | Crosswalk review between strategic plan, QAPI plan, and budget. | Documented alignment between all three documents. |
| Corrective action is taken for off-track objectives. | Review of Governing Body minutes for directives when objectives are off track. | Corrective action documented for any objective off track for 2 consecutive quarters. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
While CMS standard survey does not specifically require a strategic plan, surveyors may review strategic planning documentation as evidence of governing body engagement and oversight. Accreditation surveyors (if applicable) will specifically look for:
Evidence that the agency has a documented, forward-looking plan that demonstrates purposeful governance.
Evidence that the Governing Body is engaged in setting direction and not merely reacting to events.
Evidence that quality improvement activities are aligned with organizational priorities.
Evidence that financial resources are allocated to support organizational goals.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No strategic plan exists. | Demonstrates reactive rather than proactive governance; accreditation finding. | Develop plan annually per Section 6.1. |
| Strategic plan exists but is not reviewed or updated. | Outdated priorities; misalignment with current conditions. | Conduct quarterly progress reviews per Section 6.2. |
| Goals are vague and not measurable. | Cannot demonstrate progress or outcomes. | Require KPIs, targets, and timelines for each objective. |
| Strategic plan is disconnected from QAPI and budget. | Siloed operations; resource misallocation. | Require alignment crosswalk per Section 6.4. |
| Department heads do not communicate goals to their teams. | Staff unaware of organizational direction; goal achievement undermined. | Require cascaded communication per Section 6.1.7; verify compliance. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All department heads and senior leadership shall receive an orientation to the approved strategic plan within 14 calendar days of Governing Body approval. The Administrator shall conduct this orientation.
10.2 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.3 Department heads shall communicate strategic goals relevant to their departments to their teams within 14 calendar days of receiving the approved plan.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

# Policy 5 of 7: GV-OG-005
# Delegation of Authority
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-OG-005 |
| Policy Title | Delegation of Authority |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | OG — Organizational Governance |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy defines the conditions, limitations, documentation requirements, and accountability mechanisms for delegating administrative, clinical, and operational authority within Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Delegation of authority is necessary to enable efficient day-to-day operations while maintaining clear accountability. However, delegation must be carefully controlled to ensure that the Governing Body retains ultimate accountability for the agency's operation and management as required by 42 CFR § 484.105(a), and that delegated authority is exercised only by qualified individuals acting within defined limits. This policy prevents unauthorized assumption of authority, ensures all delegations are documented and traceable, and establishes the principle that accountability cannot be delegated even when tasks and authority are.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Agency Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Compliance Officer
All department heads, supervisors, and managers who exercise delegated authority
All individuals who have been formally designated as a "designee" for any leadership position (Administrator Designee, Clinical Designee)
This policy does not apply to clinical delegation of nursing tasks (e.g., RN delegation to LVN or HHA), which is governed by the California Nursing Practice Act and agency clinical policies (CL-SD-008, CL-SD-006).
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. delegates authority to the Administrator for the day-to-day management of the agency, subject to the retained responsibilities and limitations defined in GV-GB-001. This delegation shall be documented in writing and recorded in Governing Body meeting minutes.
4.2 The Administrator may delegate specific authority to department heads, supervisors, and designees to enable efficient operations, provided that: (a) the delegation is in writing; (b) the delegatee meets minimum qualifications for the delegated function; (c) the scope and limits of the delegation are clearly defined; (d) the delegation includes accountability mechanisms and reporting requirements; (e) the Administrator retains accountability to the Governing Body for all delegated functions.
4.3 The following authorities shall not be delegated by the Governing Body to any individual or entity:
Ultimate legal authority for agency operation and management (42 CFR § 484.105(a))
Appointment of the Administrator, Director of Nursing, and Compliance Officer
Approval of REQUIRED-tier policies
Approval of the annual QAPI plan
Approval of the annual operating budget
Approval of the scope of services
Approval of the emergency preparedness plan
4.4 The following authorities shall not be delegated by the Administrator without explicit Governing Body approval:
Authority to enter into contracts exceeding $25,000
Authority to terminate key leadership personnel
Authority to modify the organizational structure (material changes per GV-OG-001)
Authority to commit the agency to regulatory or legal settlements
Authority to expand or reduce the scope of services
4.5 All delegations of authority shall be documented using the Delegation of Authority Form (Appendix A) and maintained in the agency governance file.
4.6 Delegation does not transfer accountability. The delegating party remains accountable for the outcomes of the delegated function.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Delegation of Authority | The formal transfer of decision-making power and responsibility for specific functions from one individual or body to another, documented in writing with defined scope, limitations, and accountability requirements. |
| Delegator | The individual or body that transfers authority. The Governing Body delegates to the Administrator; the Administrator may delegate to department heads and designees. |
| Delegatee | The individual who receives delegated authority and is authorized to act within the scope of the delegation. |
| Retained Authority | Authority that the delegator explicitly retains and does not transfer to the delegatee. Retained authority must be exercised directly by the delegator. |
| Scope of Delegation | The defined boundaries of the delegated authority, specifying what functions, decisions, and actions the delegatee is authorized to perform. |
| Limitations of Delegation | The specific restrictions on the delegated authority, specifying what the delegatee is not authorized to do without further approval from the delegator. |
| Designee | An individual formally authorized to act on behalf of a leadership position during absences or as specifically assigned (e.g., Administrator Designee, Clinical Designee). |
| Sub-Delegation | The further delegation by a delegatee to a third party. Sub-delegation is permitted only when the original delegation explicitly authorizes it. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Governing Body Delegation to the Administrator

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Governing Body | Document the delegation of authority to the Administrator in a formal resolution or action recorded in Governing Body meeting minutes. The delegation must specify: (a) the scope of delegated authority; (b) retained authorities (Section 4.3); (c) financial authority limits; (d) reporting requirements; (e) effective date. | At the time of Administrator appointment; renewed annually at the first quarterly meeting. |
| 6.1.2 | Governing Body Chair | Issue or update a written Delegation of Authority Letter to the Administrator that incorporates the terms approved by the Governing Body. The letter shall reference this policy (GV-OG-005) and the retained authorities in Section 4.3. | Within 7 calendar days of Governing Body action. |
| 6.1.3 | Administrator | Acknowledge receipt of the Delegation of Authority Letter by signing and returning a copy to the Governing Body Chair. | Within 7 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.1.4 | Governing Body | Review the delegation to the Administrator annually. At the first quarterly meeting, the Governing Body shall confirm, modify, or revoke any delegated authority based on: (a) the Administrator's performance; (b) changes in agency operations; (c) regulatory changes. Document the review in meeting minutes. | Annually at the first quarterly meeting. |

### 6.2 Administrator Delegation to Department Heads and Designees

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Administrator | Identify functions that require delegation to department heads or designees to support efficient operations. Ensure the proposed delegatee meets minimum qualifications for the delegated function. | As needed; reviewed annually. |
| 6.2.2 | Administrator | Complete the Delegation of Authority Form (Appendix A) for each delegation. The form must include: (a) identity of the delegatee; (b) specific function(s) delegated; (c) scope and limitations; (d) duration (standing or temporary); (e) reporting requirements; (f) conditions for revocation; (g) whether sub-delegation is permitted. | Prior to the delegation becoming effective. |
| 6.2.3 | Administrator | Ensure the delegatee receives a copy of the completed Delegation of Authority Form and acknowledges understanding of the scope and limitations by signing the form. | Prior to the delegation becoming effective. |
| 6.2.4 | Administrator | Maintain a Delegation of Authority Register (Appendix B) that lists all active delegations, delegatees, scope, effective dates, and expiration dates. | Maintained continuously; updated within 7 calendar days of any change. |
| 6.2.5 | Administrator | Review all standing delegations at least annually and upon any change of personnel in a delegated role. Revoke, modify, or reissue delegations as necessary. Report active delegations to the Governing Body at the first quarterly meeting. | Annually; upon personnel change. |

### 6.3 Specific Delegation Requirements
#### 6.3.1 — Administrator Designee

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1.1 | Administrator | Designate a qualified individual to serve as Administrator Designee during planned and unplanned absences per GV-OG-002, Section 6.5 and HR-JD-002. The designee must meet minimum qualifications defined in GV-OG-002. | Prior to any absence; standing designation maintained at all times. |
| 6.3.1.2 | Administrator | Document the designation using the Delegation of Authority Form (Appendix A). Specify: (a) functions the designee may perform; (b) financial authority limits during the Administrator's absence; (c) decisions that must be deferred until the Administrator's return; (d) reporting requirements to the Administrator during the absence. | Prior to the designation becoming effective. |
| 6.3.1.3 | Administrator | Notify the Governing Body Chair when the Administrator Designee is activated for any absence exceeding 5 consecutive business days, per GV-OG-002, Section 6.5.2. | At least 7 calendar days before planned absence; within 24 hours for unplanned absence. |

#### 6.3.2 — Clinical Designee (Director of Nursing Designee)

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.2.1 | Director of Nursing | Designate a qualified registered nurse to serve as Clinical Designee during absences per HR-JD-004. The designee must hold a current California RN license and demonstrate competency in clinical oversight functions. | Prior to any absence; standing designation maintained at all times. |
| 6.3.2.2 | Director of Nursing / Administrator | Document the designation using the Delegation of Authority Form (Appendix A). The Clinical Designee's scope must clearly define authority over clinical operations, clinical staff supervision, and OASIS oversight. | Prior to the designation becoming effective. |

### 6.4 Monitoring Delegated Authority

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator | Monitor the exercise of delegated authority by reviewing: (a) decisions made by delegatees; (b) reports submitted by delegatees; (c) outcomes of delegated functions; (d) compliance with scope and limitations. | Ongoing; formally reviewed at least quarterly. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator | Address any exercise of authority that exceeds the scope of delegation immediately. If a delegatee acts beyond their authority: (a) notify the delegatee in writing; (b) assess whether the action can be ratified or must be reversed; (c) determine whether the delegation should be modified or revoked; (d) document the incident and corrective action taken. | Immediately upon identification. |
| 6.4.3 | Compliance Officer | Periodically review the Delegation of Authority Register (Appendix B) as part of the internal compliance audit program (CO-RA-002) to verify: (a) all active delegations are current and documented; (b) delegatees meet qualification requirements; (c) no unauthorized delegations exist; (d) retained authorities are being exercised by the appropriate party. | At least annually as part of the compliance audit cycle. |

### 6.5 Revocation of Delegated Authority

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Delegator (Governing Body, Administrator, or Director of Nursing) | A delegation may be revoked at any time by the delegator when: (a) the delegatee no longer meets qualifications; (b) the delegatee has exceeded the scope of authority; (c) the delegation is no longer operationally necessary; (d) the Governing Body directs revocation; (e) the delegatee's employment is terminated. | As needed; effective immediately upon written notice. |
| 6.5.2 | Delegator | Notify the delegatee in writing of the revocation, including: (a) effective date; (b) reason (unless employment-related confidentiality applies); (c) any transition requirements. | At time of revocation. |
| 6.5.3 | Administrator | Update the Delegation of Authority Register (Appendix B) within 7 calendar days of revocation. Notify affected parties of the revocation. | Within 7 calendar days. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| An individual exercises authority without a valid delegation. | Department head reports to Administrator; Administrator investigates. | Administrator determines whether the action can be ratified or must be reversed. Unauthorized exercise of authority is documented and may result in disciplinary action per HR-ER-002. | Investigation within 7 calendar days. |
| Delegatee exceeds scope of delegation. | Administrator addresses directly with delegatee. | Written notice to delegatee; delegation modified or revoked as appropriate. If the action caused harm or regulatory violation, reported to Compliance Officer and Governing Body. | Immediately upon identification. |
| Governing Body retains authority that should be delegated for operational efficiency. | Administrator presents proposal to Governing Body. | Governing Body reviews and modifies delegation scope as appropriate. | At next regular meeting. |
| No documented delegation exists for a function being exercised by a non-designated individual. | Compliance Officer flags during audit. | Administrator creates formal delegation or reassigns the function. | Within 14 calendar days. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Governing Body delegation to Administrator | Governing Body minutes documenting delegation; written Delegation of Authority Letter. | Governing Body Chair | Agency governance file; Administrator personnel file. | At appointment; renewed annually. |
| Individual delegations | Completed Delegation of Authority Form (Appendix A) for each active delegation. | Delegator (Administrator, Director of Nursing) | Agency governance file; delegatee personnel file. | Prior to delegation becoming effective; retained for minimum 7 years after revocation. |
| Delegation of Authority Register | Current register listing all active delegations (Appendix B). | Administrator | Agency governance file. | Maintained continuously; updated within 7 calendar days of any change. |
| Annual delegation review | Documentation of annual review of all delegations, presented to Governing Body. | Administrator | Agency governance file; Governing Body minutes. | Annually at first quarterly meeting. |
| Revocation documentation | Written notice of revocation to delegatee. | Delegator | Agency governance file; delegatee personnel file. | At time of revocation. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy (Appendix C). | Each individual (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of policy effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Governing Body delegation to Administrator is documented. | Review of Governing Body minutes and Delegation of Authority Letter. | Current delegation documented and renewed within the past 12 months. |
| All active delegations are documented on the Delegation of Authority Form. | Review of Appendix A forms and Appendix B Register. | 100% of active delegations documented with signed forms. |
| Delegation of Authority Register is current. | Review of Register against actual practice. | Register updated within 7 calendar days of any change; all entries current. |
| Retained authorities are exercised by the appropriate party. | Review of Governing Body minutes and decision records. | No instance of retained authority exercised by a delegatee. |
| Annual review of delegations is conducted. | Review of Governing Body minutes for annual delegation review. | Annual review documented at first quarterly meeting. |
| No unauthorized exercise of authority identified. | Compliance audit per CO-RA-002. | Zero instances of undocumented or unauthorized authority exercise. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors will verify:
Evidence that the Governing Body has delegated authority to the Administrator in writing. Surveyors will review Governing Body minutes and the delegation documentation.
Evidence that the Governing Body retains ultimate accountability. Surveyors will look for evidence that the Governing Body makes key decisions directly (budget, scope, QAPI plan, key personnel).
Evidence that delegated authority is clearly defined. Surveyors may interview the Administrator and other leaders to confirm understanding of their authority and its limits.
Evidence that designated individuals are qualified. Surveyors will review qualifications for anyone exercising delegated authority, particularly the Administrator Designee.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No written documentation of the Governing Body's delegation to the Administrator. | Surveyor may question the Administrator's authority to manage the agency. | Document delegation in Governing Body minutes and formal letter; renew annually. |
| Retained authorities are exercised by the Administrator without Governing Body approval. | Surveyor may cite failure of governing body oversight. | Clearly define retained authorities; ensure all budget, policy, and scope decisions go through the Governing Body. |
| No delegation of authority form for the Administrator Designee. | During Administrator absence, the designee may lack documented authority, creating operational and survey risk. | Maintain standing designation with current form at all times. |
| Delegation register does not exist or is outdated. | Compliance audit finding; inability to demonstrate accountability. | Maintain Appendix B; update within 7 calendar days of any change. |
| Sub-delegation occurs without authorization. | Accountability gaps; unauthorized decision-making. | Delegation forms must specify whether sub-delegation is permitted. |

| Policy ID | Policy Title | Relationship to GV-OG-005 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| GV-GB-001 | Governing Body Authority & Responsibilities | Defines retained Governing Body authorities. |
| GV-OG-001 | Organizational Structure & Reporting | Delegation follows organizational hierarchy. |
| GV-OG-002 | Administrator Qualifications & Responsibilities | Administrator's delegated authority and designee requirements. |
| HR-JD-002 | Administrator Designee | Job description for Administrator Designee. |
| HR-JD-004 | Clinical Designee | Job description for Clinical Designee. |
| CL-SD-008 | Clinical Supervision & Oversight | Clinical delegation of supervisory functions. |
| CO-RA-002 | Internal Compliance Auditing Program | Auditing of delegations for compliance. |
| HR-ER-002 | Disciplinary Action & Progressive Discipline | Consequences for unauthorized exercise of authority. |
| EN-TG-001 | Enterprise Policy Taxonomy & Classification Governance | Framework under which this policy is classified. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All individuals who exercise delegated authority (Administrator, department heads, designees) shall receive training on this policy at the time of initial delegation and annually thereafter. Training shall cover: (a) the principle that delegation transfers authority but not accountability; (b) retained authorities at each level; (c) documentation requirements; (d) scope and limitations of their specific delegation; (e) monitoring and revocation procedures.
10.2 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

# Policy 6 of 7: GV-PM-001
# Policy Development & Approval Process
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-PM-001 |
| Policy Title | Policy Development & Approval Process |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | PM — Policy Management |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 1 — Public |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Compliance Officer |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the standardized process for developing, reviewing, approving, and disseminating agency policies at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. A consistent, documented policy development process ensures that all agency policies are evidence-based, regulatory-compliant, operationally sound, and approved by the appropriate authority before implementation. CMS Conditions of Participation require that the governing body ensures the development of policies and procedures governing the agency's services (42 CFR § 484.105(a)), and the OIG Compliance Program Guidance requires written standards, policies, and procedures as a foundational element of an effective compliance program. This policy also ensures that the agency's policy framework meets IBM Knowledge Catalog governance artifact standards for lifecycle management, version control, and approval documentation.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All individuals who develop, draft, review, approve, or disseminate policies for Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Governing Body (approval of REQUIRED-tier policies)
The Administrator (approval of ESSENTIAL, RECOMMENDED, and GOOD TO HAVE-tier policies)
The Compliance Officer (policy framework governance and quality assurance)
All department heads and Policy Owner/Stewards
All subject matter experts and stakeholders who contribute to policy development
This policy governs all written policies, procedures, and protocols that operate under the enterprise policy taxonomy framework (EN-TG-001). It does not govern informal departmental standard operating procedures (SOPs), clinical standing orders, or physician orders, though these documents must be consistent with approved policies.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a standardized policy development and approval process that ensures all agency policies are developed systematically, reviewed by appropriate stakeholders, approved by the appropriate authority, and disseminated to all affected personnel before implementation.
4.2 All policies shall be classified within the agency's enterprise policy taxonomy framework per EN-TG-001 and assigned a classification tier (REQUIRED, ESSENTIAL, RECOMMENDED, or GOOD TO HAVE) that determines the approval authority.
4.3 The following approval authorities apply:

| Classification Tier | Approval Authority |
| --- | --- |
| REQUIRED | Governing Body (documented in meeting minutes per GV-GB-002) |
| ESSENTIAL | Administrator (with Compliance Officer review) |
| RECOMMENDED | Administrator (with Compliance Officer review) |
| GOOD TO HAVE | Administrator |

4.4 No policy shall be implemented, disseminated, or enforced until it has completed the full development and approval process defined in this policy.
4.5 All policies shall include the IBM-mandated metadata fields defined in the enterprise policy taxonomy framework (EN-TG-001, Section C): Policy ID, Title, Domain, Subdomain, Classification Tier, Status, Review Cycle, Access Tier, Policy Owner/Steward, Effective Date, Version, Approved By, Last Reviewed, Next Review Date, and Supersedes.
4.6 The Compliance Officer shall serve as the enterprise policy framework steward and is responsible for ensuring the integrity, consistency, and quality of the agency's policy library.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of any policy shall be considered valid. Superseded versions must be archived per EN-LC-001.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Policy | A formal written statement approved by the appropriate authority that establishes a required standard of conduct, procedure, or governance for the agency. |
| Procedure | The specific steps, actions, and responsibilities required to implement a policy. Procedures are contained within the policy document. |
| Policy Owner / Steward | The individual or role responsible for the content, accuracy, and currency of a specific policy. The Policy Owner initiates reviews, proposes revisions, and ensures the policy reflects current regulatory and operational requirements. |
| Subject Matter Expert (SME) | An individual with specialized knowledge or expertise relevant to the policy topic who is consulted during the development or review process. |
| Stakeholder | Any individual or department materially affected by a policy who should be consulted during development or review. |
| Draft Status | The initial development stage of a policy before it has been approved. Policies in Draft status are not valid for operational use. |
| Active Status | A policy that has been approved, published, and is in force. |
| Classification Tier | The priority classification assigned to a policy (REQUIRED, ESSENTIAL, RECOMMENDED, GOOD TO HAVE) that determines approval authority, review priority, and survey relevance. |
| Policy Header | The standardized metadata block at the beginning of each policy containing IBM-mandated governance fields. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Policy Initiation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Any individual or department | Submit a policy development request to the Compliance Officer when: (a) a new regulatory requirement necessitates a new policy; (b) a gap analysis identifies an area not covered by existing policies; (c) a QAPI finding, audit result, or incident investigation identifies the need for a new policy; (d) the Governing Body or Administrator directs development of a new policy. The request must include: (i) the identified need or gap; (ii) the proposed policy topic; (iii) the regulatory or operational basis; (iv) the recommended Policy Owner/Steward. | As needed; within 14 calendar days of identifying the need. |
| 6.1.2 | Compliance Officer | Review the policy development request within 7 calendar days. Determine: (a) whether the request is appropriate (not duplicative of an existing policy); (b) the appropriate domain, subdomain, and classification tier per EN-TG-001; (c) the Policy Owner/Steward; (d) the priority and target completion date. Notify the requestor and designated Policy Owner of the determination. | Within 7 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.1.3 | Compliance Officer | Assign a Policy ID per the naming conventions established in EN-TG-001 (format: [XX]-[XX]-[NNN]). Create the initial policy header with Status: DRAFT. | At the time of approval of the development request. |

### 6.2 Policy Drafting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Policy Owner / Steward | Draft the policy using the agency's standardized 11-section policy template (Appendix A), which includes: (1) Policy Header; (2) Purpose; (3) Scope; (4) Policy Statement; (5) Definitions; (6) Procedures; (7) Documentation Requirements; (8) Compliance & Audit Considerations; (9) References; (10) Training & Acknowledgment Requirements; (11) Version Control. Appendices shall be added as needed. | Per target date established by Compliance Officer; standard target is 30 calendar days from assignment. |
| 6.2.2 | Policy Owner / Steward | Ensure the policy: (a) addresses the identified need or regulatory requirement; (b) contains no placeholder text; (c) includes all applicable regulatory citations; (d) cross-references all related agency policies; (e) defines all technical terms; (f) includes measurable compliance indicators; (g) identifies common failure points and mitigation strategies; (h) addresses training and acknowledgment requirements. | During drafting. |
| 6.2.3 | Policy Owner / Steward | Consult subject matter experts and stakeholders during drafting. Document all consultations and incorporate feedback. | During drafting. |

### 6.3 Stakeholder Review

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Policy Owner / Steward | Submit the completed draft to the Compliance Officer for quality review. | Upon completion of draft. |
| 6.3.2 | Compliance Officer | Conduct a policy quality review including: (a) compliance with the 11-section template; (b) accuracy and completeness of the policy header; (c) correct domain, subdomain, and tier assignment; (d) consistency with existing policies (no conflicts); (e) regulatory citation accuracy; (f) IBM metadata compliance. Return the draft to the Policy Owner with feedback if deficiencies are identified. | Within 7 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.3.3 | Compliance Officer | Distribute the reviewed draft to all stakeholders for comment. Stakeholders include, at minimum: (a) the department(s) most affected by the policy; (b) the Director of Nursing for any clinical policy; (c) the HR Director for any personnel-related policy; (d) the CFO for any financial policy; (e) the Administrator. | Stakeholder review period: 14 calendar days. |
| 6.3.4 | Policy Owner / Steward | Incorporate stakeholder feedback. Document all substantive feedback received and the disposition of each comment (accepted, modified, rejected with rationale). | Within 7 calendar days of the close of the stakeholder review period. |

### 6.4 Approval

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Compliance Officer | Submit the final draft to the appropriate approval authority based on classification tier (Section 4.3). For REQUIRED-tier policies, the Compliance Officer shall place the policy on the agenda for the next Governing Body meeting per GV-GB-001, Section 6.3.3. | Within 7 calendar days of final draft completion. |
| 6.4.2 | Approval Authority (Governing Body or Administrator) | Review and approve, approve with modifications, or reject the policy. If approved: (a) the approval date becomes the effective date unless a future effective date is specified; (b) for REQUIRED-tier policies, approval is documented in Governing Body meeting minutes. If rejected, written feedback is provided to the Policy Owner for revision. | REQUIRED: at next Governing Body meeting. ESSENTIAL/RECOMMENDED/GOOD TO HAVE: within 14 calendar days. |
| 6.4.3 | Compliance Officer | Upon approval, update the policy Status from DRAFT to ACTIVE. Assign the effective date, version number, and all remaining header fields. Add the policy to the Master Policy Index (enterprise policy taxonomy, Section G). | Within 3 calendar days of approval. |

### 6.5 Dissemination and Implementation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Compliance Officer | Publish the approved policy in the agency's electronic document management system and ensure it is accessible to all personnel within the policy's defined scope. | Within 3 calendar days of effective date. |
| 6.5.2 | Policy Owner / Steward | Distribute notification of the new policy to all personnel within scope. Notification shall include: (a) the policy title and ID; (b) effective date; (c) summary of the policy's purpose and key requirements; (d) location of the full policy document; (e) acknowledgment requirements and deadline per GV-PM-003. | Within 7 calendar days of effective date. |
| 6.5.3 | Policy Owner / Steward | Coordinate any required training with the HR Director per the policy's Training & Acknowledgment Requirements section (Section 10 of each policy). | Per the timeframes specified in the policy's Section 10. |
| 6.5.4 | Administrator | Collect signed policy acknowledgments from all personnel within scope per GV-PM-003. | Within 14 calendar days of effective date. |

### 6.6 Expedited Policy Development (Emergency)

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | Administrator or Compliance Officer | When an urgent regulatory change, patient safety event, or critical operational need requires a policy before the standard process can be completed, initiate the expedited development process. | As needed. |
| 6.6.2 | Policy Owner / Steward | Develop the policy using the standard template. The Compliance Officer may abbreviate the stakeholder review period to 3 calendar days. | Expedited timeline per urgency. |
| 6.6.3 | Administrator | For ESSENTIAL and below-tier policies, the Administrator may approve immediately. For REQUIRED-tier policies, the Administrator may approve on an interim basis with ratification by the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Interim approval must be documented in writing. | Immediate for ESSENTIAL and below. Interim for REQUIRED; Governing Body ratification at next meeting. |
| 6.6.4 | Compliance Officer | Document the expedited process including the justification for expedited development and any abbreviated steps. | At time of expedited development. |

### 6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Policy Owner fails to complete draft by target date. | Compliance Officer notifies Administrator. | Administrator directs the Policy Owner to prioritize completion and provides a revised deadline. If not completed within 14 calendar days of the revised deadline, the Administrator reassigns the policy. | Revised deadline: 14 days. |
| Stakeholder disagreement on policy content cannot be resolved by the Policy Owner. | Policy Owner escalates to Compliance Officer; if unresolved, escalated to Administrator. | Administrator makes final determination on the disputed content. If the dispute involves a REQUIRED-tier policy, the Administrator presents the issue to the Governing Body for resolution. | Resolution within 14 calendar days of escalation. |
| REQUIRED-tier policy requires approval but no Governing Body meeting is scheduled within 30 calendar days. | Compliance Officer requests a special Governing Body meeting per GV-GB-001, Section 6.3.2, or requests interim approval per Section 6.6.3. | Special meeting convened, or interim approval issued. | Within 14 calendar days. |
| Approved policy is implemented without the required acknowledgments. | Compliance Officer flags per GV-PM-003. | Administrator ensures acknowledgment collection within 7 additional calendar days. Non-compliance escalated per HR-ER-002. | 7 additional calendar days. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Policy development request | Written request identifying the need, topic, and regulatory basis. | Requestor | Compliance files. | At initiation; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Draft policy | Policy document in DRAFT status. | Policy Owner / Steward | Electronic document management system (draft folder). | During development. |
| Stakeholder review feedback | Documented feedback and disposition log. | Policy Owner / Steward | Compliance files. | During review period; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Approval documentation | For REQUIRED: Governing Body minutes. For ESSENTIAL and below: Administrator's written approval. | Approval authority | Agency governance file; compliance files. | At time of approval. |
| Published policy | Final ACTIVE policy in standardized 11-section format. | Compliance Officer | Electronic document management system. | Within 3 calendar days of effective date; retained per EN-LC-001. |
| Dissemination notification | Written notification to all personnel within scope. | Policy Owner / Steward | Compliance files. | Within 7 calendar days of effective date. |
| Expedited development documentation | Written justification for expedited process. | Compliance Officer | Compliance files. | At time of expedited development. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed acknowledgment forms per GV-PM-003. | Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of effective date. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All policies follow the standardized 11-section template. | Compliance Officer quality audit of policy library. | 100% of policies comply with the template. |
| All policies have correct and complete header metadata. | Compliance Officer audit per IBM metadata standards. | 100% of policies have all required header fields completed. |
| All REQUIRED-tier policies are approved by the Governing Body. | Review of Governing Body minutes for each REQUIRED policy. | 100% of REQUIRED policies have documented Governing Body approval. |
| Policies are not implemented before approval. | Compliance audit of effective dates versus approval dates. | Zero instances of implementation before approval. |
| Stakeholder review is documented. | Review of feedback and disposition logs. | All policies have documented stakeholder review. |
| Policy acknowledgments are collected within 14 calendar days. | Review of acknowledgment records per GV-PM-003. | 100% acknowledgment within 14 calendar days. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors will verify:
Evidence that the agency has a formal policy development and approval process. Surveyors will request this policy (GV-PM-001) and evidence of its application.
Evidence that the Governing Body approves agency policies. Surveyors will review Governing Body minutes for policy approval actions.
Evidence that policies are current and reviewed. Surveyors will check effective dates, review dates, and version numbers.
Evidence that policies are disseminated to staff. Surveyors may ask staff if they are aware of specific policies and how they received them.
Evidence that policies are operationally enforced. Surveyors will compare observed practice against written policies.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Policies are developed informally without a standardized process. | Inconsistency, gaps, and regulatory non-compliance. | Enforce this standardized process for all policy development. |
| Policies lack required metadata or use inconsistent formats. | IBM governance non-compliance; poor policy management. | Use standardized template (Appendix A); Compliance Officer enforces quality review. |
| REQUIRED-tier policies are not presented to the Governing Body for approval. | Condition-level deficiency; failure of governing body oversight. | Compliance Officer tracks all REQUIRED policies through Governing Body approval. |
| Policies are implemented before completion of the approval process. | Enforcement of unapproved standards; legal and regulatory risk. | No policy may be disseminated before Status changes to ACTIVE. |
| Staff are not notified of new or revised policies. | Non-compliance due to lack of awareness. | Enforce dissemination per Section 6.5 and acknowledgment per GV-PM-003. |
