## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All Policy Owner/Stewards shall receive training on this policy and the standardized policy development process at the time of their first policy assignment and annually thereafter. Training shall cover: (a) the 11-section template and formatting requirements; (b) the development, review, and approval workflow; (c) IBM metadata requirements; (d) classification tier and approval authority; (e) stakeholder review process; (f) dissemination and acknowledgment requirements.
10.2 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new assignment as a Policy Owner/Steward.
10.3 The Compliance Officer shall conduct an annual training refresher for all department heads on the policy development and approval process at the beginning of each calendar year.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Compliance Officer with notification to the Administrator and the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

# Policy 7 of 7: GV-PM-002
# Policy Review & Revision Cycle
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |

| Policy ID | GV-PM-002 |
| Policy Title | Policy Review & Revision Cycle |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | PM — Policy Management |

| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 1 — Public |

## 2. Purpose
This policy mandates the periodic review and revision of all policies at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. on a defined cycle with documented evidence of review and revision. Policies that are not regularly reviewed become outdated, may not reflect current regulatory requirements, and create compliance risk during CMS surveys and audits. 42 CFR § 484.105 requires the governing body to ensure that the agency's policies and procedures are current and appropriate, and CMS surveyors evaluate whether the agency has a policy review process as part of the standard survey. This policy establishes three review cycle categories (Annual, Biennial, and Triggered) as defined in the enterprise policy taxonomy framework (EN-TG-001) and ensures that all 244 policies in the agency's framework are reviewed within their assigned cycle, with documented evidence that each review was conducted, findings were assessed, and the policy was either reaffirmed or revised.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All 244 policies in the agency's enterprise policy framework (EN-TG-001)
The Compliance Officer (policy lifecycle governance)
All Policy Owner/Stewards responsible for their assigned policies
The Administrator (approval authority for non-REQUIRED-tier revisions)
The Governing Body (approval authority for REQUIRED-tier revisions and oversight of the review cycle)
All department heads who supervise Policy Owner/Stewards
This policy does not apply to external documents referenced in agency policies (e.g., CMS guidance, regulatory texts) or to informal departmental procedures that are not part of the formal policy framework.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Every policy in the agency's enterprise policy framework shall be reviewed on a defined cycle. The three review cycles and their definitions are:

| Review Cycle | Frequency | Applicable Policies |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Annual | Reviewed every 12 months from the Last Reviewed date. | High-risk, regulatory, clinical, and compliance policies. Per the enterprise framework (EN-TG-001), 212 of 244 policies (86.9%) are on an annual cycle. |
| Biennial | Reviewed every 24 months from the Last Reviewed date. | Lower-risk operational and administrative policies. Per the framework, 26 of 244 policies (10.7%) are on a biennial cycle. |
| Triggered | Reviewed upon occurrence of a defined triggering event regardless of calendar cycle. | Policies activated by regulatory change, enforcement action, incident, or audit finding. Per the framework, 6 of 244 policies (2.5%) are on a triggered cycle. These policies are also reviewed at least biennially as a minimum floor. |

4.2 A policy review constitutes a documented assessment by the Policy Owner/Steward that evaluates: (a) whether the policy reflects current federal, state, and local regulations; (b) whether the policy reflects current CMS guidance and interpretive guidelines; (c) whether the policy reflects current agency operations and practices; (d) whether the policy is internally consistent with other agency policies; (e) whether the policy's compliance indicators and common failure points are still accurate; (f) whether any incidents, audit findings, or complaints have identified deficiencies in the policy.
4.3 A review may result in one of two outcomes: (a) reaffirmation (the policy is current and requires no changes) or (b) revision (the policy requires substantive or non-substantive changes). Both outcomes must be documented.
4.4 No policy shall exceed its review cycle deadline by more than 30 calendar days without documented justification and Compliance Officer approval for an extension.
4.5 The Compliance Officer shall maintain a Policy Review Calendar (Appendix A) that tracks the review status of all 244 policies and ensures timely completion of all reviews.
4.6 The Governing Body shall receive an annual summary report on the status of the policy review cycle at the first quarterly meeting of each calendar year.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Policy Review | A documented assessment of a policy by the Policy Owner/Steward to determine whether the policy is current, accurate, and compliant with applicable regulations and operational practices. |
| Reaffirmation | The outcome of a policy review in which the Policy Owner/Steward determines that the policy remains current and requires no revision. Reaffirmation must be documented and updates the Last Reviewed date. |
| Revision | A change to a policy's content resulting from a policy review. Revisions may be substantive or non-substantive. |
| Substantive Revision | A change to a policy that modifies requirements, responsibilities, procedures, timelines, or compliance standards. Substantive revisions require re-approval by the original approval authority and re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope. |
| Non-Substantive Revision | A change limited to formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references, or clarifying language that does not alter the policy's requirements. Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator (or Compliance Officer for PM-domain policies) without re-acknowledgment. |
| Triggered Review | A review initiated by a specific event (regulatory change, enforcement action, incident, audit finding, corrective action plan) regardless of the policy's calendar-based review cycle. |
| Review Cycle Deadline | The date by which a policy must be reviewed, calculated as the Last Reviewed date plus the assigned cycle period (12 months for Annual, 24 months for Biennial). |
| Policy Review Calendar | A master tracking document maintained by the Compliance Officer that lists all policies, their review cycle, last reviewed date, next review deadline, and review status. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Policy Review Calendar Management

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Compliance Officer | Maintain a Policy Review Calendar (Appendix A) that lists all 244 policies with: (a) Policy ID; (b) Policy Title; (c) Policy Owner/Steward; (d) Review Cycle (Annual/Biennial/Triggered); (e) Last Reviewed date; (f) Next Review Deadline; (g) Status (Pending/In Review/Completed/Overdue). | Maintained continuously; updated within 7 calendar days of any review completion. |
| 6.1.2 | Compliance Officer | At the beginning of each quarter, generate a list of all policies with review deadlines within the upcoming quarter. Distribute the list to all applicable Policy Owner/Stewards with a reminder of their review obligation. | Within the first 7 calendar days of each quarter. |
| 6.1.3 | Compliance Officer | At the beginning of each calendar year, prepare an annual policy review schedule that distributes review workload evenly across the four quarters to prevent review overload in any single period. Distribute to all department heads and Policy Owner/Stewards. | By January 15 of each calendar year. |

### 6.2 Conducting a Policy Review

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Policy Owner / Steward | Upon receiving the quarterly review reminder (or upon triggering event for triggered reviews), conduct a comprehensive review of the assigned policy. The review shall assess: (a) regulatory currency — has any applicable federal, state, or CMS regulation changed since the last review? (b) operational currency — does the policy reflect current agency practices and structure? (c) internal consistency — is the policy consistent with all cross-referenced policies? (d) effectiveness — have any incidents, audit findings, complaints, or QAPI data identified deficiencies related to this policy? (e) completeness — does the policy address all known compliance risks? (f) metadata accuracy — are all header fields correct? | Complete the review by the review cycle deadline; no later than 30 calendar days before the deadline to allow time for revision if needed. |
| 6.2.2 | Policy Owner / Steward | Document the review findings on the Policy Review Worksheet (Appendix B). The worksheet must record: (a) the date of review; (b) the reviewer's name; (c) assessment of each review criterion (6.2.1 items a–f); (d) sources consulted (regulations, CMS guidance, audit reports, incident data); (e) outcome determination: REAFFIRMED or REVISION NEEDED. | At the time of review. |
| 6.2.3 | Policy Owner / Steward | Submit the completed Policy Review Worksheet to the Compliance Officer. | Within 7 calendar days of completing the review. |

### 6.3 Reaffirmation (No Revision Needed)

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Compliance Officer | Upon receiving a Policy Review Worksheet with outcome REAFFIRMED, verify that: (a) all review criteria were assessed; (b) the reviewer is the designated Policy Owner/Steward; (c) the review is within the required cycle. | Within 7 calendar days of receipt. |
| 6.3.2 | Compliance Officer | Update the policy header: change Last Reviewed date to the date of review; update the Next Review Date per the assigned cycle. File the Policy Review Worksheet. | Within 7 calendar days. |
| 6.3.3 | Compliance Officer | Update the Policy Review Calendar (Appendix A) to reflect the completed review. | Within 7 calendar days. |

### 6.4 Revision Process

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Policy Owner / Steward | When the review outcome is REVISION NEEDED, prepare the revised policy following the process defined in GV-PM-001 (Section 6.2 through 6.5). For revisions, the existing policy serves as the base document. | Per GV-PM-001 timelines; standard target is 30 calendar days for revision. |
| 6.4.2 | Policy Owner / Steward | Classify the revision as Substantive or Non-Substantive per Section 5 definitions. Document the classification and rationale. | At the time of revision. |
| 6.4.3 | Compliance Officer | Route the revised policy to the appropriate approval authority: (a) Substantive revisions follow the same approval authority as the original policy (REQUIRED → Governing Body; ESSENTIAL and below → Administrator); (b) Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator (or Compliance Officer for PM-domain policies) with notification to the Governing Body at the next meeting. | Per GV-PM-001 approval timelines. |
| 6.4.4 | Compliance Officer | Upon approval of the revision: (a) update the Version number; (b) update the Last Reviewed and Effective Date; (c) update the Supersedes field to reference the prior version; (d) change Status to ACTIVE for the new version; (e) archive the prior version as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE" per EN-LC-001; (f) update the Policy Review Calendar. | Within 3 calendar days of approval. |
| 6.4.5 | Policy Owner / Steward | For substantive revisions, initiate re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope per GV-PM-003. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment. | Within 14 calendar days of revised effective date. |

### 6.5 Triggered Reviews

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Any individual or department | When a triggering event occurs that may affect one or more policies, notify the Compliance Officer. Triggering events include: (a) federal or state regulatory change; (b) CMS guidance update; (c) CMS survey finding or Plan of Correction; (d) significant adverse event or root cause analysis finding; (e) QAPI corrective action plan; (f) compliance audit finding; (g) legal or enforcement action; (h) change of ownership or organizational restructuring. | Within 7 calendar days of the triggering event. |
| 6.5.2 | Compliance Officer | Assess the triggering event and identify all policies potentially affected. Notify the applicable Policy Owner/Stewards to initiate triggered reviews. | Within 7 calendar days of notification. |
| 6.5.3 | Policy Owner / Steward | Conduct the triggered review per Section 6.2 and complete the Policy Review Worksheet noting the triggering event as the reason for review. | Within 30 calendar days of notification; or within the regulatory compliance deadline if shorter. |

### 6.6 Annual Reporting to the Governing Body

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | Compliance Officer | Prepare an annual Policy Review Status Report for the Governing Body that includes: (a) total policies reviewed during the prior year; (b) number reaffirmed vs. revised; (c) number of overdue reviews (with explanation); (d) summary of substantive revisions; (e) triggered reviews conducted; (f) policy review compliance rate (target: 100%); (g) recommendations for the upcoming year. | Annually; submitted to the Governing Body at least 7 calendar days before the first quarterly meeting. |
| 6.6.2 | Governing Body | Review the Policy Review Status Report. Direct corrective action if the review compliance rate falls below 95%. Document review and any directives in meeting minutes. | At the first quarterly meeting. |

### 6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Policy Owner fails to complete review by the deadline. | Compliance Officer sends written overdue notice to Policy Owner and their department head. | Policy Owner must complete review within 14 calendar days of the overdue notice. If not completed, Compliance Officer escalates to Administrator who may reassign the review or direct performance management action. | 14 calendar days from overdue notice. |
| A policy's review deadline is exceeded by more than 30 calendar days. | Compliance Officer escalates to Administrator with written justification required from the Policy Owner. | Administrator issues a formal extension (maximum 30 additional calendar days) or directs immediate completion. Policy remains marked as OVERDUE in the Policy Review Calendar. | Extension: maximum 30 additional days. |
| Regulatory change requires immediate policy revision but the Policy Owner is unavailable. | Compliance Officer assigns an interim reviewer or assumes the review directly. | Expedited review per Section 6.5 and expedited development per GV-PM-001, Section 6.6. | Per regulatory deadline. |
| Governing Body-approved REQUIRED policy requires substantive revision but no Governing Body meeting is scheduled within 30 calendar days. | Compliance Officer requests a special meeting or Administrator issues interim approval per GV-PM-001, Section 6.6.3. | Interim approval with Governing Body ratification at next meeting. | Within 14 calendar days. |
| Annual report shows review compliance rate below 95%. | Compliance Officer escalates to Governing Body with root cause analysis. | Governing Body directs corrective action, which may include additional resources, process improvements, or personnel accountability. | Per Governing Body directive. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Policy Review Calendar | Master tracking document for all 244 policies (Appendix A). | Compliance Officer | Compliance files; electronic document management system. | Maintained continuously. |
| Policy Review Worksheet | Completed worksheet for each review conducted (Appendix B). | Policy Owner / Steward | Compliance files. | At each review; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Updated policy header | Revised Last Reviewed and Next Review Date upon each reaffirmation or revision. | Compliance Officer | Policy document; electronic document management system. | Within 7 calendar days of review completion. |
| Archived superseded versions | Prior versions archived per EN-LC-001 and marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE." | Compliance Officer | Electronic document management system (archive). | At each revision; retained per CO-HP-007. |
| Annual Policy Review Status Report | Written report to Governing Body. | Compliance Officer | Agency governance file; Governing Body meeting files. | Annually at first quarterly meeting. |
| Overdue notices and extensions | Written communications regarding overdue reviews. | Compliance Officer | Compliance files. | As needed. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy (Appendix C). | Each individual (completion); Compliance Officer (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of policy effective date, revision, or new assignment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Policy Review Calendar is current and complete. | Compliance Officer audit; Governing Body review of annual report. | All 244 policies listed with current dates; zero missing entries. |
| All policies are reviewed within their assigned cycle. | Comparison of Last Reviewed dates against cycle deadlines. | 100% on-time review rate (target); minimum 95%. |
| All reviews are documented on the Policy Review Worksheet. | Review of filed worksheets. | Completed worksheet for every review. |
| Substantive revisions follow the approval process. | Review of approval documentation and Governing Body minutes (for REQUIRED-tier). | 100% of substantive revisions properly approved. |
| Annual Policy Review Status Report is presented to Governing Body. | Review of Governing Body minutes. | Report presented at first quarterly meeting annually. |
| Overdue reviews do not exceed 30 calendar days without extension. | Review of Policy Review Calendar for overdue items. | Zero reviews overdue by more than 30 calendar days without documented extension. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors will verify:
Evidence that the agency has a defined policy review process. Surveyors will request this policy (GV-PM-002) and the Policy Review Calendar.
Evidence that policies are current. Surveyors will check Last Reviewed dates on policies they select for review. An outdated policy (reviewed more than 12–24 months ago depending on cycle) is a common finding.
Evidence that reviews result in documented action. Surveyors may review Policy Review Worksheets to verify that reviews are substantive assessments and not mere "check the box" exercises.
Evidence that the Governing Body oversees the policy review process. Surveyors will look for Governing Body minutes documenting receipt of the annual policy review report and approval of revised REQUIRED-tier policies.
Evidence that regulatory changes trigger policy updates. Surveyors may ask whether a recent CMS rule change (e.g., OASIS-E2 updates) has been incorporated into affected policies.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Policies have not been reviewed within their assigned cycle. | Surveyor cites outdated policies; potential condition-level finding if the policy relates to a CMS CoP. | Maintain Policy Review Calendar; send quarterly reminders; escalate overdue reviews. |
| Reviews are documented as "reviewed — no changes" without evidence of assessment. | Surveyor may view this as inadequate; no evidence of substantive review. | Require completion of the Policy Review Worksheet (Appendix B) documenting each assessment criterion. |
| Regulatory changes occur but affected policies are not updated. | Agency operates under outdated standards; direct compliance violation. | Monitor regulatory changes per CO-RA-001; trigger reviews per Section 6.5. |
| No annual report to the Governing Body on policy review status. | Governing Body oversight gap. | Compliance Officer prepares annual report per Section 6.6. |
| Superseded versions are not archived or remain accessible to staff. | Staff may follow outdated policies. | Archive per EN-LC-001; mark as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE"; remove from active document management system. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All Policy Owner/Stewards shall receive training on this policy and the policy review process at the time of their first policy assignment and annually thereafter. Training shall cover: (a) review cycle definitions and deadlines; (b) how to conduct a policy review; (c) completing the Policy Review Worksheet; (d) when to reaffirm vs. revise; (e) the triggered review process; (f) escalation procedures for overdue reviews.
10.2 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new assignment.
10.3 The Compliance Officer shall include policy review cycle compliance in the annual compliance training program per CO-CP-008.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Compliance Officer with notification to the Administrator and the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the requirements for documented acknowledgment and attestation by all workforce members upon the issuance, revision, or assignment of agency policies. Acknowledgment ensures that all individuals subject to a policy have received, read, and understood their obligations under that policy. This policy supports the agency's compliance with CMS Conditions of Participation, OIG Compliance Program Guidance, and California state employment and licensing requirements by creating an auditable record of workforce policy awareness and accountability.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All employees of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. (full-time, part-time, and per diem)
All contracted clinical and non-clinical staff performing services on behalf of the agency
All members of the Governing Body
All students, interns, and volunteers functioning within the agency
All agency leadership, including the Administrator, Director of Nursing, and Compliance Officer
This policy does not apply to external vendors or business associates who are not directly performing services under agency supervision, except where a specific policy expressly requires their acknowledgment.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall require documented acknowledgment of all agency policies by all individuals within scope of each policy, as a condition of employment, contract, and Governing Body membership.
4.2 Policy acknowledgment shall occur:
Within 14 calendar days of initial hire or new appointment for all policies applicable to the individual's role
Within 14 calendar days of the effective date of any new policy applicable to the individual's role
Within 14 calendar days of the effective date of any substantive revision to an existing applicable policy
Within 14 calendar days of any role change that expands the scope of policies applicable to the individual
4.3 Acknowledgment shall be documented using the standardized Policy Acknowledgment Form (see Appendix A of the applicable policy, or the general acknowledgment form in Appendix A of this policy). Electronic acknowledgment through the agency's designated platform is an acceptable alternative to paper forms, provided the system captures the individual's identity, date, and a confirmation of receipt and understanding.
4.4 The Administrator shall maintain a centralized policy acknowledgment tracking log for all agency policies and all individuals within scope. This log shall be current at all times and available for CMS survey review.
4.5 Non-compliance with acknowledgment requirements shall be escalated per Section 6.4 of this policy. Failure to acknowledge assigned policies within required timeframes may result in corrective action, up to and including suspension from patient care duties pending completion.
4.6 Acknowledgment of a policy does not substitute for training. Where a policy requires training in addition to acknowledgment, both requirements must be independently satisfied and documented.
4.7 Governing Body members and senior leadership shall acknowledge all REQUIRED-tier and Tier 4 — Privileged policies within 14 calendar days of appointment and within 14 calendar days of any revision.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Policy Acknowledgment | The documented confirmation by an individual that they have received, read, and understood a specific policy and accept accountability for compliance with its requirements. |
| Attestation | A formal declaration, signed or electronically confirmed, affirming that the individual understands and will comply with the obligations stated in the policy. |
| Substantive Revision | A change to policy content that alters requirements, procedures, responsibilities, or compliance expectations. Formatting corrections and typographical fixes are non-substantive and do not require re-acknowledgment unless the Administrator determines otherwise. |
| Electronic Acknowledgment | A digitally captured confirmation through an approved platform, containing the individual's identity credentials, timestamp, and affirmative confirmation of receipt and understanding. |
| Acknowledgment Tracking Log | The centralized record maintained by the Administrator or designee documenting each individual's acknowledgment status for every assigned policy, including date completed and method of acknowledgment. |
| Role-Based Policy Assignment | The process by which specific policies are assigned to individuals based on their position, scope of duties, and access tier, as governed by policy EN-LC-003. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Policy Assignment and Acknowledgment Trigger Events

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Compliance Officer | Maintain and update the role-based policy assignment matrix identifying which policies apply to each position or role category within the agency, per policy EN-LC-003. | Updated within 7 calendar days of any new policy, revision, or role change. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator | Upon hire, appointment, or role change, provide each individual with a list of all policies assigned to their role and the corresponding acknowledgment forms or electronic acknowledgment links. | At the time of onboarding, appointment, or role change notification. |
| 6.1.3 | Compliance Officer | Upon issuance of any new policy or substantive revision to an existing policy, notify the Administrator of the acknowledgment requirement, the scope of individuals required to acknowledge, and the acknowledgment deadline. | Within 3 calendar days of policy approval and issuance. |
| 6.1.4 | Administrator | Distribute acknowledgment forms or electronic acknowledgment requests to all individuals within the scope of the newly issued or revised policy. | Within 3 calendar days of receiving notification from the Compliance Officer. |

### 6.2 Acknowledgment Completion

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Each Individual Within Scope | Complete and return the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix A) or complete the electronic acknowledgment within the required timeframe. The acknowledgment must include: (a) full legal name; (b) title and role; (c) policy ID and version number; (d) signature or electronic confirmation; (e) date of acknowledgment. | Within 14 calendar days of policy issuance, revision, or assignment. |
| 6.2.2 | Direct Supervisor / Department Manager | Actively monitor acknowledgment completion among direct reports and provide reminders to individuals who have not completed acknowledgment by day 7 of the 14-day window. | Reminder issued no later than day 7 of the acknowledgment window. |
| 6.2.3 | Administrator | Collect, verify completeness, and file all paper acknowledgment forms in the Policy Acknowledgment File maintained by the agency. For electronic acknowledgments, confirm system capture and back up records as required. | Within 3 calendar days of receiving completed forms. |
| 6.2.4 | Administrator | Update the Acknowledgment Tracking Log to reflect completion status for each individual and policy upon receipt of each acknowledgment. | Within 24 hours of receipt of each acknowledgment. |

### 6.3 Acknowledgment Tracking and Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator | Maintain a centralized Acknowledgment Tracking Log (Appendix B) documenting: (a) policy ID and version; (b) effective date; (c) names of all individuals required to acknowledge; (d) acknowledgment status (complete/incomplete); (e) date of acknowledgment; (f) method (paper/electronic). | Maintained continuously; updated within 24 hours of each acknowledgment receipt. |
| 6.3.2 | Administrator | Generate and report policy acknowledgment completion statistics to the Compliance Officer on a monthly basis, identifying any individuals with outstanding acknowledgments. | Monthly, by the 5th of the following month. |
| 6.3.3 | Compliance Officer | Include policy acknowledgment compliance rates in the quarterly compliance report presented to the Governing Body per policy GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.3.2. | Quarterly. |
| 6.3.4 | Administrator | Retain all acknowledgment records for a minimum of 7 years from the date of acknowledgment, per policy CO-HP-007. Records must be accessible within 24 hours of a CMS survey request. | Retained for minimum 7 years; immediately accessible upon request. |

### 6.4 Non-Compliance and Escalation

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Individual has not acknowledged within 14-day window | Administrator issues written notice to the individual and copies their direct supervisor. | Individual is granted a mandatory extension of 7 additional calendar days. Supervisor must confirm acknowledgment within this extended period. | Written notice issued on day 15; extension deadline at day 21. |
| Individual has not acknowledged within 21-day extended window | Administrator escalates to the Compliance Officer and the individual's Department Head. | Formal corrective action initiated per HR-ER-002. For clinical staff, consideration of suspension from patient care duties pending completion. | Escalation on day 22; corrective action initiated within 3 calendar days. |
| Governing Body member has not acknowledged within 14-day window | Administrator notifies the Governing Body Chair in writing. | Governing Body Chair issues a mandatory completion directive with a 7-day deadline. Non-compliance documented in Governing Body minutes. | Notification on day 15; Chair directive within 3 calendar days. |
| Contractor or per diem staff has not acknowledged | Administrator notifies the contracting entity or supervisor. | Contract may be suspended pending acknowledgment completion. Persistent non-compliance reported to the Compliance Officer for contract review. | Notification on day 15; suspension consideration at day 22 if unresolved. |

### 6.5 Acknowledgment During Leaves of Absence

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator | Track individuals on approved leave of absence who have pending acknowledgment requirements. | Ongoing; leave status noted in Acknowledgment Tracking Log. |
| 6.5.2 | Administrator | Upon the individual's return from leave, provide all outstanding acknowledgment requirements and grant a 7-calendar-day completion window from the date of return. | Within 1 business day of the individual's return. |
| 6.5.3 | Compliance Officer | Policies that are operationally critical (REQUIRED-tier, patient safety-related) shall be reviewed with the returning individual by their supervisor on the first day of return, regardless of the formal acknowledgment timeline. | First day of return from leave. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Individual policy acknowledgments | Completed Policy Acknowledgment Forms (Appendix A) or electronic acknowledgment records. | Each individual (completion); Administrator (collection and filing) | Policy Acknowledgment File (physical or electronic). | Collected within 14 calendar days of trigger event; retained 7 years. |
| Acknowledgment Tracking Log | Centralized log tracking acknowledgment status for all individuals and all assigned policies (Appendix B). | Administrator | Agency administrative records; accessible for survey review. | Updated within 24 hours of each acknowledgment receipt; maintained continuously. |
| Non-compliance escalation notices | Written notices issued to individuals and supervisors for failure to acknowledge within required timeframes. | Administrator | Personnel file of the individual; Compliance file. | Issued on day 15; retained for minimum 7 years. |
| Monthly acknowledgment completion report | Summary of acknowledgment completion rates by policy and by department or role. | Administrator | Compliance file; reported to Compliance Officer. | Generated monthly by the 5th of the following month. |
| Governing Body acknowledgment records | Signed acknowledgment forms for all Governing Body members for all applicable policies. | Each Governing Body member (completion); Administrator (collection) | Governance file; Policy Acknowledgment File. | Collected within 14 calendar days of trigger event; retained 7 years. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Policy acknowledgment completion rate | Review of Acknowledgment Tracking Log; comparison of required vs. completed acknowledgments. | 100% completion within 14-calendar-day window for all assigned policies. |
| Timeliness of acknowledgment distribution | Review of distribution dates vs. policy effective dates in agency records. | Acknowledgment requests distributed within 3 calendar days of policy issuance or notification. |
| Acknowledgment Tracking Log currency | Audit of log update dates against acknowledgment receipt dates. | Log updated within 24 hours of each acknowledgment receipt. |
| Non-compliance escalation adherence | Review of escalation notices and corrective action records against non-compliance timelines. | 100% of overdue acknowledgments escalated per Section 6.4 timelines. |
| Governing Body acknowledgment compliance | Review of Governing Body member acknowledgment records for all required policies. | 100% completion by all Governing Body members within 14 calendar days. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors and OIG auditors will specifically examine:
Evidence that workforce members have acknowledged the policies governing their scope of practice and compliance obligations, particularly REQUIRED-tier clinical, compliance, and patient safety policies.
Evidence that the Governing Body has acknowledged all REQUIRED-tier governance policies, including this policy.
Currency and completeness of the Acknowledgment Tracking Log.
Evidence that non-compliance with acknowledgment requirements is tracked and acted upon.
Consistency between the agency's stated acknowledgment requirements (in individual policy documents) and the tracking system maintained by the Administrator.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Acknowledgment forms collected but not tracked centrally. | Surveyor unable to verify workforce policy awareness; potential citation for lack of staff education oversight. | Maintain Appendix B Tracking Log as single source of truth; update within 24 hours of receipt. |
| New hires complete onboarding acknowledgments but revised policies are not re-acknowledged. | Documentation gaps; workforce unaware of updated requirements. | Use Compliance Officer notification process (Section 6.1.3) to trigger re-acknowledgment for all revisions. |
| Electronic acknowledgment platform records not backed up or accessible. | Data loss; inability to produce records during survey. | Back up electronic records monthly; maintain exportable reports. |
| Per diem and contractor acknowledgments not tracked consistently. | Compliance gaps in contracted workforce; potential survey finding. | Extend Acknowledgment Tracking Log to include all contracted personnel. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All individuals within the scope of this policy shall receive orientation to the acknowledgment process as part of new employee orientation or Governing Body onboarding, conducted by the Administrator or designee.
10.2 All individuals within scope shall acknowledge this policy (GV-PM-003) within 14 calendar days of their effective date of hire, appointment, or this policy's effective date.
10.3 Annual refresher on acknowledgment obligations shall be incorporated into the agency's annual mandatory training program per policy HR-TD-001.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose.
11.3 Any substantive revision requires Governing Body approval (documented in meeting minutes), re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days, and update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting and do not require re-acknowledgment.

# GV-PM-004 — Communication & Notification Standards
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-PM-004 |
| Policy Title | Communication & Notification Standards |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | PM — Policy Management |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 1 — Public |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 1.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the standards governing internal and external communication and notification at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Effective communication is essential to patient safety, regulatory compliance, operational continuity, and workforce accountability. This policy defines timeliness requirements, acceptable communication channels, escalation protocols, and documentation obligations for all categories of organizational communication, ensuring that critical information reaches the appropriate individuals in a reliable, accurate, and auditable manner.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All employees of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. at all levels
All contracted clinical and non-clinical personnel performing services on behalf of the agency
The Administrator, Director of Nursing, Compliance Officer, and all department leads
The Governing Body, with respect to notifications directed to or from the Governing Body
All communications involving patients, families, referral sources, payers, and regulatory bodies
This policy does not govern clinical documentation standards (see CL-CD-001) or HIPAA-specific privacy and security requirements for PHI communications (see CO-HP-001, CO-HP-002), which are addressed in their respective policies but must be read in conjunction with this policy.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain clear, timely, and documented communication channels at all organizational levels to support patient safety, compliance, and operational effectiveness.
4.2 All communications that require action, document a material decision, or affect patient care or regulatory compliance shall be documented in writing (paper or electronic) and retained per the agency's record retention requirements in policy CO-HP-007.
4.3 Verbal communications regarding patient care, compliance concerns, or operational decisions shall be confirmed in writing within the timeframes specified in Section 6 of this policy.
4.4 All communications involving PHI shall comply with HIPAA privacy and security requirements per policies CO-HP-001 and CO-HP-002. PHI shall not be transmitted via unsecured channels, including personal email or unencrypted text messaging, without explicit patient authorization or a documented operational emergency.
4.5 The agency shall maintain an escalation protocol ensuring that urgent clinical, compliance, and regulatory matters are communicated to the appropriate leadership level without delay. No individual shall impede or delay the escalation of a patient safety or compliance concern.
4.6 Regulatory agencies, accrediting bodies, and government authorities shall receive all required notifications within the timeframes mandated by applicable law or regulation, and responses shall be coordinated through the Administrator with oversight from the Compliance Officer.
4.7 All staff are obligated to communicate concerns about patient safety, compliance, or operational risk through established channels and are protected from retaliation for doing so per policy CO-CP-005.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Internal Communication | Any communication occurring between agency personnel, departments, leadership, or the Governing Body regarding operational, clinical, compliance, or administrative matters. |
| External Communication | Any communication directed to or received from parties outside the agency, including patients, families, referral sources, payers, vendors, regulatory bodies, accreditors, and the media. |
| Escalation | The process of elevating a matter to a higher level of authority when it cannot be resolved at the current level, when patient safety is at risk, or when a defined escalation threshold has been met. |
| Urgent Notification | A communication that must be transmitted within 24 hours due to patient safety risk, regulatory deadline, or operational emergency. |
| Critical Notification | A communication that must be transmitted immediately or within 1–4 hours due to an imminent threat to patient or staff safety, a serious adverse event, or an active regulatory enforcement action. |
| Secure Communication Channel | A method of transmitting information that protects confidentiality, integrity, and availability, including encrypted email, agency-approved messaging platforms, telephone calls to verified recipients, and in-person communication. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Internal Communication Standards
#### 6.1.1 — Routine Internal Communication

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1.1 | All Staff | Use agency-approved communication channels for all internal communications. Approved channels include the agency's electronic health record (EHR) messaging system, agency email, telephone, and in-person communication. Personal email or personal text messaging shall not be used for agency business communications without explicit Administrator approval. | At all times. |
| 6.1.1.2 | All Staff | Document all clinical and compliance communications that require follow-up or action in the appropriate system of record (EHR, compliance system, or administrative file), regardless of the original communication channel used. | Within the same business day as the communication. |
| 6.1.1.3 | Supervisors and Department Leads | Conduct regular team communication meetings (in-person or virtual) no less than monthly. Meeting agendas and attendance records shall be documented and retained. | Monthly; documentation retained for minimum 3 years. |

#### 6.1.2 — Urgent and Critical Internal Notifications

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.2.1 | Any Staff Member | Immediately notify the direct supervisor and/or the Director of Nursing of any patient safety concern, adverse event, or clinical emergency discovered during or following a patient visit. | Immediately upon discovery; no later than 1 hour after the visit during which the event occurred. |
| 6.1.2.2 | Director of Nursing | Notify the Administrator of any reportable adverse event, potential CMS survey trigger, or patient safety concern requiring escalation. | Within 2 hours of being notified by staff. |
| 6.1.2.3 | Administrator | Notify the Compliance Officer of any event that may have legal, regulatory, or compliance implications, including but not limited to: (a) suspected fraud, waste, or abuse; (b) HIPAA breach; (c) patient complaint with legal risk indicators; (d) CMS survey initiation. | Within 4 hours of the Administrator becoming aware. |
| 6.1.2.4 | Administrator | Notify the Governing Body Chair immediately (within 24 hours) of: (a) a CMS survey resulting in a Condition-level deficiency; (b) a serious adverse event; (c) a key leadership vacancy; (d) any regulatory enforcement action. Per policy GV-GB-001, Section 6.3.2, the Governing Body Chair may convene a special meeting based on this notification. | Within 24 hours; certain events (imminent patient safety) require immediate notification. |

#### 6.1.3 — Policy and Operational Notification

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.3.1 | Compliance Officer | Upon approval of a new or revised policy, notify the Administrator and relevant department leads of: (a) the policy ID and title; (b) the effective date; (c) the scope of affected personnel; (d) the acknowledgment deadline. | Within 3 calendar days of policy approval. |
| 6.1.3.2 | Administrator | Distribute policy notification to all affected personnel per policy GV-PM-003 (Section 6.1.4) and confirm distribution in the Acknowledgment Tracking Log. | Within 3 calendar days of receiving notification from the Compliance Officer. |
| 6.1.3.3 | Administrator | Notify all affected personnel of operational changes (scheduling, service area, staffing structure, leadership changes) affecting their duties. | Within 3 business days of the operational change being finalized; immediately for urgent operational changes. |

### 6.2 External Communication Standards
#### 6.2.1 — Patient and Family Communication

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1.1 | Assigned Clinician / Care Coordinator | Maintain open and proactive communication with patients and families regarding care plans, scheduled visits, and any changes in service delivery. | Ongoing; patients notified of visit changes no less than 2 hours in advance when operationally possible. |
| 6.2.1.2 | Director of Nursing / Administrator | Notify patients and families in writing of any significant change in the patient's plan of care, including changes to ordered services, disciplines, or discharge. | Within 48 hours of the change, or per the timeframe specified in the applicable clinical policy. |
| 6.2.1.3 | Administrator | Respond to all written patient or family complaints within 5 business days of receipt, per policy OP-PA-001. | Within 5 business days of complaint receipt. |
| 6.2.1.4 | All Clinical Staff | Obtain verbal confirmation from patients or caregivers when communicating instructions, care plan changes, or discharge planning. Document the communication and confirmation in the clinical record. | At the time of the communication; documented within the same business day. |

#### 6.2.2 — Referral Source and Care Coordination Communication

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.2.1 | Director of Nursing / Designated Care Coordinator | Communicate the patient's start of care status and initial plan of care to the referring physician or practitioner. | Within 5 business days of start of care. |
| 6.2.2.2 | Assigned Clinician | Notify the referring physician of any significant change in patient condition, new clinical findings, or changes to the plan of care. Document all physician communications in the clinical record. | Within 24 hours of the clinical finding or change; immediately for urgent changes. |
| 6.2.2.3 | Director of Nursing / Administrator | Coordinate discharge summaries and transitions of care communications to receiving providers, facilities, or community agencies. | Per the timeframes specified in policy CL-CP-006 and CL-CP-007. |

#### 6.2.3 — Payer and Revenue Cycle Communication

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.3.1 | Revenue Cycle / Administrator | Respond to all payer requests for additional documentation (ADR), pre-claim review requests, and audit inquiries within the timeframes specified by the payer or applicable regulation. | Per payer-specified deadline; no less than 5 business days before deadline to allow internal review. |
| 6.2.3.2 | Administrator | Notify the Compliance Officer of any payer communication suggesting a pattern of claim denials, audit focus, or suspected billing investigation. | Within 3 business days of receiving such communication. |

#### 6.2.4 — Regulatory and Government Communication

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.4.1 | Administrator | All communications with CMS, HCAI (California Department of Health Care Access and Information), DHCS, or any state or federal regulatory body shall be coordinated by the Administrator in consultation with the Compliance Officer. No staff member shall respond independently to regulatory inquiries without Administrator authorization. | At all times; immediately upon receipt of any regulatory communication. |
| 6.2.4.2 | Administrator | Provide written acknowledgment of receipt of all regulatory correspondence within 3 business days. Forward the correspondence to the Compliance Officer and, if applicable, Legal Counsel within 1 business day of receipt. | Acknowledgment within 3 business days; internal forwarding within 1 business day. |
| 6.2.4.3 | Administrator and Compliance Officer | Submit all required regulatory notifications (e.g., license renewals, change of ownership, adverse event reports) within mandated timeframes. Maintain copies of all regulatory submissions and responses. | Per applicable regulatory deadline; copies retained per CO-HP-007. |
| 6.2.4.4 | Administrator | During a CMS or HCAI survey, designate a single point of contact (typically the Administrator or designee) to accompany surveyors, respond to informational requests, and coordinate document production. Notify the Compliance Officer and Governing Body Chair immediately upon surveyor arrival. | Immediately upon surveyor arrival. |

#### 6.2.5 — Media and Public Communications

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.5.1 | Administrator | All media inquiries, press releases, and public statements regarding the agency shall be reviewed and authorized by the Administrator prior to release. No employee shall speak to media on behalf of the agency without explicit written authorization. | Prior to any media communication. |
| 6.2.5.2 | Administrator | In the event of a public health emergency, significant adverse event, or regulatory action generating public or media interest, coordinate all external statements with the Compliance Officer and, if applicable, Legal Counsel. | Prior to any public statement; immediately upon identification of media interest. |

### 6.3 Escalation Protocol

| Escalation Level | Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Level 1 — Staff to Supervisor | Clinical concern, patient safety issue, or operational problem identified by frontline staff. | Staff notifies direct supervisor or Director of Nursing. | Immediately; no later than 1 hour after discovery. |
| Level 2 — Supervisor to Administrator | Supervisor cannot resolve, issue has compliance or regulatory implications, or adverse event has occurred. | Supervisor or Director of Nursing notifies Administrator. | Within 2 hours of Level 1 escalation. |
| Level 3 — Administrator to Compliance Officer | Issue has legal, regulatory, billing, or HIPAA implications. | Administrator notifies Compliance Officer. | Within 4 hours of Administrator notification. |
| Level 4 — Administrator / Compliance Officer to Governing Body Chair | Condition-level survey finding, serious adverse event, key leadership vacancy, regulatory enforcement action, or fraud concern. | Administrator or Compliance Officer notifies Governing Body Chair. | Within 24 hours; immediately for imminent patient safety threats. |
| Level 5 — Emergency Escalation | Imminent threat to patient or staff safety requiring emergency response. | Any staff member may directly contact 911 and/or the Administrator simultaneously, bypassing normal escalation hierarchy. | Immediately. |

### 6.4 Communication During Declared Emergencies

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator | Activate the agency's emergency communication plan (per policy OP-FM-005) when a declared emergency or disaster affects agency operations. | Immediately upon declaration or operational impact. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator | Establish and communicate an emergency contact roster and alternative communication channels (e.g., backup phone lines, emergency messaging system) to all staff prior to and during declared emergencies. | Prior to and immediately at onset of emergency. |
| 6.4.3 | All Staff | Check in with the agency via the designated emergency communication channel within 2 hours of any declared emergency affecting the service area. | Within 2 hours of emergency declaration affecting operations. |
| 6.4.4 | Director of Nursing | Initiate patient welfare communication per policy RM-EP-003 to confirm patient safety status and service continuity. | Within 4 hours of emergency declaration. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Patient and family communications | Documentation in patient's clinical record of all significant communications regarding care, plan changes, and discharge. | Assigned Clinician | EHR / Clinical Record | Same business day as communication. |
| Regulatory correspondence | Copies of all incoming and outgoing communications with CMS, HCAI, DHCS, and other regulatory bodies. | Administrator | Regulatory affairs file; Compliance file | Within 1 business day of receipt or transmission; retained per CO-HP-007. |
| Adverse event notifications | Documentation of all notifications made following adverse events, including who was notified, method, date, and time. | Administrator / Director of Nursing | Compliance file; QA file; Clinical record | Within 24 hours of notification. |
| Internal escalation records | Written documentation of all Level 2 and above escalations, including the nature of the concern, parties notified, and actions taken. | Administrator | Administrative file; Compliance file | Within 24 hours of escalation; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Emergency communication records | Log of all staff check-ins and patient welfare contacts during declared emergencies. | Administrator | Emergency preparedness file | Within 24 hours of emergency activation; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Media authorization records | Written authorization for any media communication, including a copy of the approved statement. | Administrator | Administrative file | Prior to release; retained minimum 7 years. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Timeliness of adverse event escalation | Review of adverse event reports and escalation documentation dates and times. | 100% of Level 1 escalations within 1 hour; Level 4 within 24 hours. |
| Regulatory correspondence response timeliness | Review of correspondence files and response dates. | 100% of regulatory correspondence acknowledged within 3 business days. |
| Patient and family communication documentation | Audit of clinical records for documentation of significant communications. | Documentation present in clinical record for ≥95% of audited records. |
| Staff emergency check-in compliance | Review of emergency communication log against staff roster. | ≥95% of staff check-in within 2 hours during declared emergencies. |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Staff respond independently to regulatory inquiries without Administrator involvement. | Inaccurate or unauthorized statements; regulatory and legal exposure. | Train all staff on communication chain of command; post escalation protocol in agency. |
| Verbal communications not confirmed in writing. | No auditable record of critical communications; disputes regarding instructions. | Reinforce documentation requirements in orientation and annual training. |
| PHI transmitted via unsecured personal messaging applications. | HIPAA breach; regulatory citation. | Enforce use of agency-approved secure communication channels only. |
| Delayed notification to Governing Body of critical events. | Governing Body unable to fulfill oversight obligations; CMS survey citation. | Administrator escalation checklists; on-call contact protocol for Governing Body Chair. |

10.2 Annual refresher training shall be incorporated into the agency's mandatory training program per policy HR-TD-001.
10.3 All individuals within the scope of this policy shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of hire, appointment, or the policy's effective date per policy GV-PM-003.

# GV-PM-005 — Stakeholder Grievance & Feedback Management
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |

| Policy ID | GV-PM-005 |
| Policy Title | Stakeholder Grievance & Feedback Management |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | PM — Policy Management |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 1 — Public |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 1.0 |

| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the process for receiving, documenting, investigating, resolving, and trending grievances and feedback from patients, families, staff, referral sources, and other stakeholders of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. A robust grievance and feedback management program supports patient rights, workforce engagement, quality improvement, and regulatory compliance. This policy integrates with the agency's QAPI program and patient rights framework to ensure that all concerns are treated seriously, addressed promptly, and used to drive continuous improvement.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All patients receiving services from Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
All family members, legal representatives, and caregivers acting on behalf of patients
All agency employees and contracted staff
Referral sources, physicians, discharge planners, and care coordination partners
Community members or organizations with concerns about agency practices
All agency leadership, including the Administrator, Director of Nursing, and Compliance Officer
This policy applies to all types of grievances and feedback, including those received in person, by telephone, in writing, electronically, or through third parties (e.g., regulatory hotlines, ombudsman offices).
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain an accessible, non-retaliatory, and responsive grievance and feedback management process that ensures all concerns from patients, families, staff, and other stakeholders are received, documented, investigated, and resolved in a timely and respectful manner.
4.2 Patients and families shall be informed of their right to file a grievance and the process for doing so at the time of admission, per policy CL-PR-001 (Patient Rights & Responsibilities), and shall not be subject to retaliation, discrimination, or changes in care as a result of filing a grievance.
4.3 All grievances shall be acknowledged within 5 business days of receipt and resolved or formally responded to within 30 calendar days of receipt, unless the nature of the grievance requires a longer investigation, in which case interim status updates shall be provided every 14 calendar days.
4.4 Staff grievances and complaints shall be received through the process defined in this policy and in policy HR-ER-003 (Employee Grievance & Complaint Process). Staff shall not be subject to retaliation for filing a good-faith grievance, per policy CO-CP-005.
4.5 All grievances and feedback shall be logged in the agency's Grievance and Feedback Register and trended quarterly as part of the QAPI program (policy QA-PG-001) to identify systemic issues and opportunities for improvement.
4.6 Grievances alleging abuse, neglect, exploitation, or HIPAA violations shall be immediately escalated to the Compliance Officer and handled per their respective specialized policies (CL-PR-006, CO-HP-003).
4.7 The Administrator shall report grievance volume, resolution rates, and trend data to the Governing Body at each quarterly meeting as part of the operational oversight report.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Grievance | A formal or informal expression of dissatisfaction by a patient, family, staff member, referral source, or other stakeholder regarding the agency's services, practices, personnel, or decisions. Grievances include complaints about care quality, staff conduct, billing, scheduling, access, and patient rights. |
| Feedback | Information provided by stakeholders about their experience with the agency, which may be positive, neutral, or negative. Feedback that expresses dissatisfaction is treated as a grievance for purposes of documentation and follow-up. |
| Complainant | The individual or entity submitting a grievance or feedback. |
| Grievance Coordinator | The designated individual (typically the Administrator or designee) responsible for receiving, logging, investigating, and resolving grievances. |
| Resolution | A documented determination that a grievance has been investigated, a conclusion has been reached, and the complainant has been notified of the outcome and any corrective actions taken. |
| Escalated Grievance | A grievance that involves an allegation of abuse, neglect, exploitation, HIPAA violation, fraud, or serious patient safety concern, requiring immediate referral to the Compliance Officer or Director of Nursing beyond the standard grievance process. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Receiving and Logging Grievances

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Any Staff Member | Upon receiving a grievance or complaint from any stakeholder, regardless of method (phone, in person, written, electronic), document the information and forward it to the Grievance Coordinator (Administrator or designee) immediately. Staff shall not attempt to resolve grievances independently without Grievance Coordinator involvement, except for immediate service issues (e.g., visit timing) that can be resolved on the spot. | Immediately upon receipt; forward to Grievance Coordinator within 4 business hours. |
| 6.1.2 | Grievance Coordinator | Log every grievance in the Grievance and Feedback Register (Appendix A) within 1 business day of receipt, capturing: (a) date and time received; (b) name of complainant and relationship to agency; (c) method of receipt; (d) category of grievance (clinical care, staff conduct, billing, scheduling, patient rights, other); (e) summary of the concern; (f) initial assigned investigator; (g) status. | Within 1 business day of receipt. |
| 6.1.3 | Grievance Coordinator | Acknowledge receipt of the grievance to the complainant in writing (letter, email, or documented phone call) confirming: (a) the grievance has been received; (b) the agency's commitment to investigate; (c) the expected timeframe for resolution (30 calendar days); (d) the contact information for the Grievance Coordinator. | Within 5 business days of receipt. |

### 6.2 Triage and Escalation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Grievance Coordinator | Upon logging, triage each grievance into one of three categories: (a) Standard — addressed through normal investigation and resolution process (Section 6.3); (b) Urgent — involves potential patient safety, current clinical care concern, or immediate service failure requiring same-day response; (c) Escalated — involves alleged abuse, neglect, exploitation, HIPAA breach, fraud, or serious regulatory violation. | Within 4 business hours of logging. |
| 6.2.2 | Grievance Coordinator | For Urgent grievances, notify the Director of Nursing and Administrator immediately and initiate a same-day clinical or operational response. Document the urgent response in the Grievance Register. | Same business day as receipt; notify Director of Nursing within 2 hours. |
| 6.2.3 | Grievance Coordinator | For Escalated grievances, immediately notify the Compliance Officer and, if the grievance involves patient abuse or neglect, the Director of Nursing. Escalated grievances are handled per their applicable specialized policy (CL-PR-006, CO-HP-003, CO-FA-003, CO-CP-007) in parallel with the standard grievance process. | Immediately upon identification; within 2 hours of receipt. |

### 6.3 Investigation and Resolution

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Grievance Coordinator / Assigned Investigator | Investigate each grievance by: (a) reviewing relevant clinical records, billing records, scheduling records, or personnel files as applicable; (b) interviewing involved staff (ensuring non-retaliation); (c) consulting the Director of Nursing for clinical grievances; (d) consulting the Compliance Officer for compliance-related grievances; (e) documenting all investigative steps and findings. | Investigation initiated within 5 business days of receipt; completed within 30 calendar days. |
| 6.3.2 | Grievance Coordinator | For grievances requiring longer than 30 days to resolve (complex clinical investigations, multi-party disputes, pending regulatory review), provide the complainant with an interim written status update every 14 calendar days until resolution. | Every 14 calendar days until resolution. |
| 6.3.3 | Grievance Coordinator | Document the investigation findings and determination in the Grievance Register, including: (a) findings of fact; (b) determination (substantiated, unsubstantiated, or partially substantiated); (c) corrective actions taken or recommended; (d) responsible party and timeline for corrective action. | Within 5 business days of completing the investigation. |
| 6.3.4 | Grievance Coordinator | Notify the complainant in writing of the investigation outcome, including: (a) the determination; (b) any corrective actions taken; (c) the complainant's right to escalate to external regulatory bodies if not satisfied with the agency's response, including contact information for HCAI, CMS, and the California ombudsman as applicable. | Within 5 business days of determination; no later than 30 calendar days from original receipt (or with interim updates per 6.3.2). |
| 6.3.5 | Administrator | For grievances resulting in corrective actions, enter the corrective action into the agency's corrective action tracking system per policy QA-AE-003 and monitor completion. | Within 5 business days of determination; monitor per QA-AE-003 timelines. |

### 6.4 Staff Grievances

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Any Employee | Submit a staff grievance to the Administrator or Human Resources (if designated) in writing or verbally. Staff are protected from retaliation for submitting good-faith grievances per policy CO-CP-005 and HR-ER-003. | At any time; no time limit on grievance submission for active employees. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator / HR Director | Receive, log, and investigate staff grievances per the process defined in policy HR-ER-003. Cross-reference in the Grievance and Feedback Register if the grievance has QAPI or patient safety implications. | Per HR-ER-003 timelines; cross-reference within 1 business day. |
| 6.4.3 | Administrator | Staff grievances raising concerns about patient safety, compliance violations, or workforce conduct that may affect patient care shall be escalated to the Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer per policy GV-PM-004. | Within 2 business hours of receipt for safety/compliance-related staff grievances. |

### 6.5 Trending, Reporting, and QAPI Integration

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Grievance Coordinator | Compile a quarterly Grievance Trend Report (Appendix B) from the Grievance and Feedback Register, summarizing: (a) total grievances received by category; (b) resolution rates and timeliness; (c) substantiation rates; (d) corrective actions initiated; (e) repeat grievance patterns. | Quarterly; completed 10 calendar days before each Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.5.2 | Administrator | Include grievance trend data in the quarterly operational report presented to the Governing Body per policy GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.3.2. | At each quarterly Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.5.3 | QAPI Coordinator / Administrator | Submit the Grievance Trend Report to the QAPI committee for integration into the QAPI dashboard per policy QA-PI-002. Any grievance category showing a sustained pattern over two consecutive quarters shall be designated as a QAPI Performance Improvement Project trigger. | Quarterly; PIP trigger within 14 calendar days of identifying the pattern. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Grievance and Feedback Register | Centralized log of all grievances received (Appendix A). | Grievance Coordinator | Administrative file; accessible for survey review. | Updated within 1 business day of receipt; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Acknowledgment letters | Written acknowledgment sent to complainant confirming receipt. | Grievance Coordinator | Grievance file for each complainant. | Within 5 business days of receipt; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Investigation records | Notes, interviews, records reviewed, and findings for each grievance investigation. | Assigned Investigator | Grievance file; Compliance file for escalated grievances. | Completed within 30 calendar days; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Resolution notices | Written determination and outcome letter to complainant. | Grievance Coordinator | Grievance file; copy to complainant. | Within 30 calendar days; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Quarterly Grievance Trend Report | Summary of grievance volume, categories, resolution rates, and trends (Appendix B). | Grievance Coordinator | Administrative file; Governing Body file; QAPI file. | Quarterly; 10 days before Governing Body meeting; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Corrective action tracking | Documentation of corrective actions initiated from grievance investigations. | Administrator | QA-AE-003 tracking system. | Per QA-AE-003 timelines; retained per CO-HP-007. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Grievance acknowledgment timeliness | Review of Grievance Register against acknowledgment letter dates. | 100% acknowledged within 5 business days. |
| Grievance resolution timeliness | Review of Grievance Register against resolution/notification dates. | ≥95% resolved within 30 calendar days; 100% with interim updates provided per 6.3.2. |
| Grievance Register completeness | Audit of Register for all required data fields per Appendix A. | 100% of fields completed for each grievance logged. |
| QAPI integration of trend data | Confirmation of quarterly Grievance Trend Report submission to QAPI. | 100% of quarters with completed trend report submitted. |
| Staff non-retaliation compliance | Review of HR records for adverse actions following staff grievance submissions. | Zero substantiated retaliation actions post-grievance. |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Grievances received verbally and not logged. | Untracked concerns; inability to demonstrate compliance with patient rights during survey. | Train all staff to forward all complaints to Grievance Coordinator; document in orientation. |
| Grievances resolved without written notification to complainant. | CMS citation for failure to notify patients of grievance resolution. | Require written resolution notices as a mandatory closing step in the grievance process. |
| Grievance data not integrated into QAPI. | Missed systemic improvement opportunities; passive governance finding. | Use quarterly Grievance Trend Report as standard QAPI agenda item. |
| Staff fear retaliation and do not use the grievance process. | Culture of non-reporting; undetected safety and compliance risks. | Reinforce non-retaliation policy in orientation, annual training, and all-staff communications. |

| CO-HP-007 | Record Retention & Destruction | Retention of grievance records (7 years). |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All staff shall receive training on this policy during new employee orientation, including the grievance intake process, logging requirements, escalation triggers, and non-retaliation obligations.
10.2 Clinical staff shall receive specific training on informing patients and families of their right to file a grievance and providing contact information for the Grievance Coordinator.
10.3 Annual refresher training on grievance handling shall be included in the agency's mandatory training program per policy HR-TD-001.
10.4 All individuals within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of hire or appointment, and within 14 calendar days of any substantive revision, per policy GV-PM-003.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version is valid. Superseded versions shall be archived and marked "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment by all in-scope personnel within 14 calendar days, and index update per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with Governing Body notification at the next regular meeting.
