# GV-EA-001 — Interagency Agreements & Contracts
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-EA-001 |
| Policy Title | Interagency Agreements & Contracts |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | EA — External Affairs |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 1.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the requirements for initiating, reviewing, approving, executing, monitoring, and renewing contracts and interagency agreements between Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. and third-party service providers, subcontractors, referral entities, staffing agencies, vendors, and other external organizations. Effective contract governance protects the agency from legal and regulatory risk, ensures that contracted parties meet applicable compliance standards, and supports the agency's obligations under 42 CFR Part 484 and applicable California state law. This policy ensures that no third-party arrangement compromises patient safety, regulatory compliance, or the agency's legal accountability.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All contracts for services provided to or on behalf of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. by third parties, including but not limited to: clinical staffing agencies, therapy contractors, DME suppliers, billing companies, laboratory services, social work contractors, and IT vendors
All interagency service agreements with hospitals, skilled nursing facilities, physician practices, and community organizations
All Business Associate Agreements (BAAs) as defined under HIPAA (governed jointly with CO-HP-005)
All managed care and payer contracts
The Administrator, Compliance Officer, and any department lead with authority to negotiate or recommend contracts
The Governing Body, with respect to its approval authority over material contracts
This policy does not govern employment contracts for direct employees (see HR domain policies), patient care plans, or physician orders.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall not enter into any contract or interagency agreement for services affecting the agency's operations, patient care, or compliance obligations without written documentation reviewed and approved by the Administrator and, where required by Section 6.2, the Governing Body.
4.2 All contracts shall be reviewed for compliance with applicable federal and state laws prior to execution, including Anti-Kickback Statute requirements (42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7b), Stark Law (42 U.S.C. § 1395nn), HIPAA, and CMS Conditions of Participation.
4.3 All contractors and contracted entities performing services that directly affect patient care or that access PHI shall be screened against the OIG List of Excluded Individuals/Entities (LEIE) and SAM exclusion database prior to engagement and monthly thereafter, per policy HR-TA-003.
4.4 All contracts with entities accessing PHI shall include a valid Business Associate Agreement per policy CO-HP-005.
4.5 All contracts shall include provisions establishing: (a) the scope of services; (b) quality and performance standards; (c) compliance obligations and audit rights; (d) confidentiality and data protection requirements; (e) indemnification and liability allocation; (f) termination rights; and (g) record retention requirements consistent with policy CO-HP-007.
4.6 No individual staff member shall independently execute a contract on behalf of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. without written authorization from the Administrator.
4.7 All active contracts shall be maintained in the agency's Contract Register and reviewed no less than annually to assess performance, compliance, and renewal terms.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Contract | A legally binding written agreement between Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. and one or more external parties that defines the scope of services, obligations, compensation, performance standards, and legal terms governing the relationship. |
| Interagency Agreement | A formal written arrangement between the agency and another healthcare entity (e.g., hospital, SNF, physician practice) defining the terms of clinical cooperation, referral relationships, or shared service delivery. |
| Business Associate Agreement (BAA) | A contract required by HIPAA between the agency and any entity that creates, receives, maintains, or transmits PHI on the agency's behalf. |
| Material Contract | Any contract with an annual value exceeding $25,000, or any contract involving patient care delivery, access to PHI, or regulatory compliance obligations, regardless of value. |
| Contractor | Any individual or entity engaged by the agency under a contract to provide services, excluding direct employees. |
| Contract Register | The centralized log maintained by the Administrator documenting all active, pending, and recently terminated contracts, including key terms and renewal dates. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Contract Initiation and Review

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Initiating Department Lead / Administrator | Submit a Contract Request Form (Appendix A) to the Administrator for any proposed new contract or agreement. The form must identify: (a) the proposed contractor or partner; (b) the scope of services; (c) estimated annual value; (d) whether the contract involves patient care, PHI access, or regulatory compliance obligations; (e) proposed term and renewal provisions. | Prior to initiating any contract negotiation or commitment. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator | Review each proposed contract for: (a) necessity and alignment with agency scope of services; (b) potential Anti-Kickback or Stark Law implications (consult Legal Counsel per GV-EA-003 for referral-related contracts); (c) HIPAA and BAA requirements (coordinate with Compliance Officer per CO-HP-005); (d) OIG/SAM exclusion screening of the proposed contractor per HR-TA-003; (e) adequacy of insurance and liability provisions; (f) record retention and audit rights provisions per CO-HP-007. | Within 10 business days of receiving the Contract Request Form. |
| 6.1.3 | Compliance Officer | Review all contracts involving patient care delivery, PHI access, billing or coding services, or referral relationships for compliance risk. Provide written compliance clearance or identify required modifications before the contract proceeds to execution. | Within 5 business days of referral from Administrator. |
| 6.1.4 | Administrator | Engage Legal Counsel (per policy GV-EA-003) for review of: (a) all material contracts; (b) any contract with unusual risk allocation, indemnification, or liability provisions; (c) any contract with regulatory compliance obligations not clearly defined; (d) any interagency agreement with referral implications. | Prior to execution for material contracts; as needed for others. |

### 6.2 Contract Approval Authority

| Contract Category | Approval Authority | Documentation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Non-material contracts (annual value <$25,000, no patient care/PHI/compliance implications) | Administrator sole approval | Signed contract retained in Contract Register. |
| Material contracts (annual value ≥$25,000, or involving patient care/PHI/regulatory compliance) | Administrator recommendation; Governing Body approval at next regular or special meeting. | Documented in Governing Body minutes; signed contract in Contract Register. |
| Emergency contracts (urgent operational need, cannot wait for next Governing Body meeting) | Administrator executes; Governing Body ratification at next meeting within 30 calendar days. | Written notification to Governing Body Chair within 48 hours; ratification documented in minutes. |
| Interagency agreements with referral source implications | Administrator + Compliance Officer review; Governing Body approval. | Documented in Governing Body minutes; Anti-Kickback review documented in Compliance file. |

### 6.3 Contract Execution

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator | Execute the contract after all required reviews and approvals are complete. No contract shall be executed prior to completion of OIG/SAM screening of the contractor. | Upon completion of all required reviews and approvals. |
| 6.3.2 | Administrator | File a fully executed copy of each contract in the Contract Register and assign a contract ID. Notify the initiating department of execution and provide relevant operational sections to staff who will interface with the contractor. | Within 3 business days of execution. |
| 6.3.3 | Administrator | If the contract involves PHI access, confirm that a fully executed BAA is in place before the contractor accesses any PHI, per policy CO-HP-005. | Prior to any PHI access by the contractor. |
| 6.3.4 | Administrator | Conduct initial OIG/SAM exclusion screening of the contractor before services begin and document the screening result in the Contract Register and the contractor's compliance file. | Before commencement of services; monthly thereafter. |

### 6.4 Contract Monitoring and Performance Oversight

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Responsible Department Lead | Monitor contractor performance on an ongoing basis, including: (a) quality and timeliness of services; (b) compliance with applicable agency policies; (c) staff competency and credential currency (for clinical contractors); (d) incident and complaint rates. | Ongoing; formal performance documentation quarterly. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator | Conduct an annual formal review of all active contracts for: (a) performance against contractual standards; (b) cost and value; (c) compliance with law and agency policy; (d) renewal, modification, or termination decisions. | Annually; within 60 calendar days of each contract's anniversary date. |
| 6.4.3 | Compliance Officer | Conduct or commission an annual audit of a sample of contractor compliance with applicable requirements, including OIG/SAM screening currency, credential verification (for clinical contractors), and BAA compliance. | Annually; results presented at the compliance report to the Governing Body. |
| 6.4.4 | Administrator | If a contractor fails to meet performance standards or is found non-compliant with legal or policy requirements, initiate a formal corrective action process: (a) written notice to the contractor; (b) defined remediation timeline; (c) escalation to contract suspension or termination if not resolved. | Written notice within 5 business days of identified deficiency; remediation timeline per contract terms. |

### 6.5 Contract Renewal and Termination

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator | Maintain a contract renewal calendar (Appendix B) with notification alerts 90 calendar days before each contract's expiration date. | Alerts generated 90 days before expiration. |
| 6.5.2 | Administrator | Review each expiring contract 60 calendar days before expiration to determine: (a) renewal with same terms; (b) renewal with negotiated modifications; (c) competitive replacement; or (d) termination. | 60 calendar days before expiration. |
| 6.5.3 | Administrator | Material contract renewals and terminations shall be reviewed and approved per the approval authority in Section 6.2. | Prior to renewal execution or termination notice. |
| 6.5.4 | Administrator | Upon contract termination: (a) provide notice per contractual terms; (b) ensure return or destruction of PHI per HIPAA and BAA requirements; (c) document the termination in the Contract Register; (d) notify affected staff and arrange service continuity as needed. | Per contractual notice requirements; PHI return/destruction documented within 30 days of termination. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Contractor appears on OIG/SAM exclusion list | Administrator notifies Compliance Officer immediately. | Suspend services pending investigation; notify payers as required; document in Compliance file. | Immediate upon discovery. |
| Contract dispute or threatened litigation | Administrator engages Legal Counsel per GV-EA-003 and notifies Governing Body Chair. | Legal Counsel leads response; Governing Body informed at next meeting or special meeting if urgent. | Within 24 hours of dispute escalation. |
| Material contract executed without Governing Body approval | Administrator documents the circumstances and presents for ratification. | Emergency ratification at next Governing Body meeting; process failure reviewed by Compliance Officer. | Ratification within 30 days of execution. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Contract Register | Centralized log of all active, pending, and recently terminated contracts (Appendix B). | Administrator | Administrative file; accessible for survey and audit review. | Updated within 3 business days of each execution, renewal, or termination; retained per CO-HP-007. |
| Fully executed contracts | Signed original or electronic copy of each executed contract. | Administrator | Contract Register / Contract file. | Filed within 3 business days of execution; retained minimum 7 years after termination. |
| OIG/SAM screening records | Documentation of exclusion screening for each contractor at engagement and monthly. | Compliance Officer | Compliance file; Contract Register. | At engagement; monthly thereafter; retained minimum 7 years. |
| BAA documentation | Executed BAA for each contractor accessing PHI. | Administrator / Compliance Officer | Contract file; HIPAA compliance file. | Executed before PHI access; retained minimum 6 years after BAA termination per HIPAA. |
| Governing Body approval records | Governing Body meeting minutes documenting approval of material contracts. | Designated Secretary | Governance file. | At the meeting; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Annual contract review documentation | Written summary of annual contract performance review and renewal/termination decision. | Administrator | Contract file. | Completed annually within 60 days of anniversary; retained minimum 7 years. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All active contracts documented in Contract Register | Audit of Contract Register against known vendor/contractor relationships. | 100% of active contracts registered. |
| OIG/SAM screening current for all active contractors | Review of monthly screening logs against Contract Register. | 100% of contractors screened at engagement and monthly. |
| BAAs in place for all PHI-accessing contractors | Cross-reference of Contract Register against HIPAA BAA log. | 100% of PHI-accessing contractors have executed BAAs before accessing PHI. |
| Material contracts approved by Governing Body | Review of Governing Body minutes against Contract Register. | 100% of material contracts with documented Governing Body approval. |
| Annual contract reviews completed | Review of contract files for annual review documentation. | 100% of active contracts reviewed annually within 60 days of anniversary. |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PHI-accessing vendor engaged without BAA. | HIPAA breach; regulatory citation; OIG investigation risk. | BAA checklist in Contract Request Form; Compliance Officer review required before PHI access. |
| Excluded contractor not identified before services begin. | False Claims Act exposure; Medicare exclusion violation. | Pre-engagement OIG/SAM screening mandatory per Section 6.3.4. |
| Material contracts not presented to Governing Body. | Governing Body fails oversight obligation; undocumented financial and legal risk. | Contract approval matrix enforced; Administrator accountable for routing per Section 6.2. |
| Contracts auto-renew without review. | Unfavorable terms perpetuated; performance deficiencies unaddressed. | 90-day renewal alert system per Section 6.5.1. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 The Administrator and all department leads with contract initiation authority shall receive training on this policy, including contract review requirements, approval authority, and Anti-Kickback/Stark Law compliance fundamentals, upon appointment and annually.
10.2 All individuals within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of hire, appointment, or policy effective date, per policy GV-PM-003.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained per policy EN-LC-001. 11.2 Only the most current approved version is valid for any operational or compliance purpose. 11.3 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment by all in-scope personnel within 14 calendar days, and index update per EN-TG-001. 11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with Governing Body notification at the next regular meeting.

# GV-EA-002 — Community Liaison & Public Relations
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-EA-002 |
| Policy Title | Community Liaison & Public Relations |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | EA — External Affairs |
| Classification Tier | GOOD TO HAVE |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Biennial |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 1.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2027-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy defines the approach of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to community engagement, referral source relationship management, and public communications. Effective community liaison and public relations activities support the agency's mission by building trust within the communities it serves, developing ethical referral relationships, and maintaining a positive public reputation consistent with the agency's compliance obligations. This policy ensures that all community and public relations activities comply with the Anti-Kickback Statute, Stark Law, CMS marketing guidelines, and applicable California state advertising standards.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Administrator and all staff engaged in external marketing, community outreach, or referral development activities
Clinical staff who interact with referral sources, discharge planners, or community partners in their professional capacity
Contracted marketing personnel or agencies retained by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
All Governing Body members engaged in public representation of the agency
Any communications, events, materials, or activities conducted in the name of or on behalf of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall engage with the community, referral sources, and the public in a manner that is truthful, ethical, compliant with applicable law, and consistent with the agency's mission of providing high-quality home health services.
4.2 All marketing materials, public communications, and community outreach activities representing the agency shall be reviewed and approved by the Administrator prior to distribution or publication. Materials shall not contain false, misleading, or unsubstantiated claims about the agency's services, quality outcomes, or credentials.
4.3 No remuneration — including gifts, meals, entertainment, payments, or any item of value — shall be provided to referral sources, physicians, discharge planners, or their staff in exchange for, or to induce or reward, referrals to the agency. All interactions with referral sources shall comply with the Anti-Kickback Statute (42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7b) and Stark Law (42 U.S.C. § 1395nn), as governed by policy CO-FA-001.
4.4 The agency may provide nominal educational materials, agency informational materials, or de minimis items of no more than $15 in value per instance and $75 per year per source (consistent with OIG guidance), provided these are not conditioned on referrals and are offered uniformly.
4.5 The Administrator is the sole authorized spokesperson for Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. in communications with media, regulatory bodies, and the general public, unless the Administrator expressly delegates this authority in writing per policy GV-PM-004.
4.6 All social media activities conducted in the name of the agency shall comply with policy IT-UP-003. Staff shall not make public statements about the agency's operations, clinical outcomes, or patients on personal or professional social media accounts in a manner that could identify patients, disclose PHI, or misrepresent the agency.
4.7 The agency shall participate in community health initiatives, local coalitions, and professional associations in a manner that advances patient access, health literacy, and the visibility of home health services, without compromising the agency's compliance obligations.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Community Liaison | Activities directed at building relationships with community organizations, healthcare entities, and the populations served by the agency, for the purpose of raising awareness of the agency's services and supporting patient access. |
| Referral Source | Any individual or entity in a position to refer patients to the agency, including physicians, hospitals, skilled nursing facilities, discharge planners, case managers, and insurance case coordinators. |
| Marketing Material | Any printed, digital, or verbal communication designed to promote the agency's services to potential patients, families, or referral sources. |
| Public Relations | Activities designed to build and maintain a favorable public image of the agency and its services, including media relations, community events, social media presence, and professional association participation. |
| Nominal Gift | An item of value provided to a referral source or their staff that does not exceed $15 per instance or $75 per year per source and is not conditioned on referrals. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Marketing and Outreach Material Development and Approval

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Marketing Staff / Administrator | Develop marketing materials and community outreach content that: (a) accurately represent the agency's services, licensure, and credentials; (b) do not make unsubstantiated quality or outcome claims; (c) include the agency's HCAI license number (406412878) and contact information as required by California state law; (d) comply with CMS guidelines for home health marketing; (e) are reviewed for Anti-Kickback compliance. | During development, prior to finalization. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator | Review and approve all marketing materials, press releases, event announcements, and public-facing communications before distribution. Maintain copies of all approved materials with version date. | Prior to distribution; copies retained minimum 3 years. |
| 6.1.3 | Compliance Officer | Review any materials that could implicate Anti-Kickback or Stark Law compliance (e.g., materials referencing relationships with specific physicians or facilities), or that involve claims about clinical outcomes or Medicare/Medicaid compliance. | Within 5 business days of referral from Administrator. |

### 6.2 Referral Source Relationship Management

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Marketing Staff / Clinical Liaison | Conduct all referral source outreach activities in compliance with the Anti-Kickback Statute and the agency's Code of Conduct (CO-CP-004). Outreach activities shall be educational and informational in nature. | At all times. |
| 6.2.2 | Marketing Staff / Clinical Liaison | Document all referral source contacts, including date, entity, representative, and purpose, in the agency's referral source contact log. | Within 1 business day of each contact. |
| 6.2.3 | Administrator / Compliance Officer | Review the referral source contact log quarterly to identify any patterns suggesting potential Anti-Kickback risk (e.g., disproportionate concentration of referrals from a single source following a marketing expenditure). | Quarterly. |
| 6.2.4 | All Staff | Report any request by a referral source for remuneration in exchange for referrals to the Compliance Officer immediately, per policy CO-FA-001. | Immediately upon discovery. |

### 6.3 Community Engagement Activities

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator / Marketing Staff | Identify and participate in community health fairs, senior services events, faith community outreach, and professional association events in the agency's service area. | As opportunities arise; at least 2 community engagement activities per calendar year. |
| 6.3.2 | Administrator | Evaluate and document the impact of community engagement activities on patient access, community awareness, and referral relationships. | Following each community event; annually as part of strategic plan review per GV-OG-004. |
| 6.3.3 | Administrator | Maintain relationships with community organizations that serve the agency's patient populations, including senior centers, culturally specific community organizations, and patient advocacy groups. | Ongoing. |

### 6.4 Media and Crisis Communications

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator | Serve as or designate the sole agency spokesperson for media inquiries. All media contacts shall be coordinated through the Administrator. | At all times. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator | In the event of a media inquiry involving a patient care incident, regulatory action, or adverse event: (a) immediately consult the Compliance Officer and Legal Counsel (per GV-EA-003); (b) prepare a reviewed written statement; (c) do not speculate, reference specific patients (PHI), or comment on pending investigations. | Before responding to any media inquiry involving sensitive operational matters. |
| 6.4.3 | Administrator | Notify the Governing Body Chair of any media inquiry or coverage with potential reputational or regulatory implications. | Within 24 hours of the inquiry or publication. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Approved marketing materials | Copies of all approved materials with version date and approval signature. | Administrator | Marketing file. | Retained minimum 3 years from last active use. |
| Referral source contact log | Log of all referral source outreach activities with date, entity, purpose, and representative contacted. | Marketing Staff / Administrator | Administrative file. | Updated within 1 business day of contact; retained minimum 3 years. |
| Community engagement activity records | Documentation of each community event including date, location, participants, and outcome notes. | Administrator | Administrative file. | Within 5 business days of event; retained minimum 3 years. |
| Media response records | Written statements, interview notes, and communications related to media inquiries. | Administrator | Administrative file; Compliance file if regulatory implications. | Retained minimum 7 years for sensitive matters; 3 years for routine. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Marketing materials reviewed and approved before distribution | Audit of marketing files for Administrator approval documentation. | 100% of distributed materials with documented prior approval. |
| Referral source contact log maintained | Review of log currency and completeness. | Log updated within 1 business day for all contacts; no unexplained gaps. |
| Anti-Kickback compliance in referral activities | Quarterly review by Compliance Officer; staff report rate for AKS concerns. | Zero substantiated AKS violations; all concerns reported immediately. |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Marketing materials contain unsubstantiated quality claims. | False advertising violation; reputational and regulatory risk. | Administrator review and approval process per Section 6.1.2. |
| Staff provide gifts or entertainment to referral sources without AKS review. | Anti-Kickback violation; OIG investigation risk. | Training on CO-FA-001; zero-tolerance policy reinforced in Code of Conduct. |
| Staff respond independently to media inquiries. | Unauthorized statements; PHI disclosure risk; reputational harm. | All-staff communication training per GV-PM-004; media inquiry escalation protocol. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 All staff engaged in marketing, community liaison, or referral source outreach shall receive training on this policy and CO-FA-001 (Anti-Kickback compliance) upon assignment to these functions and annually thereafter.
10.2 All individuals within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of hire or appointment, and within 14 calendar days of any substantive revision, per policy GV-PM-003.
## 11. Version Control

# GV-EA-003 — Legal Counsel Engagement & Oversight
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-EA-003 |
| Policy Title | Legal Counsel Engagement & Oversight |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | EA — External Affairs |
| Classification Tier | RECOMMENDED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Biennial |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 1.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2027-07-10 |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the requirements and standards for engaging, managing, and overseeing legal counsel retained by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Legal counsel plays a critical role in protecting the agency's legal interests, ensuring regulatory compliance, managing litigation risk, and supporting governance decision-making. This policy ensures that legal resources are engaged appropriately, that attorney-client privilege is preserved, that legal expenditures are authorized, and that the Governing Body receives timely notification of material legal matters.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Administrator and all senior leadership who may identify needs for legal counsel
The Compliance Officer, with respect to legal matters arising from compliance investigations
The Governing Body, with respect to Tier 4 — Privileged legal matters requiring board-level oversight
All external legal counsel retained by the agency
All agency staff who may be contacted by legal counsel or asked to provide information in connection with legal matters
This policy applies to all types of legal matters, including regulatory compliance, contract review, employment law, litigation, government investigations, HIPAA matters, and corporate governance.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall retain qualified legal counsel with expertise in healthcare regulatory law, home health agency operations, and California state law to advise the agency on legal matters, as needed.
4.2 The Administrator is the primary point of contact for engaging and directing external legal counsel, except in matters where the Governing Body exercises direct legal oversight (Tier 4 matters). The Compliance Officer shall be involved in all legal matters with compliance implications.
4.3 No staff member shall engage legal counsel on behalf of the agency, execute a legal retainer agreement, or authorize legal fees without written authorization from the Administrator.
4.4 Attorney-client privilege attaches to communications between the agency's authorized representatives and legal counsel made for the purpose of obtaining or providing legal advice. All personnel shall take steps to protect the confidentiality of privileged communications and shall not disclose them without authorization from the Administrator or Legal Counsel.
4.5 The Governing Body shall be notified of any material legal matter, defined as any matter that could result in a financial exposure exceeding $10,000, regulatory action, exclusion from Medicare or Medicaid, or reputational harm to the agency. Notification shall occur at the earliest practicable time, not to exceed 5 business days from the Administrator's awareness.
4.6 All legal matters and associated costs shall be documented and tracked in the agency's Legal Matter Register. The Administrator shall report legal matter status and associated costs to the Governing Body at each quarterly meeting.
4.7 Legal counsel retained for compliance investigations operates under direction of the Compliance Officer, with the Administrator's authorization, to preserve privilege and independence.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Legal Counsel | Any attorney or law firm engaged by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to provide legal advice, representation, or services in connection with the agency's operations. |
| Attorney-Client Privilege | The legal principle protecting confidential communications between an attorney and their client made for the purpose of obtaining or providing legal advice from disclosure in legal proceedings. |
| Retainer Agreement | A formal written agreement between the agency and external legal counsel establishing the scope of legal services, fee arrangements, and terms of engagement. |
| Material Legal Matter | Any legal matter with potential financial exposure exceeding $10,000, or involving regulatory action, government investigation, exclusion risk, or significant reputational consequence. |
| Legal Matter Register | The centralized log maintained by the Administrator documenting all active and recently closed legal matters, including nature, assigned counsel, status, and estimated or actual cost. |
| Privileged Communication | Any communication between agency personnel and legal counsel made in the context of obtaining legal advice, protected from disclosure by attorney-client privilege. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Identifying and Engaging Legal Counsel

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Administrator / Compliance Officer | Identify the need for legal counsel when the agency faces: (a) regulatory citations or enforcement actions; (b) CMS or state agency surveys with potential adverse findings; (c) material contracts requiring legal review; (d) employment disputes or potential litigation; (e) HIPAA breach investigations; (f) OIG or DOJ inquiries; (g) Anti-Kickback or Stark Law questions; (h) change of ownership or corporate restructuring; (i) any situation where legal risk is unclear. | As matters arise; engage proactively for matters with known legal implications. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator | Select legal counsel with demonstrated expertise in the subject matter. Preferred qualifications include: (a) home health or post-acute care regulatory experience; (b) Medicare/Medicaid compliance expertise; (c) California state healthcare law familiarity; (d) healthcare litigation experience (for matters involving litigation risk). | Prior to engagement; qualifications documented in Legal Matter Register. |
| 6.1.3 | Administrator | Execute a written retainer or engagement letter with selected legal counsel defining: (a) scope of services; (b) billing rates and fee structure; (c) communication protocols; (d) reporting obligations to the Administrator and Governing Body; (e) confidentiality provisions. | Prior to commencement of legal services. |
| 6.1.4 | Administrator | Enter the new legal matter in the Legal Matter Register (Appendix A) within 2 business days of engaging counsel. | Within 2 business days of engagement. |

### 6.2 Managing Legal Matters

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Administrator | Serve as the primary liaison between legal counsel and the agency. Coordinate document requests, staff interviews, and information provision to legal counsel, ensuring that: (a) only authorized personnel communicate with external counsel; (b) privileged communications are protected from inadvertent disclosure; (c) legal hold obligations (preservation of records) are implemented when litigation is reasonably anticipated. | Ongoing for each active legal matter. |
| 6.2.2 | Compliance Officer | Participate in all legal matters with compliance implications and maintain a parallel compliance investigation record per policy CO-CP-007 where applicable. | Ongoing for compliance-related legal matters. |
| 6.2.3 | Administrator | Provide regular status updates on active legal matters to the Governing Body: (a) routine matters — reported at quarterly meetings; (b) material matters — reported within 5 business days of Administrator awareness, with a special written notification to the Governing Body Chair if a regular quarterly meeting is not imminent. | Quarterly for routine matters; within 5 business days for material matters. |
| 6.2.4 | Administrator | Review legal invoices for accuracy and reasonableness before authorization for payment. Question any unexplained fees or scope expansions with legal counsel prior to payment. | Upon receipt of each invoice; authorization before payment due date. |
| 6.2.5 | Administrator | For matters involving government investigations (OIG, DOJ, CMS, HCAI), immediately notify the Governing Body Chair and engage legal counsel before providing any response to the investigating authority. | Immediately upon receipt of any government investigation communication. |

### 6.3 Preserving Attorney-Client Privilege

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator | Brief all personnel involved in a legal matter on the importance of attorney-client privilege, including: (a) what constitutes a privileged communication; (b) the prohibition on disclosing privileged communications to third parties; (c) the requirement to route all communications with legal counsel through the Administrator. | At the initiation of each legal matter involving staff. |
| 6.3.2 | All Personnel | Do not voluntarily disclose any communication with agency legal counsel to regulatory surveyors, payers, referral sources, media, or other third parties without explicit authorization from the Administrator or Legal Counsel. | At all times. |
| 6.3.3 | Administrator | Label all written communications with legal counsel as "PRIVILEGED AND CONFIDENTIAL — ATTORNEY-CLIENT COMMUNICATION" and store in a restricted-access file. | For all privileged correspondence. |

### 6.4 Legal Holds and Document Preservation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator / Legal Counsel | Issue a Legal Hold Notice (Appendix B) to all personnel with custody of potentially relevant records when litigation is reasonably anticipated or a government investigation has been initiated. The Legal Hold Notice suspends routine record retention and destruction schedules for affected records. | Immediately upon identification of litigation or investigation risk. |
| 6.4.2 | All Personnel Receiving Legal Hold Notice | Comply with the Legal Hold Notice by preserving all identified records in their current form and not altering, deleting, or destroying them until the hold is formally released by the Administrator or Legal Counsel. | Immediately upon receipt and until hold release. |
| 6.4.3 | Administrator | Release the Legal Hold Notice in writing when the relevant matter is resolved, and resume normal record retention and destruction practices per policy CO-HP-007. | Upon written confirmation from Legal Counsel that preservation is no longer required. |

### 6.5 Governing Body Engagement with Legal Counsel

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator | Present a summary of all active legal matters, associated costs, and status updates to the Governing Body at each quarterly meeting in executive session. | Quarterly; in executive session to preserve privilege. |
| 6.5.2 | Governing Body Chair | Authorize engagement of Legal Counsel for Tier 4 — Privileged matters involving Governing Body governance, individual member conduct, or corporate authority disputes, independent of the Administrator. | As needed; Governing Body Chair may retain separate counsel on behalf of the Governing Body. |
| 6.5.3 | Governing Body | Approve significant legal settlements or decisions with financial exposure exceeding the Administrator's delegated authority as defined in policy GV-OG-005. | At a regular or special Governing Body meeting; documented in executive session minutes. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Legal Matter Register | Centralized log of all active and recently closed legal matters (Appendix A). | Administrator | Restricted administrative file (Tier 4 access). | Updated within 2 business days of new matter or status change; retained minimum 10 years. |
| Retainer/Engagement Agreements | Executed retainer or engagement letters with all retained legal counsel. | Administrator | Legal file; restricted administrative file. | Executed before services begin; retained minimum 10 years after matter closure. |
| Legal Hold Notices | Written legal hold notices issued to personnel and acknowledgment records. | Administrator | Legal file; personnel compliance records. | Issued immediately; retained for duration of matter plus 3 years. |
| Privileged correspondence | All communications with legal counsel labeled and stored in restricted-access file. | Administrator | Restricted file; labeled PRIVILEGED AND CONFIDENTIAL. | Retained per Legal Counsel's instruction; minimum 10 years for material matters. |
| Governing Body legal matter reports | Executive session documentation of legal matter summaries. | Designated Secretary | Governance file (Tier 4 access). | Quarterly; retained minimum 7 years. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All material legal matters reported to Governing Body within 5 business days | Review of Legal Matter Register against Governing Body notification records. | 100% of material matters notified within 5 business days. |
| Retainer agreements in place before legal services commence | Review of Legal Matter Register and retainer file. | 100% of engaged counsel with executed retainer or engagement letter before services begin. |
| Legal holds implemented when litigation or investigation reasonably anticipated | Review of Legal Hold Notices in Legal Matter Register. | Legal Hold Notice issued for 100% of matters with litigation or investigation exposure. |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Staff communicate directly with government investigators without counsel. | Unauthorized disclosures; privilege waiver; regulatory exposure. | Train all staff to route government contacts immediately to Administrator; document in all-staff communications. |
| Legal matters not reported to Governing Body timely. | Governing Body unable to exercise oversight; fiscal and governance risk. | Administrator notification protocol per Section 6.2.3; Legal Matter Register review at each Governing Body meeting. |
| Privileged documents disclosed to surveyors or other parties. | Waiver of attorney-client privilege; legal strategy exposure. | Privilege labeling requirement per Section 6.3.3; all-staff training on privilege preservation. |

## 9. References
### 9.1 Legal Standards

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 The Administrator, Compliance Officer, and all senior leadership shall receive training on this policy including privilege preservation, legal hold obligations, and the prohibition on staff-initiated government communications, upon appointment and biennially thereafter.
10.2 All individuals within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of hire, appointment, or the policy's effective date, per policy GV-PM-003.

# GV-EA-004 — Agency Licensure & Certification Maintenance
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-EA-004 |
| Policy Title | Agency Licensure & Certification Maintenance |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the requirements for maintaining continuous, uninterrupted licensure, Medicare certification, Medicaid enrollment, and any applicable accreditation credentials for Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The agency's legal authority to operate and to receive reimbursement from federal and state healthcare programs depends entirely on the currency and validity of these credentials. A lapse in any required license or certification — even for a single day — may result in termination of the agency's Medicare provider agreement, loss of reimbursement, state enforcement action, inability to admit new patients, and potential Condition-level deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.100. This policy ensures that all required credentials are tracked, renewed timely, and maintained in a state of continuous validity, and that the Governing Body exercises its oversight responsibility to confirm the agency's credential status at each quarterly meeting as required by policy GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.1.2.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. (oversight and quarterly verification)
The Agency Administrator (primary accountability for credential maintenance)
The Compliance Officer (regulatory monitoring and compliance verification)
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (clinical certification and accreditation support)
Any administrative staff assigned responsibility for license renewal processing, application submission, or credential file maintenance
This policy governs the following agency-level credentials:
California Home Health Agency License issued by the California Department of Health Care Access and Information (HCAI) — License No. 406412878
Medicare Certification (CMS Certification Number / CCN) issued by the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services
Medicaid (Medi-Cal) Provider Enrollment, if applicable
National accreditation, if applicable (e.g., ACHC, CHAP, Joint Commission)
Any additional state or local permits, registrations, or authorizations required for the agency to operate lawfully within its defined service area
This policy does not govern individual employee licensure or certification (see HR-TA-004) or physician credentialing. It applies exclusively to agency-level credentials.
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain current, valid, and uninterrupted licensure, certification, and enrollment with all applicable federal, state, and local regulatory authorities at all times as a condition of its legal authority to operate and to participate in federal and state healthcare programs.
4.2 The Administrator is directly accountable to the Governing Body for the maintenance and timely renewal of all agency credentials. No credential shall be allowed to lapse, expire, or become inactive without immediate corrective action and Governing Body notification.
4.3 The Administrator shall maintain a centralized Agency Credential Register (Appendix A) that documents every required agency-level credential, including the issuing authority, credential number, effective date, expiration date, renewal deadline, and current status.
4.4 Renewal applications for all credentials shall be submitted no later than 90 calendar days before the expiration date of each credential, unless the issuing authority specifies a different renewal window, in which case the issuing authority's timeline shall be followed.
4.5 The Administrator shall verify the agency's credential status at each quarterly Governing Body meeting and present documented confirmation that all credentials are current and valid, per policy GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.1.2.
4.6 The Compliance Officer shall independently monitor regulatory changes, licensing requirements, and certification standards that may affect the agency's credential obligations and shall notify the Administrator within 7 calendar days of identifying any new or changed requirement.
4.7 Any communication from a licensing, certification, or accreditation body — including routine correspondence, deficiency notifications, survey announcements, enforcement actions, or requests for information — shall be immediately forwarded to the Administrator and Compliance Officer and responded to within the timeframes specified by the issuing authority.
4.8 The Governing Body shall be notified immediately — within 24 hours — of any event that threatens the continuity of any agency credential, including but not limited to: (a) a credential approaching expiration without a submitted renewal; (b) a denial of renewal application; (c) a regulatory enforcement action threatening suspension or revocation; (d) a change in regulatory requirements affecting eligibility; (e) a state survey resulting in an Immediate Jeopardy finding.
4.9 All credential documents, renewal applications, correspondence with regulatory bodies, and survey-related records shall be maintained in the agency's credential file and retained for a minimum of 7 years per policy CO-HP-007.
4.10 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid. Any revision requires re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Agency Credential | Any license, certification, enrollment, accreditation, permit, or registration required by a federal, state, or local authority for the agency to operate lawfully and participate in healthcare programs. |
| California Home Health Agency License | The license issued by the California Department of Health Care Access and Information (HCAI) authorizing the agency to provide home health services in California. |
| Medicare Certification | The certification issued by CMS following a state survey agency's determination that the agency meets the Medicare Conditions of Participation (42 CFR Part 484), authorizing the agency to participate in the Medicare program and receive Medicare reimbursement. |
| CMS Certification Number (CCN) | The unique identification number assigned by CMS to the agency upon initial Medicare certification, used for all Medicare billing and regulatory communications. |
| Medi-Cal Provider Enrollment | The enrollment of the agency as an approved provider in California's Medicaid program (Medi-Cal), enabling reimbursement for eligible Medi-Cal beneficiaries. |
| Accreditation | Voluntary or required certification by a national accrediting organization (e.g., ACHC, CHAP, Joint Commission) that the agency meets defined quality and operational standards. Accreditation may serve as deemed status for Medicare certification purposes. |
| Renewal | The process of applying for and obtaining a new term for an existing credential before the current term expires. |
| Lapse | The expiration of a credential without a valid renewal in place, resulting in loss of the agency's legal authority to operate or to participate in healthcare programs during the period of the lapse. |
| Deemed Status | The recognition by CMS that accreditation by a CMS-approved accrediting organization satisfies the Medicare Conditions of Participation, exempting the agency from routine CMS state agency surveys. |
| Agency Credential Register | The centralized tracking document maintained by the Administrator listing all required agency credentials with their status, expiration dates, and renewal deadlines. |
| Credential File | The secure physical or electronic file containing all credential documents, renewal applications, correspondence, and survey records for each agency credential. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Agency Credential Register Establishment and Maintenance

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Administrator | Establish and maintain the Agency Credential Register (Appendix A) documenting every required agency-level credential. The Register shall include for each credential: (a) credential name; (b) issuing authority; (c) credential number; (d) original issue date; (e) current effective date; (f) expiration date; (g) renewal application deadline (90 calendar days before expiration unless otherwise specified); (h) renewal application submitted date; (i) renewal confirmation received date; (j) current status (Active / Pending Renewal / Expired / Suspended / Revoked). | Established prior to initial operations; maintained continuously; updated within 3 calendar days of any status change. |
| 6.1.2 | Administrator | Review the Agency Credential Register monthly to identify any credential with a renewal deadline within the upcoming 120 calendar days. Flag these credentials for immediate renewal processing. | Monthly; first business day of each month. |
| 6.1.3 | Administrator | Present the Agency Credential Register to the Governing Body at each quarterly meeting as part of the credential verification report required by GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.1.2. The presentation must confirm: (a) all credentials are current and valid; (b) any upcoming renewals and their status; (c) any regulatory correspondence received since the last meeting. | At each quarterly Governing Body meeting. |

### 6.2 California Home Health Agency License Maintenance

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Administrator | Maintain the agency's California home health agency license (HCAI License No. 406412878) in continuous active status. Monitor HCAI requirements for any changes to licensure standards, renewal procedures, or reporting obligations. | Continuous. |
| 6.2.2 | Administrator | Submit the license renewal application to HCAI no later than 90 calendar days before the license expiration date, including all required documentation, fees, and attestations. Retain a complete copy of the renewal application in the credential file. | No later than 90 calendar days before expiration. |
| 6.2.3 | Administrator | Track the renewal application status with HCAI. If no acknowledgment of receipt is received from HCAI within 30 calendar days of submission, contact HCAI directly to confirm receipt and processing status. Document all follow-up contacts. | Follow-up at 30 calendar days post-submission if no acknowledgment received. |
| 6.2.4 | Administrator | Upon receipt of the renewed license, verify accuracy of all information (agency name, address, license number, effective dates), file the renewed license in the credential file, update the Agency Credential Register, and post the current license in the agency's principal office as required by California law. | Within 3 calendar days of receipt of renewed license. |
| 6.2.5 | Administrator | Report to HCAI any changes that require notification under California licensure requirements, including but not limited to: (a) change of agency name; (b) change of agency address (principal office or branch offices); (c) change of administrator; (d) change of ownership; (e) addition or closure of branch offices; (f) material changes to services offered. Reports shall be submitted within the timeframes mandated by California law. | Per California statutory requirements; typically within 30 calendar days of the change. |
| 6.2.6 | Compliance Officer | Monitor California legislative and regulatory changes affecting home health agency licensure requirements. Notify the Administrator within 7 calendar days of identifying any new or changed licensure requirement. | Ongoing; notification within 7 calendar days of identification. |

### 6.3 Medicare Certification Maintenance

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator | Maintain the agency's Medicare certification in continuous active status by ensuring ongoing compliance with all CMS Conditions of Participation (42 CFR Part 484). Medicare certification does not have a fixed renewal date; it continues as long as the agency remains in compliance and is not terminated by CMS. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.2 | Administrator / Compliance Officer | Maintain continuous survey readiness per policy CO-RA-003. Ensure that all clinical, operational, administrative, and governance systems are maintained in a state of compliance that would satisfy an unannounced CMS state agency survey at any time. | Continuous. |
| 6.3.3 | Administrator | Respond to all CMS and state survey agency communications within the timeframes specified. For survey notifications, immediately implement the agency's survey readiness protocol: (a) notify the Compliance Officer and Director of Nursing; (b) assemble the survey readiness team; (c) confirm availability of all requested documents; (d) designate the survey escort and point of contact per GV-PM-004, Section 6.2.4.4. | Immediately upon receipt of any CMS or state survey agency communication. |
| 6.3.4 | Administrator | Following any CMS survey resulting in cited deficiencies, develop and submit a Plan of Correction (POC) within the CMS-required timeframe (typically 10 calendar days from receipt of the Statement of Deficiencies). The POC shall be reviewed by the Compliance Officer and Director of Nursing before submission. | Within CMS-required timeframe; typically 10 calendar days from receipt of Statement of Deficiencies. |
| 6.3.5 | Administrator | Notify the Governing Body Chair within 24 hours of receiving any CMS survey findings, particularly Condition-level deficiencies or Immediate Jeopardy determinations. Convene a special Governing Body meeting per GV-GB-001, Section 6.5 if findings require Governing Body-level action. | Within 24 hours of receiving survey findings. |
| 6.3.6 | Administrator | Report to the CMS Medicare Administrative Contractor (MAC) any changes affecting Medicare enrollment, including: (a) change of agency address; (b) change of ownership (CHOW); (c) change of administrator or medical director; (d) addition or termination of practice locations. Use the CMS-855A form or applicable electronic submission method. | Within 30 calendar days of the change, or within the timeframe specified by CMS for the specific change type. |
| 6.3.7 | Compliance Officer | Monitor CMS regulations, State Operations Manual updates, Survey and Certification memoranda, and OASIS program changes for any modifications to Conditions of Participation or certification requirements. Notify the Administrator and Director of Nursing within 7 calendar days of identifying any change. | Ongoing; notification within 7 calendar days. |

### 6.4 Medi-Cal Provider Enrollment Maintenance

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Administrator | If the agency is enrolled as a Medi-Cal provider, maintain the enrollment in active status by complying with all California Department of Health Care Services (DHCS) enrollment requirements. | Continuous, if applicable. |
| 6.4.2 | Administrator | Submit Medi-Cal re-enrollment or revalidation applications within the timeframes specified by DHCS. Track submission and processing status. | Per DHCS-specified timelines; no later than 60 calendar days before the revalidation deadline. |
| 6.4.3 | Administrator | Report any changes affecting Medi-Cal enrollment to DHCS within the required timeframes, including changes to agency name, address, ownership, administrator, or service area. | Per DHCS requirements; typically within 35 calendar days of the change. |

### 6.5 Accreditation Maintenance (If Applicable)

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator | If the agency holds national accreditation (ACHC, CHAP, Joint Commission, or other CMS-approved accrediting organization), maintain accreditation in active status by complying with all accrediting organization standards and requirements. | Continuous, if applicable. |
| 6.5.2 | Administrator | Submit accreditation renewal applications within the timeframes specified by the accrediting organization, typically 6–12 months before accreditation expiration. | Per accrediting organization timeline. |
| 6.5.3 | Administrator | Prepare for and facilitate accreditation surveys, following the same readiness protocols applied to CMS surveys per Section 6.3.3 and policy CO-RA-003. | As surveys are scheduled or announced. |
| 6.5.4 | Administrator | Report the status of accreditation to the Governing Body at each quarterly meeting, including: (a) current accreditation status and expiration date; (b) any survey findings or corrective action requirements; (c) renewal application status. | Quarterly. |
| 6.5.5 | Administrator | Notify the Governing Body Chair within 24 hours of any accreditation survey finding that could jeopardize the agency's accreditation status or deemed status with CMS. | Within 24 hours. |

### 6.6 Renewal Application Quality Control

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | Administrator | Before submitting any renewal application, conduct a quality review to verify: (a) all required fields are completed; (b) all required supporting documentation is attached; (c) all fees are calculated correctly and payment is included; (d) the agency's name, address, license numbers, and other identifying information are accurate and consistent across all documents; (e) any attestations are signed by the appropriate authorized individual. | Before each renewal submission. |
| 6.6.2 | Administrator | Maintain a pre-submission checklist (Appendix B) for each credential type to ensure consistent and complete renewal submissions. | Used for each renewal application. |
| 6.6.3 | Administrator | Retain a complete copy of every renewal application submitted, including all supporting documents, payment confirmation, and proof of submission (certified mail receipt, electronic confirmation, or hand-delivery acknowledgment). | At each submission; retained in credential file for minimum 7 years. |

### 6.7 Credential Display and Accessibility

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.7.1 | Administrator | Display the current California home health agency license in the agency's principal office in a location accessible to public view, as required by California law. | At all times; updated within 3 calendar days of receiving a renewed license. |
| 6.7.2 | Administrator | Ensure that copies of all current credentials are readily accessible for CMS survey review and can be produced within 1 business day of any regulatory or survey request. | At all times. |
| 6.7.3 | Administrator | Include the agency's HCAI license number (406412878) on all marketing materials, public-facing documents, and correspondence where required by California law and agency policy GV-EA-002. | At all times. |

### 6.8 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A renewal application has not been submitted by the 90-day deadline. | Compliance Officer notifies the Administrator in writing and the Governing Body Chair. | Administrator submits the renewal application immediately. If the credential is at risk of lapse, engage legal counsel per GV-EA-003 and the issuing authority to explore expedited processing. Governing Body notified at the next meeting or via special notification. | Renewal submitted within 5 calendar days of notification; Governing Body notified within 24 hours if lapse risk exists. |
| A credential expires before the renewal is processed (lapse occurs). | Administrator notifies the Governing Body Chair and Compliance Officer immediately. | Administrator contacts the issuing authority immediately to determine: (a) whether the agency may continue to operate under a grace period; (b) what is required to reinstate the credential. Cease admissions of new patients if required by the issuing authority until reinstatement. Notify the MAC if Medicare certification is affected. Governing Body convenes a special meeting within 48 hours. | Immediate notification; special Governing Body meeting within 48 hours; reinstatement pursued on an emergency basis. |
| A renewal application is denied by the issuing authority. | Administrator notifies the Governing Body Chair, Compliance Officer, and Legal Counsel immediately. | Legal Counsel reviews the denial and advises on appeal rights and remediation. Administrator prepares an appeal or corrective submission per the issuing authority's requirements. Governing Body informed within 24 hours and directs response. | Within 24 hours of denial notification; appeal filed within the issuing authority's appeal deadline. |
| CMS issues an Immediate Jeopardy finding that threatens Medicare certification. | Administrator notifies the Governing Body Chair, Compliance Officer, and Director of Nursing immediately. | Implement the Immediate Jeopardy corrective action plan per CMS requirements (typically 23 calendar days). Governing Body convenes a special meeting within 48 hours. Director of Nursing implements clinical corrective measures immediately. Daily status updates provided to Governing Body Chair until jeopardy is removed. | Corrective action per CMS timeline; special Governing Body meeting within 48 hours; daily updates until resolution. |
| HCAI issues a license suspension or revocation notice. | Administrator notifies the Governing Body Chair, Compliance Officer, and Legal Counsel immediately. | Legal Counsel files any available appeal or stay of action within the required timeframe. Administrator implements the patient transition plan (per GV-EA-005 and CL-CP-007) if license suspension or revocation is imminent. Governing Body convenes an emergency meeting. | Within 24 hours; emergency Governing Body meeting within 48 hours. |
| An accrediting organization issues a notice of adverse accreditation action (probation, denial, revocation). | Administrator notifies the Governing Body Chair within 24 hours. | Administrator and Compliance Officer assess the impact on deemed status and Medicare certification. If deemed status is lost, notify the CMS state survey agency and prepare for a CMS standard survey. Legal Counsel engaged to advise on appeal options. | Within 24 hours of notice; CMS notification per applicable requirements; Governing Body informed at next meeting or special meeting. |

| Survey records | All survey-related documents including survey notifications, Statements of Deficiencies, Plans of Correction, and survey follow-up documentation. | Administrator / Compliance Officer | Credential file; Compliance file; Governing Body governance file. | Filed within 3 calendar days of receipt; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Governing Body credential verification | Documentation in quarterly Governing Body meeting minutes confirming credential status review per GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.1.2. | Designated Secretary | Governing Body governance file. | At each quarterly meeting; retained minimum 7 years as part of meeting minutes. |
| Pre-submission checklist | Completed renewal pre-submission checklist for each credential renewal (Appendix B). | Administrator | Credential file. | Completed before each renewal submission; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy by all personnel within scope (Appendix C). | Each individual (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of policy effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All required agency credentials are current and valid. | Review of Agency Credential Register and current credential documents. | All credentials current at all times; zero lapses. |
| Renewal applications submitted at least 90 calendar days before expiration. | Review of renewal submission dates in the Credential Register against expiration dates. | 100% of renewals submitted at least 90 calendar days before expiration, or within the issuing authority's specified window. |
| Governing Body receives credential status verification at each quarterly meeting. | Review of Governing Body meeting minutes for credential verification documentation. | Credential verification documented in 100% of quarterly meeting minutes. |
| Regulatory correspondence responded to within required timeframes. | Review of correspondence file for response dates against issuing authority deadlines. | 100% of correspondence responded to within the specified deadline. |
| Survey Plans of Correction submitted within CMS-required timeframes. | Review of POC submission dates against Statement of Deficiencies receipt dates. | 100% of POCs submitted within the CMS deadline (typically 10 calendar days). |
| Agency Credential Register is current and complete. | Administrator self-audit; Compliance Officer verification. | Register updated within 3 calendar days of any status change; all fields complete. |
| Credential documents are displayed and accessible as required. | Physical verification of license display; document accessibility test. | License displayed in principal office at all times; all credentials producible within 1 business day. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under the State Operations Manual (SOM) Appendix B will specifically verify:
Evidence that the agency holds a current, valid state license. Surveyors will request a copy of the current California home health agency license and verify it is not expired. They will also verify that the license is displayed in the agency's principal office. An expired or suspended license is a threshold finding that may terminate the survey process and trigger immediate CMS notification.
Evidence that the agency's Medicare certification is in good standing. Surveyors will verify the CCN and cross-reference the agency's PECOS enrollment status. Any discrepancy between the agency's reported certification status and CMS records is a critical finding.
Evidence that the agency responds to survey findings. Surveyors will review prior survey history and verify that Plans of Correction were submitted timely and that corrective actions were implemented and sustained.
Evidence that the Governing Body monitors the agency's credential status. Surveyors will review Governing Body minutes for documented credential verification. Minutes that do not reference licensure or certification status reviews may result in a finding of inadequate governance oversight.
Evidence that the agency reports required changes to CMS and the state. Surveyors will verify that changes in address, ownership, administrator, or services were reported as required. Unreported changes are a common deficiency.
Evidence of accreditation currency (if the agency holds accreditation). If the agency relies on accreditation for deemed status, surveyors will verify that accreditation is current and that the accrediting organization's most recent survey results are on file.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Renewal application submitted late or not at all. | Credential lapse; loss of legal authority to operate; loss of Medicare reimbursement; potential agency closure. | 90-day advance renewal submission requirement per Section 4.4; monthly Credential Register review per Section 6.1.2; Compliance Officer monitoring per Section 6.2.6. |
| License displayed in the office is expired. | State survey citation; public perception of non-compliance. | Update display within 3 calendar days of receiving renewed license per Section 6.7.1. |
| Changes not reported to CMS or HCAI within required timeframes. | Survey deficiency; potential administrative action; Medicare enrollment discrepancy. | Procedures for change reporting per Sections 6.2.5 and 6.3.6; Compliance Officer monitoring of regulatory requirements. |
| Governing Body minutes do not document credential status verification. | Surveyor finds inadequate governance oversight under 42 CFR § 484.105. | Include credential verification as a standing Governing Body agenda item per GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.1.2 and this policy Section 6.1.3. |
| Plan of Correction not submitted within CMS deadline following survey. | CMS may initiate termination proceedings; loss of Medicare certification. | Immediate mobilization of POC development upon receipt of Statement of Deficiencies per Section 6.3.4; Governing Body notification per Section 6.3.5. |
| Accreditation lapses without CMS notification. | Loss of deemed status; agency subject to unannounced CMS survey; potential enrollment issue. | Track accreditation expiration in Credential Register; submit renewal per accrediting organization timelines; notify CMS if deemed status is affected. |
| No systematic tracking of credential expiration dates. | Ad hoc management leads to missed deadlines. | Agency Credential Register (Appendix A) with monthly review and 120-day advance flagging per Section 6.1.2. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 The Administrator and all administrative staff responsible for credential maintenance shall receive training on this policy within 14 calendar days of assuming credential-related responsibilities. Training shall cover: (a) the agency's required credentials and their renewal timelines; (b) the Agency Credential Register and how to maintain it; (c) the renewal application process and pre-submission quality review; (d) reporting requirements for changes affecting credentials; (e) the escalation protocol for credential-threatening events; (f) Governing Body reporting obligations.
10.2 The Compliance Officer shall receive training on the regulatory monitoring obligations defined in this policy, including the requirement to notify the Administrator within 7 calendar days of identifying any new or changed licensure, certification, or accreditation requirement.
10.3 All personnel within scope (Section 3) shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.4 The Administrator shall include a credential maintenance compliance update as a standing topic in the annual mandatory training program, informing all staff of the importance of the agency's licenses and certifications to continued operations.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment.
