# GV-EA-005 — Agency Closure or Change of Ownership
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | GV-EA-005 |
| Policy Title | Agency Closure or Change of Ownership |
| Domain | GV — Governance & Administration |
| Subdomain | EA — External Affairs |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Review Cycle | Triggered |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Policy Owner / Steward | Administrator |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Version | 1.0 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2027-07-10 (biennial minimum floor; Triggered review upon any event described in Section 6) |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the procedures and notification requirements for planned agency closure, merger, change of ownership (CHOW), or voluntary termination of the Medicare provider agreement of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. A change of ownership or agency closure involves significant regulatory, legal, patient safety, and operational implications that must be managed in a systematic and compliant manner. CMS requires specific notification and transition procedures under 42 CFR Part 489, Subpart B for changes of ownership and under 42 CFR § 489.52 for voluntary termination of provider agreements. California HCAI requires separate state-level notifications. Failure to comply with these requirements may result in disruption of patient services, loss of Medicare certification, financial penalties, and legal liability. This policy ensures that the Governing Body, as the ultimate authority for the agency, directs and oversees any closure, merger, or CHOW process with full regulatory compliance and with the safety and continuity of patient care as the paramount priority.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. (decision-making authority)
The Agency Administrator (operational execution)
The Compliance Officer (regulatory compliance coordination)
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (patient care transition)
The CFO / Revenue Cycle Director (financial closeout)
The HR Director (workforce notification and transition)
Legal Counsel retained by the agency
All agency personnel affected by a closure, merger, or change of ownership
This policy is activated when:
The Governing Body decides to permanently close the agency
The Governing Body decides to voluntarily terminate the agency's Medicare provider agreement
The agency undergoes a change of ownership as defined by 42 CFR § 489.18
The agency merges with or is acquired by another entity
Circumstances compel involuntary closure (e.g., regulatory enforcement, financial insolvency)
This policy does not apply to temporary service suspensions during emergencies (see OP-FM-005) or reductions in the scope of services (see GV-OG-003).
## 4. Policy Statement
4.1 Any decision to close the agency, voluntarily terminate the Medicare provider agreement, or initiate or accept a change of ownership shall be made exclusively by the Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The Administrator shall not independently initiate or agree to any such action without Governing Body authorization, documented in meeting minutes.
4.2 The Governing Body shall direct the closure, merger, or CHOW process to ensure: (a) all patients are safely transitioned to alternative providers with no disruption of essential services; (b) all regulatory notification requirements are met within mandated timeframes; (c) all employee obligations are fulfilled including appropriate notice and separation processes; (d) all financial obligations are resolved including outstanding claims, overpayments, and contractual liabilities; (e) all records are preserved and transferred in compliance with HIPAA, CMS, and California state retention requirements; (f) Legal Counsel is engaged to advise on all legal and regulatory aspects.
4.3 Patient safety and continuity of care shall be the highest priority in any closure or CHOW transition. No patient shall be discharged or transferred without a documented plan for continued care, per policy CL-CP-006 and CL-CP-007.
4.4 The agency shall comply with all CMS notification requirements for change of ownership under 42 CFR § 489.18, including notification to the CMS Medicare Administrative Contractor (MAC) and the state survey agency. Notification shall be submitted no later than the timeframes required by CMS and California state law.
4.5 The agency shall comply with all California HCAI notification requirements for change of ownership, closure, or surrender of license, including advance notice to HCAI and to all patients currently receiving services.
4.6 In the event of a change of ownership, the agency shall work cooperatively with the new owner to ensure the orderly transfer of the Medicare provider agreement, state license, patient records, and operational responsibilities, subject to CMS and HCAI approval.
4.7 This policy shall be reviewed on a triggered basis — activated upon any event described in Section 3. As a minimum floor, it shall also be reviewed biennially to ensure currency.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Agency Closure | The permanent cessation of all home health services by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., including the voluntary termination of the agency's Medicare provider agreement and surrender of the California home health agency license. |
| Change of Ownership (CHOW) | A change in the legal entity responsible for the operation of the home health agency, as defined by 42 CFR § 489.18. A CHOW includes the sale of the agency, transfer of corporate stock or membership interests resulting in a change of majority ownership, merger of the agency with another entity, and consolidation of the agency into a new entity. A CHOW does not include a change of administrator, a change of director of nursing, or internal corporate reorganizations that do not change the legal entity. |
| Voluntary Termination | The agency's decision to end its Medicare provider agreement voluntarily, as distinct from CMS-initiated termination due to non-compliance. |
| Involuntary Closure | Closure of the agency compelled by circumstances outside the Governing Body's preference, including CMS termination of the provider agreement, state license revocation, financial insolvency, or court order. |
| Merger | The combination of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. with another entity into a single surviving entity, which may or may not be Care Indeed. |
| Acquiring Entity | The individual, corporation, partnership, or other legal entity that will assume ownership of the agency in a CHOW transaction. |
| Patient Transition Plan | A documented plan for the safe and orderly transfer of each patient to an alternative provider, ensuring continuity of care services, clinical record transfer, and patient/caregiver notification. |
| Medicare Provider Agreement | The agreement between the agency and CMS under which the agency is certified to participate in the Medicare program and receive Medicare reimbursement. |
| CMS-855A | The Medicare enrollment application form used to report changes of information, including changes of ownership, to the CMS Medicare Administrative Contractor. |
| Run-Out Period | The period following agency closure during which final Medicare claims are submitted, outstanding claims are adjudicated, and financial reconciliation is completed. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Governing Body Decision and Authorization

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Governing Body | The Governing Body shall make the formal decision to close the agency, terminate the Medicare provider agreement, or initiate or accept a change of ownership. The decision shall be documented in Governing Body meeting minutes per policy GV-GB-002. The minutes shall record: (a) the nature of the decision (closure, CHOW, merger); (b) the rationale for the decision; (c) the authorized effective date or anticipated timeline; (d) the designation of the Administrator as the operational lead for the transition process; (e) the direction to engage Legal Counsel per GV-EA-003. | At a regular or special Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.1.2 | Governing Body | Authorize the Administrator to execute all necessary notifications, agreements, and transition activities required by this policy, subject to Governing Body oversight and approval of material transactions. | At the same meeting. |
| 6.1.3 | Administrator | Engage Legal Counsel with expertise in home health agency regulatory transactions per policy GV-EA-003 immediately upon Governing Body authorization. | Within 3 calendar days of Governing Body authorization. |
| 6.1.4 | Administrator | Establish a Closure/CHOW Transition Team consisting of: (a) the Administrator (team lead); (b) the Director of Nursing; (c) the Compliance Officer; (d) the CFO / Revenue Cycle Director; (e) the HR Director; (f) Legal Counsel. The team shall meet at least weekly during the transition period. | Within 7 calendar days of Governing Body authorization. |

### 6.2 Regulatory Notifications — Change of Ownership

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Administrator | Notify the CMS Medicare Administrative Contractor (MAC) of the anticipated change of ownership by submitting a CMS-855A Change of Information form. The notification must include: (a) the nature of the ownership change; (b) the anticipated effective date; (c) identification of the acquiring entity; (d) the new owner's proposed management structure. | At least 90 calendar days before the anticipated effective date of the CHOW, or as early as practicable if the transaction timeline is shorter. CMS requires notification in advance of the CHOW effective date. |
| 6.2.2 | Administrator | Notify the California HCAI of the anticipated change of ownership in compliance with California Health and Safety Code requirements. Submit all required HCAI forms, applications, and supporting documentation as specified by HCAI for the type of ownership change. | Per HCAI requirements; typically at least 90 calendar days before the anticipated effective date. |
| 6.2.3 | Administrator | Notify the state survey agency (California Department of Public Health) of the anticipated CHOW per CMS and state requirements. | Per CMS and state requirements; concurrent with MAC notification. |
| 6.2.4 | Administrator | Cooperate with the acquiring entity in completing any CMS or HCAI applications required for the new owner to assume the Medicare provider agreement and state license. Provide all requested documentation, operational data, and access to agency records as needed for the new owner's application process. | Throughout the CHOW process; responsive to requests within 5 business days. |
| 6.2.5 | Compliance Officer | Verify that the acquiring entity has undergone OIG/SAM exclusion screening for all principals, owners, and managing employees as required by CMS enrollment regulations. Document the screening results. | Before the CHOW effective date. |
| 6.2.6 | Administrator | If CMS determines that the CHOW results in a new provider agreement (rather than an assignment of the existing agreement), work with Legal Counsel and the acquiring entity to complete the CMS enrollment and survey process required for new certification. | Per CMS determination and timelines. |

### 6.3 Regulatory Notifications — Agency Closure / Voluntary Termination

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Administrator | Notify the CMS MAC of the agency's decision to voluntarily terminate its Medicare provider agreement. Submit written notice specifying the proposed termination effective date. Per 42 CFR § 489.52(a), the agency must provide at least 15 calendar days advance written notice to CMS. | At least 15 calendar days before the proposed termination effective date; the agency should provide as much advance notice as possible (recommended: 90 calendar days). |
| 6.3.2 | Administrator | Notify California HCAI of the agency's intent to surrender its home health agency license. Submit the required HCAI forms and comply with any state-mandated advance notice period and patient notification requirements. | Per HCAI requirements; at least 90 calendar days before the planned closure date. |
| 6.3.3 | Administrator | Notify the Medi-Cal program (DHCS) if the agency is enrolled as a Medi-Cal provider, requesting voluntary disenrollment effective on the closure date. | Per DHCS requirements; at least 30 calendar days before the closure date. |
| 6.3.4 | Administrator | Notify any accrediting organization of the agency's intent to close and voluntarily relinquish accreditation. | Per accrediting organization requirements; at least 30 calendar days before the closure date. |
| 6.3.5 | Administrator | Notify all referral sources, contracted service providers, vendors, and business associates in writing of the agency's planned closure date and the status of ongoing patient transitions. | At least 30 calendar days before the closure date. |

### 6.4 Patient Notification and Transition

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Director of Nursing | Compile a complete census of all patients currently receiving services from the agency, including: (a) patient name and contact information; (b) primary caregiver contact; (c) current plan of care and services ordered; (d) current episode dates; (e) attending physician; (f) clinical acuity and care needs. | Within 7 calendar days of Governing Body decision. |
| 6.4.2 | Director of Nursing / Administrator | Notify all current patients and their primary caregivers in writing of: (a) the planned closure or change of ownership; (b) the expected last date of service (for closures); (c) the patient's right to choose any qualified home health agency; (d) assistance available from the agency in identifying and transitioning to alternative providers; (e) the process for transferring medical records; (f) a list of Medicare-certified home health agencies serving the patient's geographic area; (g) contact information for the agency's transition coordinator. The notification shall be provided in the patient's primary language or with interpreter services as required by policy OP-PA-003. | At least 30 calendar days before the planned closure or CHOW effective date. Written notification retained in each patient's clinical record. |
| 6.4.3 | Director of Nursing | Develop an individualized Patient Transition Plan for each current patient, documenting: (a) the receiving agency selected by the patient (or family/representative); (b) the anticipated transfer date; (c) a clinical summary of the patient's condition, current plan of care, and active orders; (d) any pending physician orders, recertification, or face-to-face encounters; (e) the date clinical records will be transferred to the receiving agency; (f) confirmation that the patient's attending physician has been notified. | Transition plans completed for all patients no later than 14 calendar days before the planned closure or CHOW effective date. |
| 6.4.4 | Assigned Clinician | Coordinate the clinical transfer of each patient per policy CL-CP-007 (Transfer & Referral Procedures), including: (a) verbal report to the receiving agency's clinical staff; (b) transmission of the clinical summary and current orders; (c) OASIS transfer or discharge assessment as applicable; (d) medication reconciliation; (e) patient and caregiver education on the transition. | Completed before the patient's last visit from Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| 6.4.5 | Director of Nursing | Notify each patient's attending physician in writing of the planned transfer, including the name of the receiving agency and the transfer date. Obtain physician acknowledgment if possible. | At least 14 calendar days before the planned closure or CHOW effective date; before the transfer date for each patient. |
| 6.4.6 | Director of Nursing | For patients who decline to transfer to another agency or who cannot be placed, document the refusal or placement difficulty in the clinical record and continue services until the agency's last date of operation. Escalate to the Administrator and Compliance Officer if any patient's safety or access to care is jeopardized. | Ongoing; escalation immediately upon identification of care continuity risk. |
| 6.4.7 | Administrator | Confirm that all patients have been safely transitioned or discharged before the agency ceases operations. No patient shall be abandoned — services shall continue until each patient has been successfully transferred or appropriately discharged. | Before the agency's last date of operation. |

### 6.5 Employee Notification and Workforce Transition

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator / HR Director | Notify all agency employees of the planned closure, CHOW, or merger in writing, including: (a) the nature of the event; (b) the anticipated effective date; (c) the impact on each employee's employment status; (d) for closures: the anticipated last date of employment and separation process per HR-ER-006; (e) for CHOWs: whether the acquiring entity intends to offer continued employment and the process for that determination; (f) information about COBRA benefits continuation, final pay, and accrued leave; (g) contact information for questions. | For closures: at least 60 calendar days before the planned closure date (consistent with California WARN Act requirements if applicable). For CHOWs: as soon as the Governing Body authorizes the transition and employment impact is determinable. |
| 6.5.2 | HR Director | Determine whether the California Worker Adjustment and Retraining Notification (WARN) Act applies to the closure or mass layoff. If the agency employs 75 or more employees, the WARN Act requires 60 calendar days advance written notice before a plant closure or mass layoff. | At the time of Governing Body decision; WARN notice issued at least 60 calendar days before the closure if applicable. |
| 6.5.3 | HR Director | Process all employee separations per policy HR-ER-006, including: (a) final paycheck and accrued leave payout within California-required timeframes; (b) return of agency property, keys, equipment, and identification; (c) revocation of EHR access, email, and all system credentials per IT-SC-002; (d) exit interviews; (e) COBRA notification. | Per California employment law; final pay on the last day of employment or within 72 hours if resignation. |
| 6.5.4 | HR Director | Provide all separating employees with information about unemployment insurance benefits and any employee assistance program resources available during the transition. | At the time of separation. |
| 6.5.5 | HR Director | In the event of a CHOW where the acquiring entity will offer continued employment, coordinate with the acquiring entity to facilitate employee transition, including: (a) sharing employee roster information (with appropriate privacy protections); (b) coordinating credential verification for clinical staff; (c) facilitating new hire paperwork with the acquiring entity; (d) clarifying benefits transition. | During the CHOW transition period; responsive to acquiring entity requests within 5 business days. |

### 6.6 Financial Closeout

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Submit all outstanding Medicare claims within the timely filing deadline (per CMS, claims must be submitted within 12 months of the date of service). Complete all pending billing and claims submissions before the closure date and during the run-out period. | Before closure date for services rendered; run-out claims submitted within CMS timely filing deadlines. |
| 6.6.2 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Complete all pending OASIS submissions per CMS OASIS submission requirements. Ensure all discharge or transfer OASIS assessments are completed and transmitted. | Before the agency's last date of operation. |
| 6.6.3 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Reconcile all accounts receivable and accounts payable. Identify and resolve all outstanding overpayments per CMS 60-day repayment requirements and policy FN-BC-004. | Reconciliation completed within 60 calendar days of the closure date. |
| 6.6.4 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Cancel or transfer all payer contracts per the terms of each contract. Notify all payers of the agency's closure or CHOW effective date. | Per contract terms; at least 30 calendar days before closure or CHOW effective date. |
| 6.6.5 | Administrator | Cancel or transfer all vendor and contractor agreements per the terms of each agreement and policy GV-EA-001. Settle all outstanding vendor obligations. | Per contract terms; before closure date. |
| 6.6.6 | Administrator | Resolve all outstanding insurance claims, including professional liability, general liability, and workers' compensation claims, in coordination with the agency's insurers and Legal Counsel per RM-ER-004 and RM-ER-006. Arrange for tail coverage for professional liability if necessary. | Before closure date; tail coverage arranged before policy expiration. |
| 6.6.7 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Prepare a final financial report for the Governing Body documenting: (a) final revenue and expense reconciliation; (b) status of all outstanding claims and receivables; (c) status of all outstanding payables and liabilities; (d) final cash position; (e) estimated remaining financial obligations during the run-out period. | Within 30 calendar days of the closure date; presented to the Governing Body at the first meeting following closure. |

### 6.7 Record Retention and Transfer

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.7.1 | Administrator / Compliance Officer | Ensure that all patient clinical records, financial records, personnel records, compliance records, governance records, and operational records are preserved in compliance with the retention requirements of policy CO-HP-007 (minimum 7 years for most records; longer for certain categories as specified). | Beginning immediately upon Governing Body decision; record preservation confirmed before the agency's last date of operation. |
| 6.7.2 | Administrator | Designate a responsible custodian for all agency records following closure. The custodian may be: (a) the Administrator or a designated individual; (b) a contracted records management company; (c) the acquiring entity (in the case of a CHOW). The custodian must maintain records in a secure, accessible, and HIPAA-compliant manner for the full retention period. | Custodian designated before the closure date; documented in the closure plan. |
| 6.7.3 | Administrator | In the event of a CHOW, transfer patient clinical records to the acquiring entity in compliance with HIPAA per CO-HP-001 and CO-HP-002. The transfer shall be documented in a Record Transfer Agreement between the agency and the acquiring entity, specifying: (a) the records transferred; (b) the format (physical, electronic); (c) the acquiring entity's obligation to maintain confidentiality and comply with HIPAA; (d) the retention period obligation assumed by the acquiring entity. | Before the CHOW effective date; Record Transfer Agreement executed before records are transferred. |
| 6.7.4 | Director of Nursing | For patients transferring to an agency other than the acquiring entity (or in case of closure with no acquiring entity), provide the receiving agency with a copy of the patient's clinical record or clinical summary as authorized by the patient or as permitted by HIPAA for treatment purposes. | Before or at the time of patient transfer. |
| 6.7.5 | Administrator | Notify patients in writing of the location and contact information for accessing their clinical records following agency closure, per HIPAA patient access requirements (CO-HP-006). This notification shall be included in the patient notification letter (Section 6.4.2) or sent separately before closure. | Before the closure date; included in patient notification or sent separately. |
| 6.7.6 | IT Director | Ensure secure disposition of all electronic records, systems, and devices per IT-SC-006. Decommission EHR access, email systems, and electronic communication platforms in accordance with the agency's data security requirements. Do not destroy electronic records subject to retention requirements — archive per the records custodian arrangement. | Before or at the closure date; no electronic data destruction without Compliance Officer confirmation of retention compliance. |

### 6.8 Final Credential Surrender

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.8.1 | Administrator | Upon completion of all patient transitions, employee separations, and financial closeout activities, submit the final closure notification to CMS confirming the termination of the Medicare provider agreement. | After all operational closure activities are completed; on or before the effective date of termination. |
| 6.8.2 | Administrator | Submit the voluntary surrender of the California home health agency license to HCAI with confirmation that all patients have been safely transitioned and all state notification requirements have been met. | After all patients have been transitioned; per HCAI requirements. |
| 6.8.3 | Administrator | Surrender or terminate any accreditation, Medi-Cal enrollment, and local permits or registrations. | After all patients have been transitioned; per each issuing authority's requirements. |
| 6.8.4 | Administrator | Update the Agency Credential Register (GV-EA-004, Appendix A) to reflect the final status of all credentials as surrendered, terminated, or transferred. | Within 7 calendar days of each credential surrender or termination. |

### 6.9 Post-Closure Obligations

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.9.1 | Designated Records Custodian | Maintain custody of all agency records for the full retention period specified in policy CO-HP-007. Respond to patient requests for record access within the timeframes required by HIPAA (typically 30 calendar days). | For the full retention period following closure. |
| 6.9.2 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director (or designated individual) | Continue to process claim adjudications, appeals, and overpayment refunds during the run-out period until all financial obligations are resolved. | Until all financial obligations are resolved; monitor for at least 12 months following closure. |
| 6.9.3 | Administrator | Respond to any post-closure inquiries from CMS, state agencies, or payers regarding the agency's operations during the period of its active certification. | Ongoing for the retention period. |
| 6.9.4 | Governing Body | Retain the Legal Counsel engagement for any post-closure legal matters including claims, audits, or regulatory inquiries. | As needed; for a minimum of 3 years following closure. |

### 6.10 Involuntary Closure Procedures

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.10.1 | Administrator | In the event of involuntary closure (CMS termination, state license revocation, court order, financial insolvency), immediately convene an emergency Governing Body meeting per GV-GB-001, Section 6.3.2. | Within 48 hours of receiving notice of involuntary closure. |
| 6.10.2 | Governing Body | Direct the Administrator to engage Legal Counsel immediately, assess appeal rights, and implement the patient transition plan on an emergency basis. | At the emergency Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.10.3 | Director of Nursing | Implement the patient transition plan (Section 6.4) on an expedited timeline. Prioritize patients based on clinical acuity, with the most vulnerable patients (those with complex care needs, limited caregiver support, or active safety risks) transitioned first. | Immediately upon Governing Body direction; all patients transitioned before the mandated cessation of services. |
| 6.10.4 | Administrator | Notify all regulatory bodies, payers, referral sources, and contracted entities of the involuntary closure and the patient transition plan. | Within 48 hours of Governing Body direction. |
| 6.10.5 | HR Director | Implement emergency separation procedures per Section 6.5, complying with all California employment law requirements for final pay, WARN Act obligations (if applicable), and COBRA notification. | Per California employment law timelines. |

### 6.11 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| One or more patients cannot be placed with an alternative provider before the agency's last date of service. | Director of Nursing escalates to Administrator and Compliance Officer. | Administrator contacts the state survey agency and CMS MAC to report the placement difficulty. Legal Counsel engaged to assess the agency's obligation to continue services pending placement. Agency continues services until all patients are safely placed, regardless of the planned closure date. | Immediately upon identification; services continue until safe placement. |
| Employee challenges the adequacy of WARN Act notice. | HR Director escalates to Administrator and Legal Counsel. | Legal Counsel assesses compliance with WARN Act requirements and advises on remediation. | Within 5 business days of the challenge. |
| CMS rejects or delays the CHOW application. | Administrator escalates to Governing Body Chair and Legal Counsel. | Legal Counsel engages with CMS to identify and resolve application deficiencies. The existing agency continues to operate under its current certification until the CHOW is approved. | Immediately upon CMS notification; resolution per CMS timelines. |
| Overpayments are identified during financial closeout that exceed the agency's ability to repay. | CFO escalates to Administrator and Governing Body. | Governing Body directs financial resolution strategy in consultation with Legal Counsel. CMS Extended Repayment Schedule requested if applicable. | Within 14 calendar days of identification. |
| Post-closure, a regulatory audit requests records that cannot be located. | Designated Records Custodian notifies the former Administrator and Legal Counsel. | Conduct an exhaustive search. If records are truly unavailable, Legal Counsel advises on the agency's disclosure and remediation obligations to the requesting authority. | Within 7 calendar days of the request. |

| Closure/CHOW Transition Plan | Master transition plan document tracking all activities, responsible parties, deadlines, and status. | Administrator | Administrative file; Governing Body governance file. | Developed within 14 calendar days of Governing Body authorization; updated weekly during transition; retained minimum 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgment | Signed acknowledgment of this policy by all personnel within scope (Appendix B). | Each individual (completion); Administrator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of policy effective date, revision, or new appointment. |

## 8. Compliance & Audit Considerations
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Governing Body authorized and documented the closure/CHOW decision. | Review of Governing Body meeting minutes. | Decision documented in minutes with rationale, authorization, and designated lead. |
| All regulatory notifications submitted within required timeframes. | Review of notification submission dates against regulatory deadlines. | 100% of notifications submitted within required timeframes. |
| All patients notified in writing at least 30 calendar days before closure. | Review of patient notification records. | 100% of patients and caregivers notified at least 30 calendar days before closure. |
| All patients safely transitioned before the agency's last date of service. | Review of Patient Transition Plans and discharge/transfer documentation. | 100% of patients transitioned; zero patients abandoned. |
| Employees received required advance notice. | Review of employee notification dates against WARN Act requirements. | WARN notice provided at least 60 calendar days in advance if applicable; all employees notified. |
| Financial closeout completed and reported to Governing Body. | Review of final financial report. | Report completed within 30 calendar days of closure; all outstanding financial obligations documented. |
| All records preserved and custodian designated. | Review of custodian designation and records inventory. | Records custodian formally designated before closure; all records accounted for. |
| All credentials surrendered or transferred. | Review of Agency Credential Register and surrender confirmations. | All credentials surrendered, terminated, or transferred with confirmation documented. |

### 8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For
CMS surveyors and state agency reviewers assessing a closure or CHOW will verify:
Evidence that patients were properly notified and transitioned. This is the primary regulatory concern. CMS will verify that all patients received advance written notice, were offered a choice of alternative providers, and were safely transitioned with appropriate clinical documentation (OASIS, clinical summaries, medication reconciliation). Patient abandonment — a patient left without arranged continued care — is the most serious potential finding.
Evidence that CMS and state notifications were timely. CMS will review the timing of the agency's notification relative to the closure or CHOW effective date. Late or absent notifications are regulatory violations.
Evidence that clinical records were preserved and accessible. CMS may request records for patients served during the agency's period of certification. Inability to produce records is a post-closure compliance failure.
Evidence that OASIS submissions were completed. CMS will verify that all required OASIS assessments (transfer, discharge) were completed and transmitted per OASIS requirements.
Evidence that financial obligations were resolved. CMS may conduct a post-closure audit of claims submitted during the agency's period of certification. Unresolved overpayments, late-filed claims, or billing irregularities are compliance concerns.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Patients not notified in advance; services abruptly cease. | Patient abandonment; CMS enforcement; state investigation; potential harm to patients. | Patient notification per Section 6.4.2 at least 30 calendar days in advance; individualized transition plans per Section 6.4.3; continuation of services until all patients are placed per Section 6.4.7. |
| Regulatory notifications not submitted or submitted late. | Violation of 42 CFR Part 489; California licensure violation; potential penalties. | Notification timelines per Sections 6.2 and 6.3; Compliance Officer tracking of all notification deadlines. |
| Clinical records not preserved after closure. | HIPAA violation; inability to respond to patient access requests or regulatory audits; potential False Claims Act exposure. | Records custodian designated per Section 6.7.2; records preserved for full retention period per CO-HP-007. |
| OASIS transfer/discharge assessments not completed. | OASIS submission violation; CMS data integrity finding. | Director of Nursing ensures all OASIS assessments completed before last date of service per Section 6.4.4 and Section 6.6.2. |
| WARN Act notice not provided when required. | California WARN Act violation; employee lawsuits; financial penalties. | HR Director assesses WARN Act applicability per Section 6.5.2; 60-day notice issued if applicable. |
| Overpayments not identified or refunded during closeout. | False Claims Act exposure; CMS audit finding; potential civil monetary penalties. | Financial reconciliation per Section 6.6.3; overpayment reporting per FN-BC-004. |
| Governing Body does not authorize the closure/CHOW through a formal vote. | Unauthorized agency action; governance failure; legal challenge by stakeholders. | Formal Governing Body authorization per Section 6.1.1; documented in meeting minutes. |

## 10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements
10.1 The Administrator, Director of Nursing, Compliance Officer, CFO, and HR Director shall receive training on this policy within 14 calendar days of appointment. Training shall cover: (a) the types of events that trigger this policy; (b) the regulatory notification requirements and timelines for CMS, HCAI, and DHCS; (c) the patient transition process and the paramount priority of patient safety; (d) the employee notification and WARN Act requirements; (e) the financial closeout process; (f) the record retention and transfer obligations; (g) the post-closure obligations.
10.2 All Governing Body members shall receive orientation to this policy as part of their governance onboarding per GV-GB-001, Section 10.1, with emphasis on: (a) the Governing Body's exclusive decision-making authority for closure and CHOW; (b) the Governing Body's oversight role during the transition; (c) the regulatory consequences of non-compliance.
10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.
10.4 In the event this policy is activated, the Administrator shall conduct an orientation session for all agency staff within 7 calendar days of the Governing Body's closure/CHOW decision, informing staff of: (a) the nature and timeline of the event; (b) their role in patient transition activities; (c) the employee notification and separation process; (d) confidentiality obligations; (e) the prohibition on patient abandonment.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment.
11.5 This policy is assigned a Triggered review cycle. It shall be reviewed: (a) immediately upon any event described in Section 3; (b) biennially as a minimum floor, regardless of whether a triggering event has occurred; (c) upon any change to CMS regulations governing CHOW or voluntary termination (42 CFR Part 489) or California HCAI licensure requirements.
